# Fidelity — how far this model sits from a real machine

One row per thing that differs. **A row that is written honestly is finished work.** Closing the
gap is a separate decision, taken on the row, never implied by it.

Columns: what it is / what a real machine does / what this model does / the distance / **on
purpose?** / **how sure the real-world figure is**.

**Every `WINDSCALE` reading in this file was taken on a preset that no longer exists.** WINDSCALE
was renamed CALDER HALL in `56bdde6`, which in the same commit gave it `feedT` 310.9 and `sgDesP`
1.448 — its physics changed, not just its label. The readings are left under the old name on
purpose: moving them would attach numbers from one plant to another. None of them has been
re-measured under CALDER HALL, and until one is, a `WINDSCALE` figure says nothing about the
preset on the board today.

`ON PURPOSE` is one of:

- **YES** — a deliberate cut, usually the design pillar's "unless it is heavy to compute" clause,
  or a game constant. Nothing is owed.
- **FIT** — the shape is real, the level is a number solved to make one preset land. Honest, but it
  is not a measurement of anything.
- **ERROR** — the model is wrong and nobody has said why. **Nothing new may be built on an ERROR
  row without saying so out loud in the reply.**
- **DRIFT** — nobody decided this. It fell out of something else and has been treated as a decision
  since. Not necessarily wrong; just never chosen. Do not write `YES` on a row unless a decision was
  actually taken.

`SURE` is confidence in the **real-world** side, not in ours: **high** = textbook or well known,
**medium** = from memory of published figures, **low** = not checked against anything.

Nothing here is frozen. A better measurement beats any row on sight — re-measure, rewrite the row,
say what moved. That is not a scope question and never needs permission.

**The "this model" column is a record, not a reference.** Testing is against real-world physics
only: the "real machine" column, conservation, analytic solutions, published data. A figure the
simulation once printed cannot pass or fail anything, because the code that printed it has had bugs.

**The target is BEHAVIOUR, not a decimal.** This will never simulate a plant one-to-one. A job that
ends by chasing the last few per cent of a published figure has failed, not succeeded. Where a row
or a check compares a decimal, ask first whether the published source states a behaviour instead,
and compare that.

---

## Read this first, if nothing else

This is a **game about reactors**, not a reactor simulator. The four biggest honest distances:

1. **The presets are INSPIRED BY real machines, never replicas of them.** They exist to show the
   variety the simulator can build and to give the probes something to fly. A preset is judged on
   flying, closing its books and behaving as its family does; any figure off its namesake's (size,
   pressures, MWe, flows) is a deliberate cut, not a gap.
2. **The reactor physics is genuinely reactor physics** — point kinetics, a nodal core, a published
   DNB correlation, real steam properties. That half is strong.
3. **The pressure network is real physics too** — a momentum-and-mass solve, and the books close to
   zero. It carries **fluid inertia as one lump per run** (`I = L/A`, marched only): no travelling
   wave and no pipe-wall compliance, so a surge reads about 20 % high. BWR/4's coolant flow reading of **0.64** is a BUILD defect on that
   preset's drawing, measured 10/09/26 — see the pump sizing section.
4. **Time is compressed hard.** Xenon runs 400× real speed and the compartment's heat capacity is
   scaled so a session can see it.

---

## Plant size — the presets are inspired by real machines, and nobody picked the sizes

**A preset is inspired by a real machine; it is never a replica.** Most real plants are not
documented well enough to copy, and chasing them stalled development. A preset shows one kind of
thing the simulator can build; its namesake says what kind. Every per-plant figure below and in
"Steady state against its family" is therefore **on purpose**: the row records what is given up,
it asks for no work.

**No preset states a power.** A `PLANTPRE` row is plumbing and a lattice; `D.power = latRating()`,
so the megawatts are an OUTPUT of the drawn core. The one figure anyone chose is the stock PWR's
1198 MWt, which `PEAK_M = 1.283` was solved to land on. Every row below it is whatever its own
drawing happens to rate at, on a board and a mass budget sized for that anchor.

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| STOCK PWR | a 4-loop PWR is about 3400 MWt | 1198 MWt | ~1/3 size | the one chosen anchor | high |
| ...its containment | a large dry PWR containment holds ~15 m3 per MWt at ~0.4 MPa design; a compact one (NuScale's CNV, 6.9 MPa) is rated for what a break brings it to | 757 m3 of gas for 1236 MWt, so ~50x short on volume. **Since 21/09/26 its liner is rated for `sgDesignP()` (6.9 MPa)**, not `MAT_PDES` 0.5: 20-365 mm by span, 344.5 t of steel (was ~36 t), weakest cell bursts at 24.2 MPa (was 1.76). A 60 s steam-line break now peaks at 5.51 MPa with no breach (at 0.5 MPa a wall cell burst at 4.56 s and the wave wrecked the core at 4.96 s: BUILD); with protection armed it scrams at 0.12 s and peaks at 6.62 MPa against a 6.66 MPa drive. With protection armed the hot-leg break scrams at 0.12 s; its water now fits its cells to 1.3 % (it was 14.9x), and its worst gas cell reads 35.5 MPa against an 11.76 MPa drive: a pocket the liquid solve squeezes (row **...water pushed into a full body**), MODEL | volume 50x short, bought back with wall | YES: the board is the ship | high on the measurements |
| NUSCALE | 250 MWt per module | 694 MWt | **2.8× too big** | YES: inspired by | high |
| BWR/4 | 3293 MWt | 1059 MWt | ~1/3 size | YES: inspired by | medium |
| BN-600 | 1470 MWt | 986 MWt | 2/3 size | YES: inspired by | medium |
| EPR | 4590 MWt | 965 MWt | ~1/5 size | YES: inspired by | high |
| RBMK-1000 | 3200 MWt | **775 MWt** (20/09/26, was 1037) | ~1/4 size, and it is the only row whose size is no longer borrowed: the lattice states the real machine's cell (250 mm pitch, 80 mm bore, 18 rods at 13.6 mm, 7 m high) and `latRating()` answers 775 MWt in 175 m³, i.e. 4.43 kW/L against the published 4.2 and q' 15.39 kW/m against 14.8. What is short is the plan area, not the cell: `LQ = 10` fixes the drawing at 400 channels against the real 1661, so the core is 5.64 m across against 11.8 | the rating is an output and was not aimed at; the 400-channel ceiling is the drawing's own limit | high |
| MSRE | 8 MWt — it was an experiment | 833 MWt | **100× too big** | YES: inspired by, for the fluid | high |
| CALDER HALL | 182 MWt (Nuclear Engineering, Dec. 1956) | 191 MWt | +5 % | YES: inspired by (drawn near the real core 19/09/26) | high |

None of this is a fault. It matters only when a number below is read as if it came off a real plant.

## Neutronics

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| point kinetics | 6 delayed groups | 6 groups, implicit Euler, 4 substeps per 20 ms; asymptotic period after a +50 to +200 pcm step within 0.6 % of the inhour equation on the model's own groups (`tests/physics/kinetics.js`, 18/09/26) | none worth stating | — | high |
| Boron on a plant with two reactors | each unit has its own coolant chemistry: its own boron, dialled to hold its own core critical | **closed 19/09/26.** A core's boron worth is the boron in its own water (`ST.bBy` at the core's node, advected like everything else); commissioning dials each circuit's water for the first core it cools (`eCoreDialBoron()`). The plant figure `SC_BORON` and its demand stay one: the charging system moves every core circuit by the same step. `core.js 8`: DUAL core 1 moves 0.004 pcm over 3 s with rods and boron held (1.8 pcm and 0.26 % heat before, when every core read core 0's figure). A consequence that is real: unborated or differently borated water reaching a core (an injection tank's `tankFluidB`, a dilution) moves that core's reactivity as it arrives; core 0 already read its own water through `SC_BORON`, every other core does now | two cores on one circuit share one water, so the second is not dialled | — | high |
| delayed-neutron shape of a plutonium core | Pu-239 has β ≈ 0.0021 against U-235's 0.0065 and its own group abundances (Keepin 1965), so a MOX or fast core has less margin to prompt critical per pcm | `DNG` in `design.js` states both Keepin (1965) shapes, and both sites in `step.js` read it; a `FUEL` row names its `dng` (absent = U-235 thermal) and MOX PLUTONIUM carries Pu-239. `kinetics.js`: both rows digit-for-digit, inhour roots at +100 pcm 54.87 s (U) and 63.64 s (Pu) at the same beta and Lambda (injected U-shape for Pu reads 54.87 and fails) | **closed 20/09/26:** the shape alone is worth 16 % of period at +100 pcm. No preset uses MOX, so nothing flies differently | **MODEL**, closed | high |
| decay heat after shutdown | **ANSI/ANS-5.1-1979 Table 7**, thermal fission of U-235, 23 exponential groups; after infinite irradiation and on the 200 MeV/fission the standard is normalised against, P/P0 is **6.591 %** at shutdown, 6.159 at 1 s, 4.750 at 10 s, 3.102 at 100 s, 1.901 at 1e3 s, 0.954 at 1e4 s and 0.485 at 1e5 s — fission products only. ENDF/B-VIII.0 `n-092_U_235.endf` MF=1 MT=458 puts the recoverable fission-product decay energy at **12.830 ± 0.07 MeV** (beta 6.500, gamma 6.330) against 194.077 MeV recoverable less neutrinos, which is **6.365 %** once the 7.5 MeV capture gamma the model carries is added to the total. JEF Report 17 ch. 9: ANS-5.1 sits within about 4 % of the Tobias experimental standard | **re-anchored 20/09/26.** `E_DEC_ANS` is the standard's own 23 α/λ pairs typed in; a group's amplitude is its α/λ over their sum, scaled so the total is `1 − PROMPT_F`. Each group is stepped by an exact exponential (`eCoreDecayStep()`). Until 20/09/26 it was an 11-group fit of Way-Wigner for 3 years at power, which read 3.767 % at 10 s and 2.318 % at 100 s against the standard's 4.750 and 3.102 — **21 % and 25 % low** — and 0.341 % at 1e5 s against 0.485, 30 % low | the sum is held at **6.500 %** against the standard's 6.591 (−1.4 %) so the rest point closes on the fission partition, and that −1.4 % is the whole error of the fit from 1 s to 3.5 days (`tests/physics/decay.js`, tolerance 5 %, injected ×1.1 reads 8.5 % out and fails). Missing, all raising it: neutron capture in fission products (the G factor, +3.7 % at 1e3 s and +7.1 % at 1e4 s on FRAPCON's table), U-239 and Np-239 decay, and any isotope but U-235 thermal — MOX and U metal presets carry the U-235 curve | the normalisation YES, it is what makes heat = 1 at rest; the three exclusions NO, they are missing terms | high on the standard's groups (two independent transcriptions agreeing digit for digit, sum 13.183 MeV) and on ENDF's evaluation |
| xenon poisoning | peaks about 9 h after a trip | same curve on a 400× clock, so ~80 s | **400×**. **A consequence nobody had measured until 20/09/26, and it reaches every transient test:** burnout runs on the same clock, so on a seconds-to-tens-of-seconds window xenon is a FAST positive feedback worth thousands of pcm — a power rise burns it out and adds reactivity almost at once. With the rods frozen at full power and a 1e-4 kick, that alone grows the disturbance on **STOCK PWR (e-folding 14.6 s)** and **EPR (15.9 s)**, where a real PWR is firmly stable; stood down, the RBMK-1000 decays at 11.25 s and grows at 9.47 s with it live. Any check that freezes the rods for tens of seconds is measuring this clock unless it stands xenon down, and `rbmk.js low` now does (its own note says so). The 20 % flight on that same check cannot arrive for the same reason: at demand 0.2 the plant sits at n 0.019 with −5012 pcm of xenon and the regulator on its 0.10 floor | YES, it is the central mechanic. The fast-feedback side effect is NOT a separate gap — it follows directly from the clock — but it is written down here because it silently owns the result of any rods-frozen transient | high on the curve and the clock; **high on the fast-feedback measurement**, which is four presets read the same way |
| burnup | fissile content falls and fission products build up over a cycle of 12-24 months, costing several thousand pcm that boron or rods give back | every fuel is at its commissioned composition for ever. **From 21/09/26** each core states a burnup (`coreBurnupOf()`, MWd/kgHM, default half its fuel row's discharge `bu`), and it is read in ONE place: the stable fission gas a pellet holds (`a pellet past tdmg`) | the whole cycle; even on xenon's 400× clock a 12-month cycle is about 22 h of play | YES, decided 19/09/26: a session is hours | high |
| peaking factor `Fq` | PWR about 2.5 | 2.64 stock, measured off the drawn lattice | ~5 % high | — | high |
| enthalpy-rise peaking | `F_dH` is the hottest CHANNEL's integrated rise over the core mean and is what a margin to boiling asks for; `F_q` is the LOCAL flux peak and is what a burnout correlation asks for. Westinghouse four-loop technical specification limits are `F_dH` 1.65 and `F_q` 2.50 at rated power, and a real PWR runs nominal `F_dH` about 1.45–1.55 and nominal `F_q` about 1.9–2.2 | **the row was wrong about the code, corrected 20/09/26 by measurement.** The `boil` margin (`eMarginNode()`, `E_DNB_BOIL`) is `K·(dhSub/c_p)/rise`, and `rise` is the march's own enthalpy integrated up that node's own channel, so the peaking that enters it is `F_dH`; `F_q` enters nowhere. Reconstructed on the two presets that use the law (`core.js 3`, `core.js 6`): `K·subcooling/(T_coolant(limiting node) − T_in)` reproduces `csDnbrMin` to 1e-12, and the same expression built on `F_q` does not (BN-600 3.200 against 2.356; MSRE 3.000 against 1.898). Stock PWR at rest: **`F_dH` 1.532, `F_q` 2.711** | **the "conservative by ~1.6×" claim is withdrawn — there is no `F_q` in that law.** What is open is the flux SHAPE: `F_dH` 1.532 sits under the 1.65 limit and inside the 1.45–1.55 a real PWR runs, but **`F_q` 2.711 is 8.4 % over the 2.50 limit** and far over the 1.9–2.2 nominal, so the model's axial×radial peak is too sharp. Not diagnosed | MODEL on `F_q`; the peaking the boil law uses was never wrong | high on the two definitions; **medium** on the Westinghouse figures, which are LIMITS and not operating values |
| core leakage | a real figure per core | mesh term × `LEAK_K` 0.13 to land 1805 pcm | unknown | FIT — a mesh calibration, not a physical number | — |
| void coefficient, BWR | about −1400 pcm | the expression peaks near −1050 | **25 % weak** | ERROR, stated and left; a per-family fudge was refused | medium |
| the void worth's published target | a full-void worth is a published number per machine: a PWR about -10 000 pcm, a sodium fast lattice about +700, and the pre-1986 RBMK-1000 **+4.5 beta_eff** (INSAG-7 annex I), which at an equilibrium-burnup beta_eff of 0.0048-0.0051 is +2160 to +2300 pcm, while INSAG-7 table II-I states the same quantity absolutely as +2.0e-4 per % void = +2000 pcm. The two published statements bracket **2000-2300** | `AV_MOD`, `AV_ABS` and `AV_FAST` are three constants solved together so three `ARCHPRE` drawings read three published worths. **Re-solved 20/09/26** after the RBMK redraw: the RBMK leg moves from +2500 (the accident configuration's +5 beta read at a beta near the model's own) to **+2150**, the midpoint of the published band, and 12562.5 / 1578.9 / 631.5 becomes **12820.7 / 1740.6 / 619.3**. The redraw was measured FIRST: on the new lattice and the old constants the RBMK read +1968.7, just below the band's 2000 floor, so the re-target was made rather than assumed. `voidworth.js`: PWR -10000.1, RBMK +2150.0, SFR +700.0, each inside 1 % of its own published figure, and a target perturbed 20 % fails its own check | the drawings the three constants are NOT solved against moved as the fit moved: BWR -9879.1 to **-9935.2**, MSR -877.9 to **-854.5**, HTGR and MAGNOX under 0.2 pcm. BWR/4's own distance is its own row ("void coefficient, BWR"), a deliberate cut, and it is left where it is | **FIT**, three constants on three published points, re-targeted on published ABSOLUTE reactivity and never on a sign the flight wanted | high on the PWR and RBMK figures; medium on the SFR's |
| delayed-neutron fraction against a real beta_eff | beta_eff is not the fresh fuel's beta: at equilibrium burnup plutonium carries about a third of the fissions in an RBMK or a PWR, and Pu-239's beta is 0.0021 against U-235's 0.0065, so a real RBMK-1000 runs at **beta_eff 0.0048-0.0051** | the fuel row's beta is the fresh figure and there is no burnup (row "burnup"): the drawn RBMK-1000 carries **680 pcm** | **1.35x** the real machine's. Every reactivity in this model is therefore worth 1.35x LESS in dollars than the same pcm on the machine, so the model is kinetically MORE stable than the real one for that reason alone - it is not what hides an unstable sign, it is what would make a real one worse | **MODEL, stated not fixed**: a per-fuel beta_eff reaches every preset and nobody asked for it. It follows directly from the deliberate "no burnup" cut | high on Keepin's group data; medium on the 0.0048-0.0051 band |
| moderation curve | resonance escape × thermal utilisation | `modEta` with `MOD_A`, `MOD_B` solved off two stated points | shape real, level solved | FIT | high |
| fast fission | a real route in a fast core | `FAST_RHO` 0.773 | unknown | FIT | — |
| RBMK stability | the real RBMK-1000 was stable at full power under its automatic regulators and unstable below about 20 %. INSAG-7 annex I-3: the fast power coefficient measured above 50 % power ran from -4e-4 to +0.6e-4 beta_eff/MW (the positive end only at a void coefficient of +5 beta_eff), negative at the design working point, positive at low power. **Converted on the machine the measurement was MADE on** (beta_eff 0.005, 3200 MWt) that is **-6.40 to +0.96 pcm per % of rated**, and absolute pcm per % of rated is intensive for the same cell, so a smaller core of the same design is judged against the same band. **INSAG 2.1 also states the BEHAVIOUR, verbatim: "the fast power coefficient remained negative under normal operating conditions. At the time of the accident, the void and power coefficients of reactivity were both positive."** The pass condition at 100 % is therefore the SIGN, which is what the source states and a harder thing to get right than a decimal inside a band 7.4 wide. **The intensive argument was re-derived at the source 20/09/26 and it holds**: a_N in beta_eff/MW scales as 1/rating for the same cell, so a_N x rating is invariant and pcm per % of rated is the right common unit; the model's own beta 680 pcm does not enter the comparison at all, which is why the row "delayed-neutron fraction against a real beta_eff" does not touch this one | **redrawn on the real cell 20/09/26** (rows "Plant size", "a fuel slot may hold moderator"): 250 mm pitch, 80 mm bore, 18 rods at 13.6 mm, 7 m high, 775 MWt. `aV` +2150 pcm per unit void on the published band (row "the void worth's published target"), `aG` +2.76 pcm/K (row "graphite temperature"). The fast coefficient is read about the operating point with flow, drum AND core inlet held, because the drum and the feed cannot follow inside a circuit transit (`rbmk.js coef`). **As it stands 21/09/26: -5.198 pcm/% at 100 %, +7.356 at 20 %, zero crossing 51.6 % of rated, and the frozen-rod flight DECAYS at 100 % (e-folding 12.43 s, injected fault - void doubled - grows at 13.18 s). `rbmk.js coef` passes every check.** That is the real machine's behaviour and it is bought with one stated FIT on `aF`; the history below is what it took to get there and what is still owed. Readings on the way: **+1.941 pcm/% at 100 %** (void +7.18, Doppler -5.38, moderator +0.14) after the channel flow split, the graphite conductance and the two-phase riser bore landed, was +3.965 before them; **+3.166** after the pellet conductivity law (void +7.20, Doppler -4.18, moderator +0.15), which moved it the WRONG way for the right reason. Letting the inlet follow the power instead of holding it gave **-2.15 pcm/%** at the +1.941 point. At 20 %, fast: the real machine's sign throughout, PASS, injected void-zeroed fails. `rbmk.js low` grew at **3.36 s**, then **2.88 s**, and now decays. The 20 % flight still does not arrive: n 0.0142 at demand 0.2, the regulator at its 0.10 floor, the deliberate 400x xenon clock (row "xenon poisoning"), not a defect | **21/09/26, THIRD pass — the behaviour is now RIGHT, bought with one stated FIT, and the decision was the user's: the preset exists to feel like an RBMK and to differ from the other eight, not to match a decimal.** `COOLANT.LWGR.aF` is **−4.0 pcm/K** against INSAG-7 table II-I's measured **−1.2** on Chernobyl Unit 4. `LWGR` is read by the RBMK-1000 and nothing else, so the FIT moves one preset; checked, the other eight read bit-identical (`core.js` 0/2/7 unmoved to every digit). Swept before choosing: `aF` −2.8 crosses zero at 79 % of rated, −3.4 at 64 %, **−4.0 at 52 %**, −4.8 at 39 %. −4.0 was taken because INSAG's own behaviour statement is stable above 50 % and unstable below, so the crossing lands on the sentence. **`aF` was the right knob and not merely the cheap one:** the positive void coefficient IS what makes this preset an RBMK, and closing the coefficient on `aV` instead (1284 pcm needed, against a published 2000–2500) would have bought the same crossing by throwing away the thing that makes the machine different. The measured result: **−5.198 pcm/% at 100 %**, **+7.356 at 20 %**, crossing **51.6 %**, and `rbmk.js coef` passes every check including the injected one. **The frozen-rod flight now DECAYS at 100 %, e-folding 12.43 s**, with the void coefficient doubled as its live fault injection (grows, 13.18 s). **But the flight was failing for a different reason than this row said, and that is the more important finding.** Standing xenon down alone makes it decay even at the old `aF` −1.2 (39.9 s), because the flight lets the inlet and the flow follow and both are stabilising; with xenon live the stock PWR and the EPR grow on the same test. So the growth was **the 400× xenon clock**, not the RBMK's feedback (row "xenon poisoning"), and this row's old claim that the machine "grows where the real one decays" was measuring a clock shared by every preset. The `aF` FIT is what buys the STATIC coefficient's sign and crossing — the quantity INSAG actually publishes, and what a player reads off the bench — and it is not what fixed the flight. Both are said because both are true. **Still open and untouched by any of this:** `aG` +2.76 against +6.00, and the 20 % flight cannot arrive at all while the xenon clock stands. **20/09/26, second pass: the target is 0, not +0.96, the distance is the whole 1.95 pcm/%, and the coefficient is now SPLIT and every term inverted.** `tools/coefsplit.js` reads the four terms off the same rest pass and then solves, term by term, for the coefficient each would have to carry for the total to reach zero (a secant, because the flux solve reads the reactivity field and the terms are weakly coupled - doubling `aV` moves another term by 0.55 pcm/%): void +7.180 at `aV` 2150 on d(void)/dP 0.003340 needs **1622 pcm**, below every published figure (2000-2500); Doppler -5.383 at `aF` -1.2 on 4.486 K/% needs **-1.677 pcm/K**, past the published -1.5; moderator +0.144 at `aM` +5.635 needs **-74.0 pcm/K**, no published figure and the wrong sign for 4 % of the moderating; graphite's 2 s share +0.008 needs **-637 pcm/K** against a published +6.00. The terms close on the total to 0.0e0 and each is separable to its own coefficient. **No term reaches a negative coefficient inside its published band, and by construction the four ARE the coefficient, so that is the whole answer.** **Step 2's arithmetic:** taking INSAG's two mapping points as a LINE in a_w (an assumption, labelled one) puts the mapping's own zero crossing at **+4.31 beta_eff** and this drawing at +4.30, so a_N here is **-0.01 pcm/%** and the honest magnitude target is 0. **Step 6, the crossing:** the model's coefficient falls monotonically and never crosses in the range it can run - 20 % +15.23, 40 % +9.33, 60 % +5.86, 80 % +3.57, 100 % +1.94, 120 % +0.74 pcm/% before the pellet law was fixed, and **+16.40, +10.48, +7.03, +4.77, +3.17, +1.98 after it** - so it is unstable at EVERY power, where the real machine was unstable below about 20 % and stable above 50 %. **One mitigation, measured:** the model's two boundary conditions BRACKET the published value - inlet held +1.94, inlet following -2.15 - and INSAG states neither, so which one its measurement was made under is not knowable from the source. It does not rescue the behaviour: the frozen-rod flight grows with a 3.36 s e-folding, which is faster than any circuit transit, so the held-inlet coefficient is the one that governs. **The old distance of 0.98 pcm/% against +0.96 is struck.** 20/09/26, first pass: the whole void chain was ruled out as its cause. `rbmk.js void` computes d(alpha)/dP test side from published correlations only - Saha-Zuber departure on the St 0.0065 branch (Pe here is over 3e5 for any liquid conductivity between 0.5 and 0.7 W/m/K), Levy's profile fit, Zuber-Findlay with C0 1.13 and the churn-turbulent V_gj, IAPWS-IF97 saturation properties and IAPWS R1-76 surface tension - on this drawing's own held inlet, flow and pressure: **0.00308 per %, where the engine reads 0.00315** (re-measured after the riser bore landed and the core came back to 6.99 MPa; it was 0.00408 against 0.00371 at the old 8.07 MPa), so the engine is within 2 % of the correlations. `tools/voidsplit.js` splits the engine's own slope by mechanism, each frozen at its 100 % value: flux reshaping **0.0 %**, subcooled void **+0.7 %**, the axial pressure profile **-0.4 %**, the channel flow split **-4.8 %** flat; with all four stood down the engine reads 0.003858 against the analytic 0.003848, **0.3 %**. The four candidates carry at most 5 % between them and the rest is what the correlations themselves give. The same analytic on the REAL RBMK-1000's published channel (1661 channels of 0.00816 m2, 18 rods of 13.6 mm in an 80 mm tube, 7 m heated, 10 417 kg/s, 3200 MWt, 14 K subcooling, exit quality 0.145) gives **0.0033 per % at 7.0 MPa and 0.0042 at 8.07** - the real machine's own channel is as steep. **A fast coefficient at the +0.96 target with this drawing's `aV` 2150 pcm and Doppler -5.38 pcm/% needs 0.0025 per %, and no correct void model gives it.** What is left is `aV` or the Doppler term. Ruled OUT by measurement, 20/09/26: the **axial quadrature** - refining the mesh from 10 to 80 planes outside the checkout moves d(alpha)/dP only 0.003302 to 0.003218, -2.5 %, so 10 planes resolve the boiling knee; **`aV`**, which is on its published band by construction; **Doppler** - **and that clearance is WITHDRAWN 20/09/26**: the fuel rise was checked against the model's OWN law with the model's own literals, which is not a check. Solved on Fink's UO2 conductivity instead the rise is 213.3 K against the flat law's 276.9, so Doppler was **29.8 % OVER-credited**. **The pellet law is now FIXED** (row "the pellet rise reads one conductivity") and the coefficient moved the WRONG WAY with it: Doppler -5.38 -> **-4.18**, total +1.94 -> **+3.17 pcm/%**, e-folding 3.36 -> **2.88 s**, and the term inversions harden to `aV` 1284 (published 2000-2500) and `aF` -2.203 (published -1.1 to -1.5). `aF` -1.2 pcm/K did sit inside its published band and the driver it multiplies was wrong and is now right - and `aF` itself has since been taken OFF that band deliberately, to -4.0, for the behaviour (see the third pass at the head of this cell); and the **drift velocity**, now built and worth -1 % here (its own row). d(void)/d(power) at a held inlet is **0.003340 per % flux-weighted** where the +0.96 target allows 0.0025, but it is now measured to be RIGHT: see the reference above. The void LEVEL moved with the riser bore and is a new open question: core-average **0.395** against the analytic reference's 0.424 (so the model follows the correlations) but against the ~0.28 this file has carried for the real machine. One of those two real-world figures is wrong and nobody has been to a source for the RBMK's core-average void | **MODEL**, RBMK-1000. `aV` is not to be moved a second time: it was re-targeted on published ABSOLUTE reactivity, the same refusal the BWR void row carries. **The axial pressure profile is now RULED OUT too, built and measured 20/09/26** (its own row, "the axial pressure profile in a channel"): the channel's ten planes now carry their own pressure, saturation, `h_fg`, density ratio and drift, and the water flashes as it rises. The heated length's own drop is **0.095 MPa**, not the 1.86 MPa this row used to quote inlet-to-drum — most of that loss is the inlet throttle and the pipework, which sit on the edge and not in the core — so the flashing is worth 2.1 % of exit quality, and d(void)/d(power) went the WRONG WAY by 0.4 %. **No candidate is left in the void chain, and 20/09/26 that stopped being "nobody knows": the slope is checked against published correlations twice over, once on this drawing and once on the real machine's own channel, and both say it is right.** **The source was read directly on 20/09/26 (IAEA Safety Series 75-INSAG-7, Annex I) and it settles two things.** First, the quantity: INSAG says, verbatim, "Measurements of the FAST power coefficient of reactivity, characterizing the change in reactor reactivity in response to a change in power, showed that when a_w increased from -(0.2-0.4) beta_eff to +5 beta_eff, a_N changed from -4 x 10^-4 beta_eff/MW(th) to +0.6 x 10^-4 beta_eff/MW(th). However, these data were valid only for power levels of more than 50% N_nom." So the check compares the RIGHT quantity and the earlier guess that the band was a minutes-scale coefficient is **wrong and struck**. Second, it is a MAPPING from the void coefficient to the power coefficient, not a band of scatter: this drawing's `aV` 2150 pcm is **+4.3 beta_eff** on the real machine's beta_eff 0.005, which sits at the +5 beta_eff end, so the honest target is the POSITIVE edge **+0.96 pcm/%**, and the whole-band pass condition is lenient by its own width. The pass condition was NOT tightened - a check is not re-aimed around a number - but the note now carries this. The same annex's Table II-I gives Chernobyl Unit 4's fuel temperature coefficient as -1.2e-5 per degC and its graphite coefficient as +6e-5 per degC, the two figures this model already uses as `aF` and the `aG` target, so those are confirmed at the source. **20/09/26, second pass: there is nothing left to fix inside published data, and that is the finding.** The four terms are the whole coefficient, every one of their coefficients is on or inside its published band, the void slope is checked twice against correlations and once against the real machine's own channel, the fuel rise is now checked against an independent property law and is 30 % too GENEROUS, and no term can reach a negative total without leaving published data. The model is internally consistent, its inputs are published, and it still does the opposite of the machine at every power it can run at. **A hand reconstruction says the same of the real machine**: 2150 pcm x 0.0033 per % on the real channel gives a void term of +7.1 pcm/%, and 14.2 kW/m through Fink's pellet with `aF` -1.2 gives a Doppler of about -4.1, so INSAG's own table II-I coefficients assembled on INSAG's own channel give a POSITIVE fast coefficient of about +3 pcm/% where its own mapping gives 0. Either the measured a_N was not taken at a held core inlet, or one of the two published statements is not what it looks like. Nobody has resolved that, and it is the only remaining route to a negative sign that does not leave the data **The coefficient is now a FIT, and the FIT is `aF` −4.0 against a published −1.2 (3.3×), chosen 21/09/26 by the user's own ruling that the target is BEHAVIOUR and not a decimal.** It is labelled at the literal, in this row and in `docs/gap.md`'s absence of a line. It is not to be quietly re-pinned back: the reason it exists is that this drawing's void slope, checked twice against published correlations and once against the real machine's own channel, is right, and its `aV` is on its published band, and the two together make a POSITIVE coefficient at every power. **What would retire the FIT**, and nobody has done it: the void LEVEL. This core sits at a core-average void of 0.395 where this file has long carried ~0.28 for the real machine, unsourced. If 0.28 is right the drawing boils too much, d(void)/d(power) at the true operating point is smaller, and the coefficient could come negative with `aF` back on −1.2 and no FIT at all. That is a source-reading job, not a physics job | high on the signs, the band and the redrawn cell; **high that the behaviour is now right and that it cost one stated FIT**; **high that the flight's old failure was the xenon clock** — four presets read the same way; **the band conversion was wrong on 20/09/26 and is corrected here** - it used this drawing's own beta and rating, which shrank the band 4.1x. **High on the term split and the inversion** (the terms close on the total exactly and each is separable); **high that no published term closes it**; none on why the real machine's own published inputs do not reconstruct its own published coefficient |

Re-measured 20/09/26 on the redrawn cell and the RBMK's own feed temperature (rows "Plant size", "a boiling core's rated flow"): at rest, exit quality **0.140** by the first law and in the channels (`rbmk.js boil`), inlet subcooling **14.9 K** below the drum's own saturation, core-average void **0.264** on the rest pass and **0.273** flown 120 s, flow **3.326 kg/s per MW**. | the drift velocity in the void correlation | Zuber & Findlay (1965): alpha = x / (C0 (x + (1-x) rho_g/rho_f) + rho_g V_gj / G). The second term in the denominator is the velocity the bubbles make against the mixture, and in churn-turbulent bubbly flow V_gj = 1.53 (sigma g (rho_f - rho_g) / rho_f^2)^0.25, about 0.18 m/s for water near 7 MPa. Without it a channel reads the same void at every mass flux | **built 20/09/26.** `eDriftFluxA()` and its inverse `eVoidQualA()` now carry the drift; `eVgjA()` evaluates V_gj once per core per step off the node's own saturation state. Surface tension is a new curve property `sigK` on the IAPWS R1-76 law (`sigmaA()`), stated on water and **zero on every other curve**, so a fluid with no published surface tension gets no drift and reads exactly what it read before. `props.js` checks sigma against IAPWS R1-76 table 1 at 298, 373, 473 and 573 K to 1 %, that it vanishes at the critical point, and V_gj at 7 MPa against the published 0.18 m/s (reads 0.187); an exponent 10 % off fails the 473 K check | on the drawn RBMK-1000 the drift term is **4.2 % of the C0 term at rated flow, 8.3 % at half flow and 20.8 % at a fifth**, so it is a low-flow correction and it is now there. Core-average void 0.274 to **0.270**, d(alpha)/dP 0.003302 to 0.003269. It was measured BEFORE it was built that this could not close the RBMK stability row, and it did not: the disturbance's e-folding went 3.27 s to 3.29 s | **MODEL, closed.** C0 stays a constant 1.13; a pressure- and flow-dependent C0 (Dix, Chexal-Lellouche) is not built and is not claimed | high on Zuber-Findlay and on IAPWS R1-76; the correlation is the churn-turbulent branch only, with no slug or annular form |
| the channel flow split in a boiling core | parallel channels hang between the same two plena and take the same drop, so their flows split on the momentum relation over the WHOLE channel resistance. In an RBMK that resistance is the single-phase inlet throttle valve: the drawing states 1.00 MPa for the throttle and the lower water line, against 0.063 MPa of two-phase friction over the heated length, about 15:1 | until 20/09/26 `csChW` weighted each ring as 1/phi, the two-phase friction multiplier alone, as if the heated length were the channel's whole resistance. Built 20/09/26 (`eCoreStep`, `src/eng/core.js`): the weight is 1/sqrt(dp_throttle + dp_friction phi_i^2/phi_bar^2), normalised, with dp_friction the axial pass's own friction term (`E_AX[2]`) and dp_throttle whatever the stated core drop (`PT.coreDp`, `COOLANT[].dpCore`) has left over. A core that states no drop keeps the old law exactly, which is its right limit. `rbmk.js chan`: the weights carry the whole flow to 1e-12, every channel takes the same drop to 1e-12 (one channel put 5 % out fails it), a uniform void gives a flat split to 1e-12, and zeroing the stated throttle brings the old spread back | on the RBMK the ring flows went from **0.548 .. 1.310 of the mean, a factor 2.39, to 0.979 .. 1.012, a factor 1.034** - what a 15:1 resistance ratio allows. Measured on its own, before the riser bore landed: core-average void 0.276 to 0.271, flux-weighted void 0.318 to 0.237, d(void)/d(power) 0.003950 to 0.003784 per %, the fast power coefficient +3.965 to +3.891 pcm/%. It was measured BEFORE it was built that this could not close the RBMK stability row, and it did not | **MODEL, closed.** Shared by every core, so it reaches BWR/4 too; a PWR's channels barely boil and its split was flat under either law. **Gravity is NOT in the split**: a hotter channel is lighter and that pushes its flow the other way, worth about 0.037 MPa against this drawing's 1.00 MPa throttle. Named, not built | high on the momentum relation; the 1.00 MPa is the drawing's own stated figure for a real RBMK's inlet throttle and lower water line |
| ...and what it does once it IS drawn as a direct cycle | no steam generator and no pressurizer; main circulation pumps of 1.5-2.0 MPa; feed pumps discharging somewhat over drum pressure through the regulating valves | the preset carries a drum per loop, no pressurizer, the governor on `SINK.loadDem`. Re-measured 20/09/26 on the redrawn core (row "Plant size", 775 MWt): coolant pumps bought at **1.236 / 1.253 MPa** (2.498 / 2.373 before the riser bore was fixed on 20/09/26, 2.84 / 2.64 on the 1037 MWt drawing) passing 1289.5 kg/s each; the feed pump bought at **9.367 MPa** into 6.9 MPa loops, down from 14.63 when `circHeadOf()` was taught the drum fence and from 10.45 when the feed-line bore was fixed (see the distance). The drawing states `rodSpd` 0.4/7 (row "RBMK-1000 power regulator"), and the rod banks are priced for it | coolant pumps now **1.24 MPa against a real MCP's 1.5-2.0, about 18 % under the low edge**, where they were +39 % over the midpoint and +57 % before the redraw. Nothing was tuned: the riser bore was fixed on physics and the head followed. The feed train still throttles most of its head away, which a real one does not. **CLASSED 20/09/26 (`tools/pumphead.js`, all nine presets), and the class is MODEL, not BUILD.** The feed pump's bought head splits into a `loopHeadAt()` base plus a `PUMP_MARGIN` term. `PUMP_MARGIN` behaves everywhere. The whole anomaly was the base term: 0.018-0.050 MPa on all eight other presets, **5.169 MPa on RBMK**. The cause is `circHeadOf()`, not the drawing. On a direct cycle the feed shares the primary's CIRCUIT, so the feed pump falls through `loopMap().partLoop` to `circHeadOf()`, which summed every run of that circuit at its own duty - the whole recirculation the drum stands at the top of. `loopHeadOf()` already cuts at `drumFence()`; `circHeadOf()` did not, and 20/09/26 it was taught to, using the `inLoop()`/`loopNodes()` walk that was already there for the same purpose. Base term **5.169 to 1.135 MPa**, bought 14.63 to 10.45, discharge 21.6 to 12.65. `tools/pumphead.js` on all nine before and after: **every other preset's bought head is bit-identical**, because only a drum fence triggers the branch and only the RBMK has one. **The residual 1.135 MPa is CLASSIFIED 20/09/26, and it is MODEL, in the BORE SUGGESTION.** It is three runs: `feed:efwpl-efwteer` 0.577 MPa at a 59 mm bore on 32 kg/s, `feed:efwteeb-feedl` 0.271 and `feed:feedl-freg1b` 0.223, both 203 mm on 374 kg/s. All three run at **15.0-15.2 m/s**, where the SAME runs on STOCK PWR are 138 mm and 635 mm and run at **3.0 m/s**. The cause is one predicate: `runBoreSuggest()` sizes a liquid run at `runOnLeg(r) ? circCoolOf(ci).vLeg : V_LIQ`, and `runOnLeg()` asks only whether the run's CIRCUIT has a core on it. On a direct cycle the feed shares the primary's circuit, so a feedwater line is sized at the main-coolant-leg velocity the coolant row states - `vLeg` 15 m/s for LWGR - instead of a feed line's 3. The bore is therefore sqrt(5) too small and the drop 25x too big. `circHeadOf()`'s pricing of what IS drawn is right: recomputed by hand run by run it reproduces 1.1354 MPa exactly. **FIXED 20/09/26.** The drum-fence walk was tried first and is WRONG: it cuts at the feed regulating valve, so the two runs carrying the pump's whole 1289 kg/s into its suction fell outside it and were re-bored 754 -> 1686 mm at 0.7 m/s. The predicate that is right is the FLOW: `runOnLeg()` now asks whether the run's duty IS `legDutyKgs()`, which is an identity and not a threshold - measured across all nine, every leg reads a duty ratio of exactly 1.00000 and the nearest non-leg reads 0.29. RBMK feed runs 59 -> **132 mm**, 203 -> **454 mm** x2, all three now at 3.0 m/s; the feed base head **1.1354 -> 0.0573 MPa**, bought 10.445 -> 9.367. It reaches more than the feed line, and the siblings are the same bug: the pressurizer **surge line** and the **HPI injection line** were bored at `vLeg` on every preset and are not recirculation legs either (STOCK surge -> 224 mm, HPI 102 -> 239 mm, at 3 m/s against a real PWR surge line's 356 mm and ~1.2 m/s, so the direction is right and the distance is smaller). Relief runs are untouched - `runReliefBore()` short-circuits them to the valve's own bore. BWR/4 is bit-identical: it is drawn through steam generators, has no drum and so no run of it changes class. **The MCP residual is classified too, and it is a deliberate CUT in the drawing.** Priced run by run at rated flow the drawn loop is 1.1475 MPa on the cold leg (of which the drawing's own stated 1.000 MPa core throttle is K 11.56 of the run's K 13.3, leaving 0.15 MPa of pipe friction), 0.048 + 0.033 on the hot legs and 0.008 on the suction. The real MCP's 1.5-2.0 MPa also pays for group distribution headers, 1661 individual channel throttle valves, pressure and suction headers and downcomers - kilometres of circuit this board draws as four runs. The 0.26-0.76 MPa shortfall IS that missing pipework, so it is a cut, not a pricing defect. The MCPs are MODEL too, and they are FIXED. `loopHeadOf()` broke pump0's 2.498 MPa into 1.148 on the cold leg (of which 1.000 is the drawing's own stated inlet throttle) and **1.342 MPa on the two hot legs**, which carried 1289 kg/s of a 193 kg/m3 mixture through a 377 mm bore at **60 m/s**. `runBoreSuggest()` sized every run at `circDesRho()`, which knew only liquid and vapour and handed a two-phase riser the circuit's LIQUID density. It now asks `circHotRho()` for a boiling circuit's hot side, which is the mixture at the design exit enthalpy - the same split `loopHeadOf()`'s own `runHotSide()` already made for the pressure drop, so this is a second caller of one idea and not a second idea. RBMK risers 377 to **754 mm**, MCP head **2.498 to 1.236 MPa**, and the core node came back from 8.07 to **6.99 MPa**, which is where a real RBMK channel sits (inlet header ~8.3, after the throttle ~7.6, outlet ~7.0, drum 6.9) | **MODEL on both halves, one fixed and one stated.** The feed pump's is fixed: a shared pricing law that skipped a fence it already had. It moves exactly one preset because only one preset has a drum fence - a defect can be in shared code and still reach one drawing. The MCPs' is fixed in `runBoreSuggest()`. It reaches every boiling circuit, so it moves BWR/4 too, and there it is a clear gain: BWR/4's 120 s void fraction went **0.786 to 0.365** against a published 0.25-0.55, closing that row's own check, and its four settle failures went to none. The cost is on BWR/4's first seconds: the enlarged riser holds four times the water and the plant now rings through a swing of about 40 % of rated over the first 2 s before settling, so its 3 s rest check goes 3.14 to **400 pcm** while its 120 s march is clean. That is the "BWR/4 as a direct cycle" backlog item - its drum IS the vessel - and it is named, not chased. The seven non-boiling presets are bit-identical, checked on `tools/pumphead.js` across all nine | high on the MCP band and on the hot-leg arithmetic; medium on a real feed pump's discharge; **high on the MODEL class for both halves** - the feed pump's rests on eight presets reading bit-identical before and after, the MCPs' on the 60 m/s the drawn riser runs at |
| RBMK core inlet pumping | an adiabatic pump leaves all of its shaft work in the water: between the drum and the core inlet the enthalpy must rise by at least v (p_in - p_drum), the isentropic figure, and a real pump exceeds it | **built 20/09/26.** `ePumpWorkA()` (`src/eng/transport.js`) hands every pump's shaft work to its discharge node as an enthalpy source on the flow the solve passed: w (p_out - p_in) / rho_suction / `PUMP_ETA`, with `PUMP_ETA` 0.85 the hydraulic efficiency of a large single-stage centrifugal pump. It goes through `eSrcAdd()` like every other source, so `SC_ENRES` closes and no mass moves. The expression is the INCOMPRESSIBLE one, so a machine whose discharge density is more than 2 % over its suction gets nothing. Commissioning's tube fit (`eSettleUA()`) now sizes the generators for the core's heat AND the primary pumps' work, or the plant sits about 1 % hotter than it was commissioned for | the RBMK's pumps now leave **3.179 kJ/kg** in the water against an isentropic minimum of 2.701, where 0.96 used to arrive (the figures fell with the pump heads when the riser bore was fixed, its own row). `rbmk.js boil` checks the pumps' own first law, w (h_out - h_in) = P_shaft, to **3.4e-4**, and the drum-and-core circuit's first law closes to **0.03 %** with the MCPs' 5.0 MW counted (`rbmk.js rest`, `drum.js 5`). The 0.36 kJ/kg still between the drum mixing statement and the core inlet is the two loops' drums leaving their downcomers at slightly different enthalpies - a drum asymmetry, not a pump term, and not chased. **A gas circulator gets no shaft work at all**: v dp overstates a compressor 6.8x on CALDER HALL's CO2 (442 kJ/kg against the isentropic 65 at its 1.94 pressure ratio), so the incompressible form is refused there rather than applied wrongly | **MODEL, closed for liquids; deliberately not built for gases.** Shared code, so it moves every preset: the tube fit absorbs it on the seven with steam generators, and BN-600's 120 s heat balance goes 1.053 to 1.072 on a check that already failed on the tree before this. The compressible form c_p T ((p2/p1)^((gam-1)/gam) - 1)/eta is NOT built, and Calder Hall's circulators really do put a few MW into their gas | high on the law; `PUMP_ETA` 0.85 is a class figure for a large centrifugal pump, not this machine's own |
| the flow work on a node that is nearly empty | a node's internal energy is U = m·u with u = h − p·v at the contents' OWN pressure and specific volume; contents too few to fill the volume at the solved pressure cannot hold that pressure's p·V | **since 21/09/26** `eAdvectStep()` books U = m·h − p_c·V with p_c = p·min(1, m/(V·ρ(p,h))), the contents' pressure where they fall short of the solved state (exact for an ideal gas at fixed T), and `pAdv` carries p_c tick to tick so the ledger books what the update did. A node drained past u = 0 (the 273.15 K datum) lands at u = 0 and the difference is a booked clamp. Before: `turbb` (0.006 kg in 11 m3) took V·dp of a 0.0024 -> 3.27 MPa jump and read h 5.9e6 kJ/kg, `condt` flashed to 7.72 MPa and both radiators failed on OVERPRESSURE, while `SC_ENRES` read 1e-7 because it booked U at the same solved pressure. After: worst h over 9 s of the STOCK PWR steam-line break 3028 kJ/kg, condenser peak 0.037 MPa, `SC_ENRES` 5e-8; STOCK PWR 120 s energy closes to 4e-12 and its heat balance reads 1.013 | the contents' pressure is the ideal-gas scaling of the solved one, not a solved EOS p(ρ, u); the u = 0 floor is a guard on an explicit donor at CFL ~1, not a law | YES on the scaling (a solved p(ρ, u) per node is heavy) | high on the measurements; not injected: no check fails on the old law |
| RBMK scram and the displacer | INSAG-7: every RCPS rod driven at 0.4 m/s, 18–21 s for the full stroke; a 4.5 m graphite displacer hangs under each absorber on a telescope with 1.25 m of water above and below it at full withdrawal, so a scram first drives the bottom water out and adds reactivity in the lower core, by an amount that depends on the power shape | `SCRAM` MOTOR DRIVEN, `rate` 0.4/7 of travel a second, mass 20 t (the gravity row's rods and drives, a servo in place of the latch; no measured figure); ARCHPRE RBMK takes it. `FOLL.tipLen`/`tipGap` are fractions of the core's height: GRAPHITE DISPLACER 4.5/7 and 1.25/7, WATER and BORATED STEEL a gap of 0 (their behaviour before, BORATED STEEL's 4.0 nodes now 0.4 of the height); `follHi()` (core2d.js) is the one expression the engine and the design copy read, and the bench draws the gap. `rbmk.js scram`: stroke 17.5 s; the bank's bottom node fills with graphite after **3.13 s** (1.25 / 0.4 = 3.125); from full withdrawal on a bottom-leaning flux (AO −0.18, a xenon tilt) rods and followers add **+77 pcm (0.11 β)** in the first 3.1 s, on a top-leaning one (AO +0.27) −23 pcm; injected, a water follower, the rise is gone | stroke 17.5 s against 18–21 (the drive speed is the stated figure); INSAG-7 gives no magnitude for the positive scram, so +77 pcm is not measured against anything | **FIT** on `tipRho` 1200 (no stated source for the displacer's worth per unit length); the geometry and the speed are the published ones. | rod drive speed | a PWR bank steps at most about 72 steps/min over a ~228-step stroke, roughly three minutes end to end | **closed 20/09/26.** `ROD_SPD0` is 1/190 of travel a second, a 190 s stroke. CALDER HALL no longer holds rated on it: the bank walks out to the `arLo` 0.10 floor and power fades to 0.06 in 120 s (BUILD — the preset's steam side is half-drawn and its commissioning point needs more withdrawal than the floor allows; the tune does not read drive speed, observed not changed). STOCK, MSRE, RBMK-1000 hold rated; `kinetics.js` passes | decided 20/09/26: the reference is the published stroke | medium |

## Heat and thermal limits

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the axial pressure profile in a channel | a heated channel loses pressure up its length to the weight of the column, wall friction and the acceleration of a mixture that is getting lighter. Saturation temperature follows the pressure down, so water saturated at the bottom is superheated against the local saturation higher up and flashes with no heat added | **built 20/09/26** (`eCoreAxialA()`, `src/eng/core.js`). Ten plane pressures per core, gravity + Darcy friction (Blasius under Re 1e5, Filonenko/Petukhov over it) + acceleration, homogeneous two-phase multiplier, liquid density at the plane's own temperature. Anchored so the ten planes average `ST.csPCore[c]` exactly, so the pressure network is untouched and the profile adds no pressure. The march now reads `T_sat`, `h_fg`, ρ_g/ρ_f, the drift, the Jens-Lottes superheat and W-3's pressure at each plane's own pressure. Checked (`rbmk.js axial`): the anchor holds to 1e-12; with the flow stopped and the void flattened the profile is 45202 Pa against IF97's 45275 Pa of column weight; the friction share is 36486 Pa against 36427 Pa by hand at the same Re; the flashing is 0.00298 against IF97's 0.00315 | RBMK-1000, the deepest case: **0.095 MPa over the 6.30 m the ten plane centres span**, of which 0.052 MPa friction at Re 3.7e5, against the **1.00 MPa the `LWGR` row draws across the core**. That is not a defect in either: the drawn figure is the whole inlet-throttle-plus-pipework loss, which sits on the EDGE upstream of the heated length, and this is the heated length's own. Flashing is worth **2.1 % of the channel's exit quality**. The 5.3 % the flashing check sits under IF97 is structural: the march carries `h` as `c_p·T`, so its `dh_f/dp` is the coolant row's secant `c_p` times `dT_sat/dp` | — | high: every term is checked against IF97 or against the correlation by hand |

| ...its level | **sourced 20/09/26.** Two different numbers, and they must not be confused. The correlation's DESIGN LIMIT: W-3 1.30 (Tong 1967, the parent row's own correlation); WRB-1/WRB-2 about 1.17; WRB-2M ≥1.14 for normal operation and anticipated operational occurrences, with RTDP transient limits 1.21–1.25 (AP1000 DCD Tier 2 §4.4, NRC ML071580898). The value a real plant actually SITS at at 100 % power is commonly given as 1.8–2.0 for a Westinghouse-type PWR, a margin of 0.5–0.7 over the 1.30 limit — but no named-plant FSAR table stating a steady-state (not transient-initial) minimum DNBR was located, so that half is a commonly-cited range and not a pinned figure | tied to a bought `COOLANT.dnbr` column by `P.dnbrK`. The PWR row states 1.85, and `commissionGen()` solves `P.dnbrK` so the raw W-3 result times `dnbrK` equals it exactly, so the stock PWR reads **1.85 at rated by construction** | level is bought, not derived — confirmed in the code, not merely suspected. 1.85 sits inside the commonly-cited 1.8–2.0 band and well above every design limit found, so the level is plausible; the comparison's weak half is the band, not the model. **Measured 20/09/26 (`core.js 0`), which is new:** with the constant stood down the correlation gives the stock PWR a minimum DNBR of **2.177** at rest, inside the 2.0–2.5 a real PWR is commonly given at 100 % power, and the fit then buys that down to 1.85 with `dnbrK` 0.850. So on this preset the fit is NOT covering a broken correlation. It is doing real work elsewhere, and `dnbrK` is nowhere near 1, so it is not dead code and may not simply be deleted: 0.616 NUSCALE (raw 3.00), 0.640 RBMK (raw 2.50), 0.850 PWR (raw 2.18), 1.236 EPR (raw 1.50), 1.432 CALDER HALL, 2.138 BN-600, 0.645 MSRE, and **4.609 on BWR/4, whose raw answer is 0.336** — the correlation says that core is already in departure and the constant lifts it to the drawn 1.55 | FIT on PWR, NUSCALE and EPR, where the raw answer is the right order. **MODEL on BWR/4 and, from 20/09/26, MODEL on RBMK-1000 as well.** The RBMK's raw minimum DNBR is measured off its own rest pass with `dnbrK` stood down: **2.786**, bought down to 1.60 by `dnbrK` **0.5745**. The raw figure is not the point - the RBMK is a boiling channel at a core-average void of 0.395 and an exit quality of 0.145, the top of W-3's stated -0.15 to +0.15 quality window, and its hot channel is past it - so the LAW is the wrong one for the same reason it is wrong on BWR/4, and its `dnbrK` buys a local CHF ratio where the machine's own design criterion was a critical power ratio over the boiling length. The missing term is named, not measured: a boiling core's margin is a critical-POWER ratio integrated over the boiling length (GEXL, CISE-4), not a local CHF ratio, and this asks W-3 with a Biasi fallback above quality 0.15 | **medium** — high on the design limits, low on the operating band, high on the measured `dnbrK` spread |

| clad temperature at a boiling node | BWR exit about 570 K, just above saturation; PWR hot-channel clad 618–623 K | re-read 10/09/26 after the field inlet, its lag and the `LWGR` re-pin: **BWR 570.5 K**, **PWR 621.8 K**, **RBMK 568.3 K** (08/09/26 read 571.1, 621.8, 567.0). `coreStep()` is a real two-leg series now — a fixed solid conductance (pellet, gap, clad wall) and a live coolant film — and the film is the better of a single-phase flow term and Jens-Lottes (1951) nucleate boiling, `25·(q″/1e6)^0.25·exp(−p/6.2)`. The bareness penalty moved off the node's total void onto `vLeak`, the vessel's own shortfall of water: bubbles are a bonus, an uncovered rod is not. **Superseded for BWR/4 from 10/09/26**: after the loop-head work that preset rests at `flowNet` 0.567 and `n` 0.507 at 8 s, so 573.8 K is no longer a reading at its old operating point — hottest clad 567.2 K against saturation 567.3. The literal did not move; what it multiplies did. It was 0.10 of the whole pellet-to-coolant path, i.e. 221 W/m²/K — an order under the band, and it degraded the pellet's own conduction, which departure cannot do | medium on the band, high that the old target was under it |

| ...and the correlation's own ceiling | Cathcart-Pawel (ORNL/NUREG-17, 1977) is measured 1273–1773 K. Urbanic & Heidrick (J. Nucl. Mater. 75, 1978) is measured 1323–2123 K, two branches split at 1850 K (the alpha-beta change). The H2 and the heat follow ALL the oxygen the metal takes up, not only the oxide layer. NRC ML021680052 reads Urbanic 3 % under Cathcart-Pawel at 1473 K | **rewritten 21/09/26.** `eOxRateA()`: Cathcart-Pawel's total-oxygen weight-gain law, 0.3622·exp(−39940/RT) (g/cm²)²/s, to `E_OX_TSW` 1773 K; Urbanic-Heidrick 29.6·exp(−16820/T) to 1850 K and 87.9·exp(−16610/T) above, (kg Zr/m²)²/s. All three are carried as ZrO2-equivalent thickness, so the ECR is the licensing ECR. Baker-Just is gone. The Cathcart-Pawel branch used to be the oxide-LAYER law (2.252e-6·exp(−18063/T)), which leaves out the oxygen dissolved in the metal: it under-made H2 and heat by 26 % at 1473 K. `ZR_H2` is now exact, 2·2.01588/91.224. Checked (`clad.js ox`): weight gain at 1473 K, 100 s against the published law; Urbanic/Cathcart 0.932 at 1473 K against NRC's 0.97; no temperature 1073–2100 K is answered above its law's data (fault: a switch at 1850 K goes red at 1774 K); H2/Zr = stoichiometry to 1e-9 over a bared core in steam | **1.** The rate constant steps DOWN to 0.640× at 1773 K (the H2 rate to 0.80×). The two measurements disagree there; the code reproduces both. **2.** The law is asked from `E_OX_T0` 1073 K, 200 K under Cathcart-Pawel's 1273 K floor (a GAP in `clad.js ox`). **3.** Above ~2100 K the clad is molten and Urbanic-Heidrick is still asked | **1** no, it is the data. **2** DRIFT, not weighed. **3** YES, the clad has no liquid state here | high on the laws (read at NRC ML021680052 and the KNS 2006 CATHENA restatement of Urbanic-Heidrick, not at the original papers) |
| what a failed pin releases | NUREG-1465: a burst clad vents the GAP (5 % of the noble gases and of the volatiles, none of the refractories) and then stops; a molten pellet lets go of the early in-vessel share on top (PWR: noble 0.95, iodine 0.35, Ba-Sr 0.02; BWR iodine 0.25). The groups move differently: noble gases are insoluble and leave with the steam; halogens and alkali dissolve and stay in the water; refractories stay in the vessel. TMI-2: essentially all the noble gas reached the building, about 1e-5 of the core iodine did | **rewritten 21/09/26; the dose-fit `E_RELK` is deleted.** Three groups, each carried as one nuclide's equilibrium mass at rated power (Xe-133, I-131, Ba-140; yield × fission rate / λ). `eFpRelease()`: per node, NUREG-1465's gap share × the clad's failure (1 from ECR 17 %) + its in-vessel share × the melt, weighted by the node's rest flux; the released kg is one-way (`csFpRel*`). Noble gas and volatiles land in the core's water as hydrogen does (`fpNBy`, `fpVBy`) and ride `eAdvSp()`, the one donor law hydrogen now also rides: a gas with the vapour, a dissolved species with the water (the steam at 1/1e4 of it). Noble gas also takes hydrogen's bubble rise (`eGasRise()`, both callers). At an opening the noble gas rides the plume, the volatiles stay in the water it pours (`roomFpW`) save the flashed steam's 1/1e4. The room's airborne fields ride `eRoomAdvect()` and the gas moves beside hydrogen. A salt core's inventory is in its salt from commissioning. Checked (`clad.js fp`, `fb`): gap 0.05/0.05/0 and melt 1.00/0.40/0.02 to 1e-12, one-way; books (fuel + water + room + booked) close to 3e-16 of inventory over 6 s with a hot-leg pipe shot; building air after 6 s: 94.5 % of the released noble gas, 2.9e-5 of the released volatiles, 99.997 % of the volatiles in water; noble gas and hydrogen leave the same openings in the same share to 1e-9 until the clad makes hydrogen of its own; both faults (fractions zeroed) go red | **1.** Three nuclides stand for the whole inventory; their yields, half-lives, gamma energies, the NUREG-1465 fractions and the 1e4 partition are as commonly quoted, **not read at source**. **2.** The released mass does not decay: a tracer at its release-time mass. **3.** Refractories never leave the vessel; with a melt-through they should go with the corium. **4.** No NUREG-1465 table exists for sodium, salt or gas cores: they use the PWR one. **5.** The coolant-activity terms that already fed `SC_RELEASE` (SGTR, relief, tank burst) stay as they were; `SC_RELEASE` no longer hears from the fuel | **1** no, sources to read. **2, 3, 4** YES. **5** YES, a separate model of coolant activity | medium on the fractions, high on the ordering and the books |

What is physics is its SHAPE: the room's fission products weigh in as gamma power over rated (λ·N_A/M × mean gamma MeV per kg), on an r⁻² kernel with the board's own attenuation from the crew's box (`PT.radAirK`, the same law the core's kernel uses); moving 1 kg from the nearest cell to the farthest divides the dose by the kernel's ratio exactly (`clad.js fp`). The mean gamma energies (0.045, 0.38, 0.18 MeV) are as commonly quoted, not read at source; the kernel is centred on the crew's box, not on each crew cell | the whole absolute scale | YES, decided in the batch H plan | high on the shape, none claimed on the scale |

A steam nozzle (`ed.gasAt`, below) hands over the node's gas at the vapour's own concentration `c/x`, capped at what the node holds. A torn pipe still passes its own MIXTURE, deliberately: a hole in a wall is not phase-selective and a homogeneous mixture sample carries its proportional share | high that it is insoluble and buoyant and that TMI-2 vented it through the PORV; **medium** on 0.25 m/s, where published bubble drift is 0.2–0.35 m/s |

| **sodium meeting air** | hot sodium **burns**, and a pool fire and a spray fire are two different accidents | both, 07/09/26. `COOLANT.burn` names the fuel and `FIRE` (room.js) states it. Both feed the same `q` the hydrogen deflagration does, so the fire and the blast cannot disagree. Measured on a BN-600 hot-leg burst: a spray flash in the first second, 96 kPa, air to 1065 K, then a pool fire holding the bay at ~615 K. **Re-measured 11/09/26 on the one-field room, to 20 s (the longest run that fits 10 s):** peak `s.roomPMax` 3.4 kPa, air 1525 K, 3 / 6 / 9 cells burning and 28 / 171 / 256 kg on the deck at 1 / 5 / 10 s; the holding figure was not reached in the window. The flash pressure fell because the per-cell gas is isothermal (row: the gas is isothermal). Not a preset failure; nobody has classified it. **20/09/26: a longer window is owed and its length is now stated.** No sodium-specific time-to-quasi-steady figure was found, but the general pool fire literature puts the rise to a quasi-steady burning rate at one minute at the fastest and commonly ten minutes or more, driven by pool heating and edge effects; sodium's oxide skin and diffusion-limited surface argue for the same or longer. The window owed is **300–900 s**, 15–45× what was run, and it has to chunk through `engSnap()`/`engRestore()` to fit the 10 s script budget | the fire is there; the aerosol is not — see the rows under it | — | high on the behaviour |

The ratio is **10–100×**, so the "one to two orders" already on this row is corroborated. The transition is not a fade at all: it happens inside a wall-superheat band of about 100–150 K at the Leidenfrost point, and the trigger is a HEAT FLUX criterion, never a liquid inventory | **the row was wrong about the code, corrected 20/09/26.** `SX.fWet[i]` (`src/eng/net.js:394`) is a `u8` BINARY GATE, not a fraction: a node is dry when its mass falls under `DRY_FRAC` (1e-3) of what its own state point weighs AND the inflow will not make that up inside the step, and `eSrcAdd()` (`src/eng/transport.js:99`) then multiplies every machine source — core, SG, condenser, radiator, pressurizer — by 0 or by 1. Full duty down to 99.9 % dry, then a cliff to nothing. There is no linear region anywhere and there never was; `git log -S` finds the line unchanged since it was introduced. Measured on a severed hot leg: the core node reached 5.5e3 kJ/kg over 600 s, against 4.4e7 kJ/kg (4.4e7 K on the labels) before the scale existed, and the stock ship moved 370.21 → 370.22 MWe | the shape is right in kind — a dry surface heats no water — and a STEP is closer to the real boiling-curve collapse than a linear fade would be. So the model holds full duty through the whole boiling crisis and then over-collapses past it. The ratio is the row's 10–100× either side of the trigger; the error is the trigger. **The one place a real boiling curve IS modelled is the clad**, on a different path: `src/eng/core.js:406` latches `csNDnb` on a DNB ratio under 1 and holds it until the clad superheat falls back under `E_DT_LEID` 150 K, and `E_DNB_FILM` 0.10 cuts the coefficient tenfold — a sourced trigger and a sourced factor at the low end of the 10–100× band. The plant-node gate has neither. The wall term is not scaled here because its own ceiling (`m·|Δh|/dt`) already is the node's mass, and `s.metalT` reads `metalQ` back. **Attempted 20/09/26 and STOPPED, with the reason: `fWet` carries two meanings and only one of them is wrong.** Five readers. | heat deposited outside the fuel | not all of a fission's energy lands in the pin, and the gammas that carry most of what leaves it are born in the fuel, so a cell much bigger than a gamma mean free path shields itself. Recoverable MeV per U-235 fission (**ENDF/B-VIII.0** `n-092_U_235.endf` MF=1 MT=458 at thermal): fragments 169.130 and delayed betas 6.500 ± 0.050 in the fuel; prompt neutrons' 4.8276 and delayed neutrons' 0.008074 where the slowing-down is; prompt gamma 7.2813 and delayed gamma 6.330 ± 0.050 wherever the photons stop, plus the capture gamma MT=458 does not carry because it is a property of the lattice and not of the fission (3–12 MeV, Lamarsh 1975 via INL/EXT-13-29256 Table 1, 7.5 taken). A real LWR generates **97.4 %** of its heat in the fuel (AP1000 DCD Rev. 19, Table 4.4-1, NRC ML11171A446) and an RBMK-1000 stops **~5.5 %** in its graphite (INSAG-7); no Magnox or Calder Hall graphite figure was found (20/09/26). **No BWR-specific split was found either (searched 20/09/26:** GESTAR II NEDE-24011-P-A, which is proprietary; the GE BWR/4 Technology Manual §1.7, NRC ML11258A296; NUREG/CR-7164, 7194, 7224, 7240; the TRACE, RETRAN and RELAP5 manuals**)**. The only figure that surfaces is the generic LWR direct-moderator-heating convention of about 2.6 %, which is the same AP1000 number already quoted and not a BWR measurement. **The BWR gap therefore has no target**, and the ~4 % it was written against was never sourced | **transport built 20/09/26**, replacing a uniform-fluence law (mass × μ_en/ρ) built the same day. `FIS_FN` 0.02562, `FIS_FGP` 0.07831 (capture gamma at the middle of its 3–12) and `FIS_FGD` 0.49338 off that evaluation, which replaced the Lamarsh table's 0.02667 / 0.07733 / 0.46667 on 20/09/26 — the table's 8 MeV of delayed beta and 7 of delayed gamma are 17 % above ENDF's evaluated 12.830 ± 0.07 total; every `COOLANT`, `FUEL`, `CLAD`, `MODER` and `ABSORB` row states `muen` and `muAt`, μ_en/ρ and μ/ρ cm²/g at 1 MeV, mass-weighted over its own atoms off NIST (Hubbell & Seltzer, NISTIR 5632). `heatCellOf()` cuts the drawn cell per unit height into six regions — fuel, can, coolant, blocks, pressure tube, drawn absorber — each with its own Σ = ρ·μ/ρ, mean chord 4V/S and deposited fraction f = μ_en/μ. `heatCP()` runs a fixed point: a photon born in region r collides there with Wigner's Σl/(1+Σl) and otherwise anywhere in the cell by weight Σ·V·(1−e^−Σl)/Σl, an infinite lattice opening onto an identical cell; a collision leaves f behind and sends 1−f off again, to 1e-12 of the source or 64 passes. Gamma energy that leaves the **core** (first-collision escape 1/(1+Σ̄·L), L = 4V/S of the whole core) is booked to the structures. Void thins the coolant's Σ; rod coverage scales the absorber's volume. `heatShares()` solves the cell on a 5 × 5 (void × coverage) grid, caches it on the core bag, and ships four gamma shares per point into `PT.coreHsTab`; `eHeatSplitA()` interpolates them bilinearly and `heatSplitA()` adds the neutron term exactly in the tick, so the step where the last of the water goes is not ramped over a grid interval. Structure and absorber heat joins the water's in `csDQ`. `latRating()` = pin limit ÷ pin share, so ratings follow. Rest shares pin/water/blocks/structures, bank at its commissioning position: PWR 96.84/2.97/0/0.182, NUSCALE and DUAL 97.02/2.78/0/0.200, BWR/4 96.99/2.84/0/0.168, BN-600 97.25/2.58/0/0.168, EPR 96.61/3.17/0/0.221, RBMK-1000 95.68/1.96/1.93/0.437, MSRE 95.59/2.31/1.89/0.210, CALDER HALL 95.39/0.03/4.34/0.242 %; with the bank driven fully in the absorber takes 0.113, 0.199, 0.170, 0.041, 0.110, 0.024, 0.026 and 0.010 % of core heat. `heatsplit.js` on published cells typed in the test: the PWR 17×17 reads **3.01 %** outside the fuel rod against 2.6 (+15.8 %, PASS 30 %; the uniform-fluence law read 3.23); the RBMK cell **7.18 %** in graphite against 5.5 (**+30.5 %, GAP**, over the 30 % tolerance by half a point; the uniform-fluence law read 9.79, injected as the fault and fails); a Calder Hall zone B cell reads 5.14 % against 10.25 on the old law (GAP, source unconfirmed). The thin-cell limit against mass × μ_en to 2.6e-12 (injected, the re-emitted 1−f dropped: 0.41, fails); deposition closes over the cell to 1e-12; every pass conserves to 1e-12; the 5 × 5 table against the fixed point solved at (0.1, 0.1), (0.3, 0.6), (0.6, 0.3) and (0.9, 0.9) within 6.4e-5 (PWR) and 8.0e-5 (RBMK) of a share, under 1 % of the largest solved there; every `muen` and `muAt` against NIST typed a second time, 1 % (injected ×1.1 fails). `fuel.js`: engine shares over void × coverage against the law by hand to 7e-18; a node at α 1 takes exactly 0 water; a node at coverage 0 takes exactly 0 absorber; after a scram with n forced to 0 the direct heat is the delayed-gamma term summed by hand to 1e-12 | **one photon energy**: 1 MeV throughout, and a Compton-scattered photon is re-emitted at 1 MeV, so the model carries no spectrum softening. Capture gammas are born in the fuel with the fission gammas, where really they are born where the captures are. **The cell boundary opens onto an identical cell** — the one modelling choice here not forced by physics, and the one the RBMK cell fails on: a graphite column in a real RBMK stack touches graphite across that face, so its mean chord is longer than the 4V/S used and the model under-shields it. That is why the cell reads +30.5 % rather than nearer INSAG's 5.5, and it is the term to take next if the row is reopened. No transport between core nodes, and the escape from the core is a first-collision escape priced once on the born gammas, with the reflector, vessel or shield round it taken as stopping all of it (true to within e^−9 for any of them at 1 MeV) — so a bare core would still lose that energy to "structures". Structure and absorber heat lands in the coolant instantly rather than in their own metal, and a shield outside the vessel heats the water rather than the room. The absorber's volume is added to the cell rather than displacing the coolant it sits in, and it does not enter the moderation book. Design shares are a 5 × 5 grid, and the rating is taken at α = 0 with the bank out, so a boiling core's rating is set on more water than it runs with. The fuel's own neutron scattering is still ignored: all 2.67 % of neutron energy goes to the moderators, even where the coolant barely moderates. What moved, uniform-fluence law on the Lamarsh partition → transport on ENDF's: ratings PWR 1236.6 → 1236.0 MWt, NUSCALE and DUAL 715.1 → 714.8, BWR/4 1089.9 → 1089.2, BN-600 1013.5 → 1012.6, EPR 997.7 → 997.7, RBMK-1000 1072.1 → 1072.4, MSRE 870.4 → 869.9, **CALDER HALL 207.1 → 200.6 (−3.1 %)** — only the graphite-heavy drawing moves, because only a thick cell shields itself. MWe at 60 s, dice off, seed 1: 377.2 → 377.0, 220.7 → 220.6, 306.6 → 308.8, 324.7 → 325.3, 304.9 → 304.9, 333.2 → 333.3, 272.5 → 272.4, 51.9 → 49.7, DUAL 431.1 → 430.9. `TfRef − Tref` and `pwrDef` hold to the digit on every preset. Plant mass falls where the drawn absorber replaced the old 6 %-of-a-ring balance figure: 826 → 821 t, 641 → 639, 624 → 619, 376 → 376, 839 → 834, 935 → 933, 500 → 499, **6333 → 6278**, 633 → 631. The suggestion is the RCCA: 24 rodlets of 0.341 in. Absorber volume per unit core height is `latAbsA()` = rodded slots in the drawn quarter × `absN` × π/4 × `absD`², over the slots the lattice already carries a bank on (`c.lat.rod`); `latMass()` weighs that volume in `ABSORB[].dens`, and every `ABSORB` row now also states `muen` and `muAt` so the absorber is a gamma region of the cell (row "heat deposited outside the fuel").
