| gap conductance | h_gap moves with burnup, gas and temperature, a few to tens of kW/m²K; the gap closes in service. Open gap: gas conductivity over the gap width plus the surfaces' roughness (Ross & Stoute 1962). Xe and Kr conduct ~25× worse than He, mixed by NUREG/CR-7024 eq. 4.1-4 | **rewritten 21/09/26.** `H_GAP` 5.7 kW/m²K is the gap at each node's REST point with its own rest gas, by construction (`clad.js fg`: 0 W/m²K off at every node); on a core with no gas out at rest that is all helium. Only gas let go SINCE rest poisons it. Away from rest the gap is `k_mix(T_gap)/(δ + δ_r)`: δ + δ_r = k_He(T_gap,rest)/H_GAP less the pellet's growth over the clad's (UO2 on FRAP's strain law, NUREG/CR-7024 eq. 2.5-1; Zircaloy diametral 6.721e-6/K, eq. 3.5-9; the metal rows on their `alpha` plus a step at each phase change), never under δ_r = 1.5·(2.0 + 0.5) µm. The gas is the rod's own He + released Xe/Kr (FRAP A·T^B, Table 4.1-2). Built as its own column (`PT.coreGGap`, `pinRes().gap`); the pellet + clad wall stay in `PT.coreGSolid`. Checked: poisoned gap = H_GAP·k_mix/k_He to 4e-16; the pellet rise follows the series resistance to 2e-15; a closed gap reads 47 kW/m²K | **1.** δ_r and the roughnesses (FRAPCON defaults 2.0/0.5 µm) are as commonly quoted, **not read at source**. **2.** No solid-contact term (Ross-Stoute h_s): a closed gap is gas-only, so under a real one. **3.** A closed gap does NOT load the clad: no PCMI hoop strain, the burst curve prices pressure only. **4.** The gap gas is at the node's clad-side temperature. **5.** Zircaloy's expansion is its alpha-phase slope at every temperature; FRAP goes to 9.7e-6/K above 1273 K. **6.** The rest gap is taken as H_GAP, not built from an as-drawn cold gap | **1** no, a source to read. **2, 3** YES, a gas gap is the mechanism asked for; PCMI needs a clad stress model this has not got. **4, 5, 6** YES, small | medium |
| a pellet past `tdmg` | UO2 lets its fission gas go by diffusion to the grain faces, climbing steeply above ~1500 K (Booth; Turnbull's D). The gas raises the rod's pressure, so the clad bursts earlier on NUREG-0630's curve, and it poisons the gap, so the pellet runs hotter and lets go of more. Natural U metal changes alpha to beta at 942 K with a volume jump | **built 21/09/26.** Per node, `ST.csNFg` is Booth's reduced time ∫D/a² dt, advanced only while the pellet is over its fuel row's `tdmg`; Booth's sphere gives the released fraction (never over 1). The inventory is the core's burnup (`coreBurnupOf()`, `D` knob `burnup` ?? `burnupSuggest()` = half the fuel row's discharge `bu`) × 0.30 stable Xe+Kr per fission (0.85 Xe) × the node's rest flux. A rod at rest has already let go of what its residence time in the core (burnup × heavy metal / rated power: STOCK PWR 769 d, BN-600 1069 d) gives at its rest temperature, so the rest point is steady. A ring's released gas raises its rods' pressure over the fill (ideal gas over a free volume 6 % of the pellet), and sets the gap's mixture. Checked (`clad.js fg`): nothing 50 K under `tdmg`; Booth to 1e-9 at +300 and +400 K; at 1200 K clad on a 15.7 MPa core a ring with its gas out bursts (NUREG-0630 1030 K) and one without does not (1477 K); a fault that stands the release down turns both red | **1.** Not read at source: Turnbull's D = 7.6e-10·exp(−35000/T), the 5 µm grain, 0.30 atoms/fission, 0.85 Xe, the 6 % free volume, each row's `bu`, U's 1.06 %/0.70 % phase volume steps. **2.** Release is driven by the node's MEAN pellet temperature; the centre is hotter. **3.** No athermal release and no gas already out at rest: a rod starts with its fill alone. **4.** Metal rows release nothing (their gas story is swelling, not diffusion). **5.** The burnup sets the gas and nothing else — no reactivity, no decay heat (see `burnup`). **6.** BN-600 rests OVER `tdmg` and its pins are LWR pins (Zircaloy, 2.2 MPa He fill, 6 % free volume): with its gas out at rest they sit at 200 MPa and 1024 K clad, 6 K under NUREG-0630's 1030 K floor, so any heating bursts them. A real fast-reactor pin has steel clad, a low fill and a gas plenum near the fuel's own volume; this model has no steel clad or its burst law | **1** no, sources to read. **2, 3, 4, 5** YES. **6** MODEL: needs a steel-clad fast pin, not built | medium on the law, low on the figures in 1 |
| the feedwater heating | a steam plant bleeds part of its own steam off the turbine into six or seven feedwater heaters at falling extraction pressures. Each bleed has already done the work down to its own stage, none of it reaches the condenser, and the water arrives at the boiler near 490 K | ONE open heater at the shell's own pressure. `feedHeatKW()` puts `fed·(h(T_FEED) − h_in)` on the feed nozzle, priced off the flow-weighted enthalpy the transport lands there (`feedInHBy`); `feedBleedKgs()` takes `q/(h_g − h_in)` kg out of `s.turbWk` and out of what reaches the condenser (`riseCond()`). The bleed does **no shaft work at all** | measured on STOCK 09/09/26 the bleed is **33.0 % of the steam raised**, 196 of 594 kg/s, against a real PWR's 25 to 30 %. Charging one bleed the FULL turbine work made `COOLANT[].eff` absorb what a staged bleed would have earned: PWR .33 → .504, BWR .33 → .496, LWGR .31 → .466, SFR .40 → .633, MSR .44 → .697, HTGR .42 → .623, so **those are no longer isentropic efficiencies**. Before 09/09/26 the heating was free and funded 465 MW on STOCK, 39 % of core power, out of nothing | YES for the single stage — a staged cycle is a second network. The `eff` figures are **FIT** | high on the 25 to 30 %; the per-family split is not measured |
| ...and what that bleed did to the signal the rods steer on | the load signal a rod controller weighs against core power is on the SAME basis as it, so holding a steady load manufactures no mismatch and the temperature programme is met at rated | **fixed 12/09/26.** `turbShare()` divided `s.turbWk` — which is NET of the bleed, `netOut.turbWk − bleedAll` — by `P.steamRef`, which is the GROSS rated raise, and `buildRodAuto()` adds `TPROG_SPAN·max(0, n − tfrac)` straight into the error its PID nulls. So the bleed WAS the mismatch, and the rods held it as a standing temperature error for as long as the plant ran. `turbShare()` now adds the bleed back — the gross-against-gross basis the commissioning governor's own flow match already used | measured on STOCK PWR through `plantPreset(0)`, dice off, 600 s: raised 599.8 kg/s, bleed 210.8, `turbWk` 389.0, so `n − tfrac` **0.332**; `TPROG_SPAN` 18 × 0.332 less the 0.8 K `AUTOROD_DB` predicts **−5.18 K** against **−5.20 K** measured, flat, with TAVG DEV (4 K) lit and unclearable on a plant holding level 50.0 % and never tripping. After: **−0.01 K** flat from 150 s, `n` 0.944 → 1.000, **386.1 → 406.1 MWe**. EPR −0.06 K. BWR/4 is unmoved, and identical to HEAD — a boiling core steers on flow (`buildFlowAuto`) and never reads `tfrac` | DRIFT, now closed — nobody chose a net-over-gross ratio. **What is NOT closed:** the feedforward still enters the error the PID nulls with no washout on it, so a GENUINE standing mismatch would still park a standing offset. It reads ~0 on the three presets measured | high on the arithmetic and on the measurement; **medium** on the real machine, where the load signal is taken off turbine first-stage pressure and calibrated at rated rather than derived from a flow |

## Plumbing and flow — the solved network

This is the best-founded part of the model. It is a real momentum-and-mass solve, not a flow
scalar, and the rows below say so. **Do not read the sizing section under it as a verdict on this
one** — they are different code and different jobs.

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| what drives a flow | a pressure difference and the fluid's own inertia | `w = C·√(2·ρ_upwind·Δp)`, absolute pressure, relinearised each solve | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| a tank's gas charge | an accumulator's nitrogen expands fast on discharge, closer to polytropic (n ≈ 1.2–1.4) than isothermal; it cools and loses pressure quicker than p·V = const says | `p·V^n = const` with `TANK_NPOLY` 1.4 on all three gas-law sites (`eTankLvlA`, `eTankCapA`, `eTankPA`), fast-blowdown adiabatic nitrogen. **Closed 20/09/26 (`tests/physics/accum.js`), re-read on a fixed 10 s sim point 20/09/26:** STOCK PWR HPI accumulator through a cold-leg break reads **−2.4 % off the hand n=1.4 law in pressure** at the same swept volume and **−2.8 % in delivered mass** at the same end pressure (isothermal was +48.2 % / +60.6 %). Against n=1.2 it reads −19.9 % / −24.5 %, which is the published band, not a gap. The stroke is not one pour: the charge stalls around 3 s with 3424 kg still in the tank and pours again as the loop falls further, so the distance depends on WHERE it is read — the 2.0 % / 2.3 % first written on this row was read wherever the test's 8 s wall-clock guard cut that march, which is machine speed, not physics; the read point is a sim time now and two runs agree to 1e-9. **RE-READ 21/09/26 after the circulating-water circuit and the seed walk landed: −5.0 % in pressure and −5.5 % in delivered mass** (−4.2 % / −4.5 % on the committed tree, so this job moved it 0.8 pp across the check's own 5 % tolerance), and **the cause is now named.** A gas-charged tank keeps TWO tracks of the same cushion: its level is read off the node's PRESSURE by the polytropic law (`eTankLvlA`), while its mass is what transport carried, and nothing forces the two to agree. Measured mid-blowdown at t = 2.00 s: the level says the gas swept to **20.356 m³**, the delivered mass at 1000 kg/m³ says **20.067 m³** — 1.4 % apart in volume, which at n = 1.4 is the whole pressure distance, growing as the stroke runs. The same two-track shape already named for the circulating-water tank. It moves with any change to how fast the tank blows down, which is why this job's 0.8 pp is not a defect in either job | **MODEL, open on the two tracks**: the polytropic law itself is right, what is wrong is that a gas tank's pressure and its mass are computed independently. Not closed and not part of batch G; it has its own `docs/gap.md` line (MEDIUM / M / batch L) | high on the polytropic range; high on the measurement |
| ...and the inertia in it | the water in a pipe has mass, so a flow cannot be changed for nothing: `I·dw/dt = Δp − friction`, `I = L/A`. That is what makes a plant resist a slam and what gives water hammer its `ρ·c·v` | **in the tree and LIVE.** Every run edge carries `ed.I` = `max(L/2, NET_COMP_LEN)/areaOf(bore)` and every machine internal path that states a duct carries `partPathI()`. `edgeG()` returns `g/(1+g·In)`, `edgeH()` adds `In·w0`, and `edgeIn()` is gated on `netMarch`: `I·dw/dt` is a TIME derivative and is identically zero in a steady solve, so a settle, a reference solve and a governor walk carry none and only `step()` marches | **all nine presets fly at 5 s and eight of nine at 60 s with the term live**, against nine at HEAD without it: STOCK 373, NUSCALE 201, BWR/4 296, BN-600 380 (362 at HEAD), EPR 296, RBMK-1000 294, WINDSCALE 13, all resting at level 50. MSRE drains to level 8 and DUAL trips its turbine at 53 s. `probe.js ledger` closes to 0.81 kg of 337 500, `nodom-probe.js` read its full 1800 samples until 10/09/26, when the loop-head work took it to **735**: the LOAD FOLLOW scenario on the reference ship fills its generator shell to 100 % level by 50 s at HEAD too, and what changed is only what happens after the turbine trips on top of a full shell (the shell goes solid at 34–48 MPa and the vessel ruptures at 73.5 s, against a relief at HEAD). With the design state alone in it reads 1136; the overfill itself is the parked feed-regulator job, not this one, and every `splice.js` BARE baseline passes. One lump per run, no travelling wave, no pipe-wall compliance, so a surge reads about 20 % high | the two that are left are NAMED, not cut | high |
| how wide a pipe is | a designer sizes it for the flow it has to carry at a design velocity, and no two lines on a plant are the same size for the same reason | since 09/09/26 exactly that: `boreForW(w, rho, v)` on the run's OWN duty (`runDutyKgs()` = the smallest figure either of its two end nodes states) at one of three velocities - `COOLANT[].vLeg` on a primary leg, `V_LIQ` 3 m/s on any other liquid, `V_VAP` 50 m/s on vapour - doubled on a pump's suction face for NPSH. It was a six-row table keyed on the run's KIND | the figures move a long way and they move the plant: on STOCK circulating water 750 to 2940 mm, feedwater 1125 to 615, EFW discharge 1125 to 139, main steam 750 to 645, turbine exhaust 750 to **15 453** (425 m3 of holdup), HPI 187.5 to 107, surge 225 to 101. `ledgerKg` 294 725.9 to 337 375.4 on the wider circulating-water and exhaust bores. A run whose two ends state nothing still falls back to `BORE_REF` 750, and a relief line is the exception - it takes the valve's own bore | the LAW is real; `V_LIQ` and `V_VAP` are one figure each for the whole board, which is the cut | high on the law, medium on the two velocities |
| the circulating water pump | HEI *Standards for Steam Surface Condensers*: a condenser's tube water runs at 1.8-2.4 m/s at design and costs 34-69 kPa tube-side, tubes plus both waterboxes. A circulating-water pump for a short closed circuit develops 15-25 m total head, of which the condenser is the largest single item | **closed 21/09/26.** The cooling path is a duct now: `pathBoreMm()` cuts it from its own duty at the velocity its `ROLE` row states, and `pathK()` prices a path that states a `tube` bore from friction over every pass plus entry, exit and a waterbox turn per pass change. `ROLE.cond` states a 1 in OD 22 BWG two-pass bundle (24 mm bore), `ROLE.radiator` a 21 mm single-pass finned panel. `circHeadOf()` walks the circuit's machines the way `loopHeadOf()` walks the loop's. Measured on all nine presets (`tests/physics/pump.js`): tube velocity **2.076-2.088 m/s**, tube-side drop **58.3-58.7 kPa**, pump passes **1.037-1.042** of its rated flow, total developed head **15.03-15.20 m** | inside every published band. Was 1.19x the bought flow and 0.026 MPa bought against 0.048 delivered | **closed.** The 08/09/26-19/09/26 readings on this row were taken against a 750 mm duct at 41.6 m/s, and the earlier attempt that "trips RBMK-1000 and WINDSCALE and halves MSRE" failed on that area, not on the resistance. A THIRD fault was found in the same job and is what made the duct honest: an unauthored circuit's water was the PRIMARY's row at its own P0 and Tref, 705 kg/m³ at 583 K, so `boreForW()` cut the duct 19 % wide and the tube velocity could not be the one it was sized for. `circIsCw()` now names the circulating-water circuit structurally - no authored coolant, no core, a sink rejecting into it - and `circDesRho()`/`circMu()` read its own state (`cwRho()`, `CW_MU` 6.6e-4 Pa s at 312 K). Injection: halving the stated K drops the drop to 30.9 kPa and the check goes red | high |
| MSRE's heat balance hunts about unity | at steady state what the core makes leaves through the exchangers and the panels, and it stays there | **measured 21/09/26 over 600 s** after the radiator's cooling path got its real resistance: removal/heat reads 0.978 at 120 s, 1.022 at 200, 0.946 at 300, 1.033 at 400 and 1.008 at 600, with T-avg hunting 918.5-922.8 K. It closes ON AVERAGE and oscillates about +-3 % | the 2 % check fails wherever the hunt happens to be. Against the committed tree this is a large improvement, not a regression: there removal was **0** at 120 s and the cycle's thermal efficiency read 0.958 against a Carnot ceiling of 0.494, which is gone | **UNCLASSIFIED** - the balance is satisfied on average and nobody has diagnosed the oscillation. MSRE is also the preset carrying a 22.98 MPa feed pump and a steam cycle the real machine never had | high on the first law |
| what a pipe costs | friction plus form loss | the same, `fricOf` (Colebrook) plus per-run `K` | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| a pump's casing | a passage of a fixed area; the water's density belongs in the flow law, not in the passage | `pumpCasingC(h, q, rho)` = the resistance costing `PUMP_DROOP` of the stated head at the stated flow, taken ONCE at the circuit's design density when the edge is built | **it was read live off `F.rho`, the EOS density at (p,h), while `flowG()` prices the flow on `F.rhoD`, the donor's own holdup.** The two never cancelled: a suction that flashed priced the casing at vapour and then passed the law liquid, and the pump became a hole - measured 09/09/26, STOCK's feed pump at **23 202 kg/s against a 636 rating** with its suction at −0.15 MPa, and the node balance 23 168 kg/s out | closed 09/09/26 | high |
| a generator's feedwater nozzle | has a check valve, so a shell above its own feed header cannot blow down through it | `ed.diode = 1` on the shell's own feed path (`ROLE.sg.internal[1]`), the same door every standby train uses | **it had none.** Measured 09/09/26 the shell ran backwards through its feed nozzle at 2 867 kg/s at every valve position, and the water then arriving at the nozzle was the shell's own, so `feedInH()` read saturated and the bleed went to zero | closed 09/09/26 | high |
| where the feedwater heaters take their flow from | the heater train stands between the condenser and the feed pump, UPSTREAM of the regulating valve, so what passes it is the condensate flow | `feedHeatKW()` off `s.steamBy[id]`, which is that flow in a closed cycle | **it read `s.sgFedBy[id]`, the flow at the shell's own nozzle, downstream of the valve.** So a valve movement was the heaters' duty: the bleed collapsed whenever the feed dipped, the turbine took the whole raised steam, and the plant over-produced by exactly `bleedFrac` - measured on STOCK, 609 MWe against a 400 MW design, falling to 392 when the station was corrected. **11/09/26: capped at the nozzle.** The duty landed on the feed nozzle whatever arrived there, so a feed line with nothing coming took the whole heater train into its own few kilograms: measured on STOCK after a 1 MPa blast in the containment, the SG feed nozzle climbed to h 1.76e6 kJ/kg (319 000 K), the runs beside it to 86 000 K, and their skins held the room air at the 20 000 K cap. Now the duty stops where the nozzle's water would pass saturation at the bleed pressure (`satH(c, boilerP())`), off what arrived last pass (`feedInMBy`) plus what the node holds. Same case after: hottest fluid 719 K, room air 727 K. At rest it never binds: `P.netRef` 7406.6, `s.flowNet` 1.0178 at 60 s, unchanged. **CLASS: MODEL** | closed 09/09/26; cap 11/09/26 | high on the station; the open-heater model itself is the row above |
| a condenser's hotwell, as its pump sees it | the condensate nozzle is UNDER the pool, so what stands on it is the vapour space plus the water above it - the head every condensate pump in the world takes suction on | the column rides ONE EDGE, not a pin: `ed.poolAt` on the sink's own internal path, `poolH()` = the machine's drawn height weighed as liquid, at full commissioning fill and falling away with the pool as it drains (`poolLvlOf()`) | the depth is the machine's whole drawn height rather than the water's own depth at that level. Held at the baseline's magnitude on purpose: the level's depth alone is about half of it, and at half the column the reference ship's feed train cavitates. The elevation between machines is `staticH`'s and is separate | FIT on the depth | high on the mechanism |
| the density in that expression | whatever is at that spot | read at each node's own `(p, h)`, two-phase included | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| ...and the figure that curve falls back to | liquid sodium is 846 kg/m³ at 723 K, FLiBe 1963 at 922 K, helium 4.4 at 7 MPa and 773 K; **water is 755 kg/m³ at 550 K and 6.9 MPa** | 907, 2094 and 4.3 — **re-pinned 07/09/26.** They read 2127, 569 and 50: sodium 2.6× heavy, FLiBe 3.4× light, helium 11× heavy. Sodium was carrying FLiBe's figure, and helium's was entered in kg/m³ with `RHO_K` then applied to it a second time. **`LWGR` re-pinned 10/09/26, `dens` 55 → 108.** It read **385 kg/m³**, water at half its own weight, where `PWR` reads 700 at 583 K and `BWR` 665 at 559 K — the only light-water row out of family, and by a factor of two. Found by asking what `satRvl` was doing on the RBMK: `rvl` read **0.1454** at 7.17 MPa against a real 0.0494, three times high, and `rhofOf` 385 against 740 was the whole of it. Nothing else reads `cool:2` (`ARCHPRE`), so the re-pin moves `RBMK-1000` and no other preset | 7 % high on all three, which is the curve's shape and not the anchor — the same overshoot the water rows have carried all along (PWR reads 820 against 700). After the `LWGR` re-pin its curve reads **754.8 kg/m³ at 7 MPa against a real 739.7**, 2 % high, in family with the rest. What it did NOT close is the vapour half: `rhogOf` reads **54.31 at 7 MPa against a real 36.53**, 49 % high, so `rvl` is still 0.0719 against 0.0494 | — | high on the anchors; FLiBe's `tc`/`pc`/`rhoc` have never been measured and the row carries the usual Guggenheim-relation estimate, so its curve away from the anchor is low |
| ...and that shape | — | `rhofRaw()` extrapolates below `T0` with a fixed exponent `RHO_N` 0.35, and it overshoots: **every** row reads 7–17 % heavy at its own operating point | one shape for six fluids | DRIFT, and now the largest density error left. A per-family fudge would close it and has been refused elsewhere | high |
| a choke | a vapour chokes, a liquid does not | exactly that, off the higher pressure | the LAW is right | — | high |
| ...and the pressure it flashes from | a liquid flashes where its local pressure falls under saturation; water at the bottom of a pool stands at the vapour pressure plus its column, so a condensate pump under a hotwell draws subcooled water | since 18/09/26 `eFlowGA()` gives the flashing test the donor's pressure plus the edge's static head toward the flow, and the exit the far node's pressure. Before, it used the vapour-space pressure alone, so STOCK's hotwell nozzle (0.0066 MPa vapour, 0.034 MPa of water over the nozzle, 311 K water) was priced as saturated liquid and choked the feed at 566 kg/s against 649 of steam. **MODEL defect, fixed**; it moves the tick's flow law on every edge with a column across it | closed for a pool nozzle; a riser now flashes at its top exit's pressure, which is where a real one does | — | high |
| ...and which edges are allowed to reach it | every vapour path | `flowG()` gates on `ed.hSrc` — the head SOURCE alone, written beside `ed.h` in `netFinish()`. The static column stays in the total (which is what drives the flow) and out of the gate, and the gate reads the source's VALUE this tick, so a stopped pump is a length of pipe | re-measured 07/09/26 (fixed the same day; the gate read `!h`, and `h` carried the column, so an edge chokes only where its two ends are at exactly the same height). Stock plant at rest, 20 s: the turbine's swallow (`vap:turb`, dp 5.95 MPa against a 3.05 cap) and its exhaust (`exh:condt-turbb`, 0.82 against 0.37) now choke; the liquid feed leg (`feed:efwt-efwpr`, dp 1.31 against 0.675) still does not; a stopped standby pump reads `hSrc` 0.000000 against 1.46 running, so its edge is free to. **The commissioning governor fit absorbs the change**: `P.turbC` +39 to +49 % on every preset (stock 0.028912 → 0.041177), and the plants rest where they did — stock 60 s MWe 373.23 → 372.11, shell 6.9051 → 6.9071 MPa, `T-avg` 583.171 → 583.177 K, primary 15.5116 → 15.5120 MPa | no gap worth stating | high |
| helium's isentropic exponent | monatomic: c_p/c_v = 5/3 = 1.6667, flat over the plant range (NIST WebBook at 7 MPa, 300-1100 K: c_p 5.19, c_v 3.12 kJ/kg/K) | the HTGR row stated no `gam`, so every choked helium flow read `GAM_VAP` 1.3; now `gam:1.667` (`props.js`: row within 1 %, critical ratio 0.4871 against 0.4867; injected `GAM_VAP` reads 0.5457 and fails) | choked at 0.546 of upstream instead of 0.487, and a fully-choked opening passed ~8.8 % less mass than it should | **MODEL**, closed 20/09/26 | high |
| Flashing discharge, the omega method near the critical pressure | homogeneous equilibrium flow: isentropic flash on IF97 saturation data, maximum mass flux: 40 380 kg/s/m2 for x 0.05 at 15 MPa, 25 915 for x 0 and 17 458 for x 0.2 at 7 MPa | Leung's omega (`omegaA()`), since 19/09/26 on the liquid's real c_p at the stagnation state (8.52 kJ/kg/K at 15 MPa; it read the pinned 5.5 before) | **-11 %** at 15 MPa x 0.05 (35 958); +0.7 % and -1.6 % at 7 MPa (`choke.js`). Leung's own 1986 omega without and with its 1 - 2p v_fg/h_fg term gives 35 765 and 36 034: the method, not the transcription. The pinned 5.5 had put it at +2.4 % | NO: nobody chose omega's reach; it linearises v(p) and leaves HEM as the saturation line steepens toward the critical point | high (HEM from the published saturation table) |
| where the water is | it has a location | a mass per node, transported upwind on the solved edges | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| pump curve | quadratic with droop in the casing | the same, and the droop is a resistance in the solver's matrix | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| affinity laws | real | head goes as `N²`, obeyed | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| cavitation | NPSH, an absolute margin | absolute subcooling at each suction | right shape; `CAV_SPAN` 12 K is typed | YES | high |
| where the subcooling instrument reads | core-exit thermocouples against pressurizer pressure — two named tappings | the hottest LIQUID node on the circuit, skipping the hold tank's bubble and the line that is that vessel's own water (`holdLineSet()`) | it is a search rather than a tapping, so any node the plant makes hot and liquid can become the reading, and each new node has to be argued out of it by hand | DRIFT — a real instrument is a place, not a maximum | high |
| a check valve | one-way | a signed one-way edge in the matrix | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| the steel around the water | stores heat, and a lot of it | a mass and a time constant per node | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| how a run gets from one nozzle to the other | a pipe route answers to stress, supports, thermal-expansion loops, drainage slope, separation between redundant trains and room for a crew to walk | an A\* over grid cells (`runRoute()`, layout.js): one per cell, `turn` 4, `cross` 8, `standoff` 1.0 beside a machine wall, `hug` −0.5 beside an existing run. It may cross another run and may never share a lane with one; a route it cannot find is refused and the run reads NO ROUTE rather than rerouting | **none of the five real constraints is in it.** What comes out is a length and a bend count, and length is resistance and mass — so the arrangement feeds the physics through one number it was not chosen for | YES, and the cost model is a FEEL knob: the four figures were settled in a dry run by eye, not measured. Re-laid the whole stock ship through it 07/09/26 — 152 cells before and after, two runs took different lanes of equal length, `P.netRef` 7205.4 kg/s and `netFlowK` 0.9937 unmoved (re-pinned 09/09/26 on the bore law: `P.netRef` **7357.6** kg/s, `netFlowK` **1.0142**. It read 7338.1 / 0.9936 on the donor-density change alone, so honest bores moved the reference 0.3 % and the ratio 2.1 % — the drawn plant now passes slightly MORE than its own nominal reference), five of six presets bit-identical at 60 s (BN-600 shell 16.978 → 16.977 MPa) | high that the real constraints are absent |
| a pump coasting after a trip | the hydraulic torque goes with the flow, bearings and windage do not, so the shaft stops — a real RCP in minutes. **Sourced 20/09/26.** Published RCP coastdown is reported as flow against time, never as a decomposed friction term: half-flow lands around 5–15 s depending on the flywheel (Qinshan Phase II: over 10 s on loss of power to both pumps, OSTI 20289829), a Westinghouse 13 200 lb flywheel is quoted as extending the total coastdown by 22–30 s (NRC ML11223A213, snippet only — the PDF would not extract), and flywheel design studies take **2 minutes of coastdown as the upper acceptance ceiling**. **No source anywhere gives a bearing-and-windage time constant on its own**, because nobody publishes the decomposition | `dN/dt = −N²/(2·τ_c) − N/PUMP_FRIC_S`, `PUMP_FRIC_S` = 60 s | the friction constant is a plausible order, not a measured machine: it is the only free number here, and it sets how long the tail is, not whether there is one. Measured 07/09/26, stock plant, blackout with the backup supply bypassed, coolant pump speed: 10 s 0.545 → 0.479, 60 s 0.167 → 0.088, 300 s 0.0385 → 0.0011, 600 s **0.0196 → 0.00001**. The old law was exactly `1/(1+t/2τ_c)` (0.03845 measured against 0.03846 predicted at 300 s), so the shaft still turned at 2 % of rated ten minutes in and the plant read that as forced circulation for ever. At 300 s of blackout `flowK` is now 0.0528 against `nat` 0.0528 — the whole of the flow is the thermosiphon, where it used to be 0.0646 against 0.0478 | the term is right; the constant is a stated **FIT**: still a guess, but 20/09/26 the PAIR was cross-checked against the published curves. The two terms split at `N = 2·τ_c/PUMP_FRIC_S` = 0.2: above 20 % speed the N² hydraulic term drives the deceleration, below it the N/60 friction term does, so **60 s sets the low-speed tail only** — it is neither a standstill time nor the 1/e constant of the whole curve. Solving the law as written with `τ_c` 6 s and no demand gives half-flow at **9.3 s** (published 5–15 s) and 1 % of rated at **172 s, 2.9 min** (against the 2 min acceptance ceiling). Both land inside the published ranges, so the constant is not contradicted — but that is the pair agreeing, not `PUMP_FRIC_S` being measured, and the tail is the only place it bites | **medium** that the pair (`τ_c`, `PUMP_FRIC_S`) reproduces real coastdown timescales; **low, unmoved**, that 60 s is the right friction constant — no source isolates one |
| pressurizer heaters | a 4-loop PWR carries about 1.8 MW of heaters in a ~51 m³ vessel | **closed 20/09/26.** `E_PZR_KW_M3` 35 kW/m³, 1.75 MW on the stock 50 m³ vessel | ~3 % light; the setpoint arrives ~18 % sooner, steady state unmoved | — | medium |
| what a feed pump has to suck against | Thoma's cavitation number is PER STAGE: sigma = NPSHr / H_stage (Stepanoff; Karassik, *Pump Handbook*). A boiler feed pump stage develops 200-400 m at 5000-6000 rpm, so a real machine's NPSHr at rated duty is 3-10 m. ANSI/HI 9.6.1 and ISO 9906 define NPSHr as the suction at which total head has fallen 3 %: above it head is flat, below it falls away | **closed 21/09/26.** `pumpStages(id)` is the machine's head over `PUMP_STAGE_H` (400 m of water, the top of Karassik's band), rounded up, floored at 1 - an answer, not a knob - and `pumpNPSH()` divides Thoma's number by it. `PT.pumpNPSHr` is a build column; `eCavStep()` ramps `ST.cavP` on the shortfall of NPSH available (p - p_sat(T) at the machine's own suction) against it, and the old subcooling trigger with its `E_CAV_SPAN` 12 K is deleted. The BN-600 collapse this row was written on is CLOSED, and the tick was never the term: a header tee was a SEPARATOR. `gasAt` was set on both ends of every vapour-law run, so a tee handed its whole flow over at `satHg` at its own pressure, charged itself back `−m(hg−h)`, and kept the liquid it had "separated" in a node with no drain. Measured over the settle 09/09/26: `mstee0` seeded dry (41.8 kg, x 1.000) and was pulled monotonically to 2004 kJ/kg and 161.6 kg over 150 passes, never converging — hg RISES as pressure falls in this range, so the node handed out more enthalpy per kg than it was fed. The march then reversed the header at 0.30 s, priced a node whose booked mass and enthalpy disagreed at 76 MPa, and burst six pipe cells | dt was never it. dt 0.005 and dt 0.001 both still collapsed the shell; so did an inflow cap on the node's own pressure step (0.03, 0.1, 0.3) and a minimum run length (1, 2, 4 m). All three failed because all three treated a **conservation** defect as a stability one. The related chatter is closed separately: a run resting on its saturation line read 982 then 0.8 then 982 kg/m³ on alternate ticks off (p,h) while its mass never moved, and `F.rhoD` (a run's donor density is its own holdup) fixed that | — the ERROR is closed, **and 20/09/26 the accident it used to produce was re-run and is gone too**. The 10/09/26 reading — a BN-600 turbine trip with dry shells giving a 115 MPa turbine node, an 849 K condenser and both radiators wrecked on OVERPRESSURE — does not reproduce (`tools/sandbox/net.js` key `bn600DryTrip`: feed pump and turbine stop valve both shut through `act()`, dice on and off, to 90 s). Tripped at 10 s the header climbs 17.96 to 20.10 MPa and plateaus at 20.1-20.3; tripped at 20 s it climbs 13.02 to 14.14, plateaus, then drifts to 17.3 by 90 s as decay heat keeps boiling the isolated header. It never runs away, the condenser stays 270-330 K, and both radiator wreck flags stay 0. That is the physical answer: a tripped turbine sees at worst what is upstream of it, and the relief holds it there. It was downstream of this row's separator defect and of the feed pump's `pumpRhoK` rating leak, both closed | high; the settle drift and the burst timeline are both measured; high on the 20/09/26 re-run |
| what a steam nozzle draws | a drum, a shell and a hotwell have a free surface, so the nozzle over it draws vapour and the water it leaves stays behind and drains. A tee in a header has no surface and no drain: it passes what arrives, mixed, and a drum that runs out of steam passes water | since 09/09/26, two statements. `separates()` (`netEdges`) sets `ed.gasAt` only where the end is a part with a level — a shell, a core, a tank, a thermal sink — asked of the PART, never of the run's kind, so `edgeLaw()` no longer decides who separates. And `advectStep` caps a gas donor at the vapour it holds plus the vapour arriving that tick (`x·m/dt + vIn`); over that it carries at its own mean and the `−m(hg−h)` charge-back is taken on the vapour share only | a shell still hands over exactly dry saturated vapour with no carry-over and no dryer, and its steam is 1.5 % wet by the first tee (`mstee0` settles at 2815 kJ/kg, x 0.985, against hg 2827 at its own 16.75 MPa) because throttling saturated steam above ~3 MPa really does wet it. There is no moisture separator or reheater anywhere on the board, so what the turbine gets is what the header holds. **And since 10/09/26 the liquid half exists.** `ed.liqAt` is the mirror of `ed.gasAt`, set on the vessel end of a non-vapour run off a TANK with a level under full - a drum's downcomer, a pressurizer's surge line - and never on the core node (the riser is a mixture and must reach the drum as one), a shell pool or the hotwell (already priced at its own liquid). The donor hands over `satH` when `x > 0`, charges the surplus back the same way the gas half does, is capped at the liquid it holds plus the liquid arriving this tick, and hands over no hydrogen. `F.rhoL`/`net.liqNodes` mirror `F.rhoG`/`net.gasNodes`; `flowG` and `staticH` take the marker the way they take `gasAt`. Measured on RBMK-1000 at rest: `s.flowNet` 0.79 -> **0.93**, core-average void 0.413 -> **0.146**, exit quality 0.244 -> **0.099** against a real 0.145, and the core inlet 3.7 K subcooled -> **33.0 K** against a real 14.5. The books close (`probe.js ledger --pre=5`, residual 0.000 kg), and STOCK PWR and EPR are inside 0.2 % of HEAD at 60 s, so the surge line handing over `satH` is invisible at rest. **The 33 K is the honest consequence and is not tuned:** the real 14.5 K is cold feed mixed into the drum's water, and a one-node drum whose steam leaves at `hg` and whose water leaves at `satH` has nowhere to keep that mixing - the node reads two-phase, and a two-phase node's liquid IS saturated by definition | — | high on the mechanism, high on the settle measurement; the drum's inlet subcooling is now wrong in the other direction |

| the core inlet, off the field instead of off the mean | the water arrives at the channel at whatever the loop delivers, and on a direct cycle that is saturated drum water plus the pump's own head | **landed 10/09/26, in two halves.** `coreInH(s,id)` (`step.js`) is the one door: `advectStep()` refills `coreInHBy[nid]` off the same donor pass `feedInHBy` rides — the kg-weighted `inH/inM` of the edges arriving at the vessel node, blended onto the node's own water only below `CORE_DT_QMIN` of its commissioned through-flow, where a rise is no longer a rise. `coreStep()` takes `hIn` instead of `Tavg`, opens the channel at it and reads the transport lag's density there; `cs.coreDT` is still the RISE and the display, `sgInertiaK` and `K.sc0` still read it. `TavgOf()` keeps every other reader. **The second half is the term that half one exposed:** `cs.nTc` was algebraic in `heat` with no time constant at all, so with the channel no longer anchored on the slow `s.Tavg` the loop n -> heat -> nTc -> mod -> rho -> n closed inside one tick. The node coolant temperature now takes the SAME transport lag `cs.nV` already takes (`tau` = core height over the coolant's own velocity): a target `cs.nTct` written in the node loop, relaxed in the existing lag block, copied `nTct -> nTc` by the commissioning walk where it already copies `nVt -> nV` (`step.js`, `probe.js`). Centring the channel on it started every core ~5 K cold. 60 s, dice off, seed 1, `n` HEAD → landed: STOCK PWR 0.9411 → 0.9412, EPR (55 s) 0.8840 → 0.8819, WINDSCALE 0.9470 → 0.9475, BN-600 1.0130 → 1.0243. **NUSCALE 0.9305 → 0.4739, BWR/4 0.9131 → 0.6068, MSRE 0.8178 → 0.6101, DUAL (10 s) 1.2176 → 0.5672.** RBMK-1000 breaches at 27.1 s against 25.6 s, and carries the `LWGR` re-pin as well. **The earlier attempt's NUSCALE 0.93 → 0.47 IS reproduced** — an earlier draft of this row said it was not | **YES, and what the first attempt at it cost is stated below.** The four that collapsed were a DEFECT, and the mechanism first written on this row was not what the parts showed. Re-measured 10/09/26 against HEAD by rule 9 (dice off, seed 1, `s.parts`): the falls are carried by `mod`, the coolant-temperature part `aM·(nTc − K.Tref)`, not by `vd`. NUSCALE at 5 s: `mod` **−897 pcm** against HEAD's −123, `vd` only **−249** (not the −929 this row first stated); at 1 s `mod` −1024 and `n` 0.443 against HEAD 1.034. MSRE at 1 s: `mod` **−2212 pcm** where HEAD reads +6, and MSRE has no void to speak of, so "now boils" cannot be the mechanism. `mod` GROWS over the first second (−439 at tick 1 → −1024 at 1 s) while the inlet barely moves (NUSCALE subcooling 46.6 → 49.0 K), so it is a transient in `nTc`, not a fixed datum offset alone. `probe.js presets --pre=1` reads RED HI FLUX at 0 s on NUSCALE where HEAD reads no alarm at 8 s. **The cause was measured, and it was not a stale datum.** Commissioning balances: at tick 1 the parts sum to the same -3565 pcm on HEAD and on the tree, boron taking the difference (HEAD -51, tree +254). Halving `dt` did not shrink it - the same tick count either way - so the gain was per TICK, which is a zero-lag algebraic loop and not physics. **60 s, dice off, seed 1, unarmed, `n` HEAD -> landed with the lag in (and the `LWGR` re-pin in `design.js`):** STOCK PWR 0.9411 -> 0.9409 (-0.02 %), EPR 0.8942 -> 0.8956 (+0.16 %), WINDSCALE 0.9470 -> 0.9467 (-0.03 %), NUSCALE 0.9305 -> 0.9288 (-0.18 %), BN-600 1.0130 -> 1.0247 (**+1.15 %**, outside the 1 % gate and unmoved by the lag - **named 20/09/26, it is the FIELD INLET term**, see below), MSRE 0.8178 -> 0.7430 (**-9.1 %**, carried by `rod` -374 pcm, the plant's own temperature programme answering a 23 K hotter channel; `mod` IMPROVED by +176), BWR/4 0.9131 -> 0.6578 (**-28 %**, carried by `xe` -692 pcm over the minute with `mod` at -43; its rest core-average void reads **0.381** against HEAD's 0.131 and a real BWR's ~0.40, so the fall is a plant nobody is steering under a xenon build, not the channel), DUAL (10 s) 0.6191 -> 1.5746 (up, not a fall). RBMK-1000 breaches at 28.7 s against HEAD's 27.0 s. `probe.js presets` reads no RED at 0 s that HEAD does not: NUSCALE's HI FLUX is gone and RBMK-1000's LO SUBCOOL with it. On the two drum plants the same read shows the drum handing **8 % quality down the downcomer** (`drum0` x 0.108, `run:cold:drum0b-pump0t` x 0.079): `separates()` gives a vapour run its `gasAt` and there is no liquid counterpart, so a separator's WATER outlet takes the mixture  **BN-600's +1.15 %, named 20/09/26 (`tools/inletsplit.js`).** A controlled ablation from one commissioned start, 60 s, dice off, marching BN-600 with each term held off in turn: nothing changed 1.000155 (+0.0155 %); LAG OFF 1.000155, identical to the sixth decimal, confirming the row's "unmoved by the lag"; **FIELD INLET OFF 1.057523 (+5.75 %, 35 pcm at beta = 640)**, and the trajectory visibly destabilises — it undershoots to 0.928 at 20 s and is still climbing at 60 s. Both off: 1.036260. The preset's OWN drift is 0.0155 %, **74x smaller than the 1.15 %**, so "it is the preset's own residual" is ruled out by measurement; and the term that owns it moves `n` 350x the noise floor when held off. **The caveat, stated:** this is a marginal-term ablation from a COMMON commissioned start, not a re-commission under the old code, so it isolates the term's own sensitivity rather than reproducing the historical HEAD-to-landed number exactly. The term identification stands regardless of that | high on the anchor finding, on the part readings and on the divergence being numerical; **high, as of 20/09/26, that the BN-600 1.15 % is the field inlet term and not the preset's drift** |

Closed 19/09/26 besides the core's shape (next row): the tick's marched flow law priced friction on the quadratic secant `2ρC²/w` even under the linear floor (`DPFRAC`), where the edge law itself is linear, so every large pipe with a small drop moved on tick 1 (5 kg/s on STOCK's cold leg); `eEdgeGH()` now takes the smaller of the quadratic secant and the linear law's own conductance. Same builder, same role, same face, same tick law: one preset moves and three do not, which is a BUILD defect on BWR/4's drawing and not the tick law. **The `COND_P0`-floor reading cannot be re-measured, and the reason matters: the preset it was taken on no longer exists.** WINDSCALE was renamed CALDER HALL in `56bdde6`, which in the same commit gave it `feedT` 310.9 and `sgDesP` 1.448 — a physics change, not a label change. CALDER HALL's condenser now reads **145.118 MW at rest and 145.118 MW on tick 1** (1.7e-6 of duty, flat) at 0.005729 MPa, sitting 0.00173 MPa clear of the 0.004 MPa floor: no clamp interaction at all. The 36.7 → 42.2 MW step is gone with the plant that made it, so **why that margin existed is UNCLASSIFIED and now unanswerable** — attributing it to an oversized condenser or colder cooling water would be a guess about a plant that is not there. Every other `WINDSCALE` reading in this file was taken on that same superseded preset and has not been re-measured under the new one. **The circulating-water tank's motion is NOT a defect, diagnosed 21/09/26 (`tests/physics/cwtank.js`, STOCK / NUSCALE / EPR, 170 s at rest).** The 20/09/26 hypothesis — commissioning seeding that node off the loop's converged field — is **FALSIFIED by measurement**. Read before tick 1, the tank is at its own zero-flow equilibrium to **1e-11 of its pressure**: its node is a BOUNDARY (`eBound` true, `PT.tankStores` 1, `tankInField` 0), so the settle never had it to freeze — the pressure is imposed by the tank's own level through the polytropic law, and the 0.011 MPa standing between it and the tie node is exactly the static head between them. `eNetSteady()`/`eFieldTankP()` is not what is happening. **What the transient IS: the circulating-water loop warming to its operating temperature, and the surge tank taking the expansion — which is what a surge tank is for.** The loop is a fixed volume, so the mass it gives up is beta.dT of itself, and measured `(dm/m)/dT` is **4.44e-4 (STOCK), 4.13e-4 (NUSCALE), 4.30e-4 (EPR) 1/K against IAPWS-IF97's 4.49e-4** for liquid water at 322 K and 0.6 MPa, all inside 8 % — that is the coefficient of thermal expansion of water, not a number this simulation chose. The SIGN also flipped under the circulating-water pump row's fix: the level now RISES 60.0 → 60.64 % over 170 s where it fell to 59.47 % before, because that job corrected the loop's commissioned density and with it its commissioned temperature. The 47.83 kg is superseded (STOCK now takes 64.40 kg in). One residual remains and is the same one named 20/09/26: the tank is a BOOKED node, so `ST.mBy` is overwritten each tick from its own level instead of integrated from the edge, and the two tracks of the same expansion disagree by **1.5e-6 of the tank's mass** (STOCK; NUSCALE 6.7e-7, EPR 9.5e-7) — a per-node bookkeeping residual that costs the plant nothing, inventory holding to 3.7e-15. The stale Boyle-law form in the level check was also corrected to the polytropic exponent the code actually uses (n = 1.4), which it now matches to 1e-6. **No repair was made and none is owed**: the authored charge stays authored and the level is its consequence. The same form on the pump edges left BWR/4's feed train in a pure 2-cycle (1262 ↔ 1298 kg/s) at the shell search's off-design pressures; a full tangent wherever the last flow agrees with the field set up a 2-cycle in STOCK's circulating water loop. Pumps keep the plain secant | 18-30 passes a steady solve where a quadratic one would take ~5 | a solver row, not physics. **Chased 20/09/26 (`tools/solvecycle.js`): the cycle does not reproduce, and two of the three candidates are dead on inspection.** Forcing the half-secant back onto all six pump edges through the real `commission()` (32 246 logged passes), plus seven single-edge off-design perturbations and two coupled trials driving BWR/4's two recirculation loops off design in opposite directions, **converges every time at gain +0.50 to +0.60** — the same contraction the non-pump edges already get. Nothing sustains a gain near −1. **The droop and the best-efficiency-point candidates are ruled out by the code itself**: `ePumpHeadA()` prices head off N² and a cavitation ratio only, never off the edge's own `w` within a pass, and the ratio is frozen while `eNetHeldOn` — so there is no flow-dependent pump curve here at all, hence no shutoff-chord sign flip and no curvature crossing. **The third candidate is real but transient**: a boiling recirculation edge's upstream density `F.fRhoD[up]`, which enters `g = C·√(2ρ·dp)`, swings 3-11x between passes across a quality-regime transition (58.66 → 180.00, 278.88 → 160.57, 673.76 → 163.63 kg/m³) and throws the one-step gain as low as **−3.48** — but it damps back to +0.50 inside one or two passes. That is a kick, not a limit cycle. The 1262/1298 reading most likely predates the density and flashing-discharge fixes recorded elsewhere in this file; **that attribution is unconfirmed** | high on the measurements; **high that the cycle is absent today and that droop and BEP cannot cause it**; none on what made it self-sustain in 09/26 |
| graphite temperature | a pile's moderator is a large thermal mass: an RBMK-1000 stops ~5.5 % of its heat in 1700 t of graphite, which runs up to its 730 C allowed maximum and sheds the heat to the channels through rings and gas; it follows power over hours, and its coefficient is positive (INSAG-7 table II-I: +6e-5 per K) | **built 19/09/26; re-measured 20/09/26 on the redrawn cell** (row "Plant size"). The share is not stated: the blocks take what the row "heat deposited outside the fuel" gives them, live per node (it read 1.93 % on the 1037 MWt drawing and has not been re-measured since the redraw). The pressure-tube coolant row states `dTg` 444 K, the hottest block over its own water at rating (730 C over 286 C), and `eCoreGraphFit()` fits the stack's conductance to it; heat capacity `latModT()` x `graphCp(T)` (Butland & Maddison 1973: 0.713 kJ/kg/K at 300 K, 1.697 at 900 K). `csNTg` per mesh node, real time; the water gets the fuel's share plus what the blocks give up (`eCoreQWaterA()`). `derived()` states `aM` (water) and `aG` (blocks). RBMK-1000 (20/09/26, `rbmk.js graph`): **267.2 t = 345 kg/MW** (was 15.2 t = 14.2 kg/MW), graphite volume fraction **0.898**, the blocks' share of the moderating **0.957**, UA **110.1 kW/K**, **tau 4265 s = 1.18 h** (was 275.5 kW/K and 0.42 h), `aG` **+2.76 pcm/K** (was +1.05). The 2.4x on the conductance was a defect in `eCoreGraphFit()`, found and fixed 20/09/26: it priced the whole stack's UA off the PEAK node's flux and the peak node's own heat split, then spread that UA over the core by node weight, so the blocks shed 2.4x the heat the definition allows and sat only **183 K** over their water where the drawing states 444. The fit now sums the block's share over the core on its own flux shape, which is exactly the heat the tick delivers to the blocks, and the stack settles at the stated **444 K** over its water - 730 C over 286 C, the real machine's own figure. The stack's energy against what crossed it closes to 4.3e-9 of the core's heat, and its relaxation against the exact first-order lag on the drivers the blocks saw to 8.9e-7 | the mass is now right: 345 kg/MW against ~370 for the real ACTIVE core (0.90 x 765 m3 x 1700 kg/m3 over 3200 MWt; the 531 kg/MW often quoted is the WHOLE stack including the reflector), and the volume fraction 0.898 against 0.90. **20/09/26, second pass: the 2 h comparator was the WRONG OBJECT and the time constant now PASSES.** The drawing has no reflector - `REFL` carries a reflector's neutronics and none of its mass, which is a deliberate cut and not a gap - so the stack on the board is the ACTIVE core, and the published lag of the active core alone is 1184 t (370 kg per MWt of 3200) at ~1.75 kJ/kg/K shedding 5.5 % of 3200 MWt over 444 K, i.e. 396 kW/K: **1.45 h**. The 2.03 h everyone quotes is the whole 1700 t, whose reflector takes essentially no fission heat and so adds mass to the numerator and nothing to the denominator. Against 1.45 h the model's **1.18 h is -18.5 %**, inside 20 %, and `rbmk.js graph` passes. The residual decomposes exactly into the two numbers beside it: mass 345 against 370 kg/MW is -6.8 %, UA 0.142 against 0.124 kW/K per MWt is +14.5 %, and 0.9324/1.1452 x 1.45 h = 1.18 h. What is still out: UA is **0.142 kW/K per MWt against the real machine's 0.124** (176 MW over ~444 K on 3200 MWt), +14 %, which is the model's block share of 6.3 % against the real 5.5 % - the gamma-transport row, not this one; and the mass is the ACTIVE core's 345 against the active core's own 370 kg/MW. **`aG` +2.76 pcm/K against INSAG's +6.00**, 0.46x, and it did not move at all when the conductance was fixed - it is the lattice's own moderation curve and nothing about the stack's heat reaches it | **MODEL on the conductance, and the FIT class on it is retired**: UA is now the definition (block heat at rating over the block-to-water rise the drawing states) and nothing is solved to land a number. What is left of it belongs to the gamma-transport row and is stated, not chased - driving UA to 0.124 with the share still at 6.28 % would put the stack at the wrong temperature from two wrong inputs. The coefficient is `MODER.aT` x the lattice's shares and is **not** re-fitted: raising `aT` to land the number on one lattice would travel to MSRE and CALDER HALL. The **BUILD** class on the time constant is retired - the drawing is the real machine's cell now. MSRE and CALDER HALL carry graphite but their coolant rows state no `dTg`, so their blocks have no temperature: their share reaches the coolant at once instead of the pin | high on the share, the limit and cp; medium on INSAG-7's coefficient |

## Pump sizing — the guess, the reference and the core's own drop

`loopHeadOf()` is the **design-time** guess at how big a pump to draw on the board. It never runs
in the tick. From 10/09/26 it and the reference solve (`eNetRef()`, `PT.nodeRefH`) read ONE design state, `loopDesignH()`: the runs
that still carry what the core sent out are priced as the mixture, and the reference solve is seeded
at the same enthalpies instead of being isothermal. `runHotSide()` answers structurally — a walk
from the core's outlet face to the first vessel that separates, so a drum counts like a generator and
a direct cycle is no longer priced as cold water. The core states its own drop at its inlet face
(`COOLANT[].dpCore`, read through `runK0()`), and `ROLE.core` no longer carries a flat `kEnd:2`.

Nine presets, `probe.js loophead`, dice off, seed 1, at 5 s. HEAD is the tree immediately before this
change (it already carried another session's uncommitted work).

| preset | `P.netRef` kg/s | `flowK` | `flowNet` at 5 s | primary pump bought MPa |

| STOCK PWR | 7476.6 → **7406.6** | 1.0306 → **1.0210** | 0.9947 → **1.0035** | 1.0937 → **1.1203** |
| NUSCALE | 4313.4 → **4294.4** | 1.0263 → **1.0218** | 0.9933 → **0.9984** | 0.7546 → **0.7813** |
| BWR/4 | 7775.2 → **6351.1** | 1.2144 → **0.9920** | 0.8227 → **0.5803** | 1.3759 / 1.1449 → **1.1664 / 0.9353** |
| BN-600 | 4669.0 → **4675.7** | 1.0075 → **1.0089** | 1.0003 → **0.9976** | 0.2409 / 0.2079 / 0.2267 → **0.6200 / 0.5871 / 0.6059** |

No preset states `D.pumpHead`, so bought = guess on every row above.

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| a BWR gets the coolant flow it was designed for | jet pumps deliver design flow within a few % | **BWR/4 rests at 0.6406** (60 s, dice off, 10/09/26). The reference is no longer isothermal, which is what the old 18 % shortfall was: with the design state alone in, BWR/4 rested at **0.9565** against a `P.netRef` of 6398.1, and the gap was closed. Stating the core's own drop then re-opened it — see the row below | **36 % under `P.netRef`, on this preset only**; 0.7984 at HEAD and 0.9565 with the design state alone | **BUILD, on BWR/4.** The 09/09/26 “not an error” reading is superseded, not because it was wrong about the isothermal reference but because that reference is gone. What is left is the DRAWING: the `kEnd:2` the core used to charge at its OUTLET face sat in a 297 kg/m³ mixture and was worth **0.2097 MPa** on `hot:corer-tee0l` alone, while the stated 0.15 MPa at the dense inlet is worth **0.0001 MPa** more than the `kEnd:2` it replaced there (cold leg 0.2652 → 0.2653 MPa). Losing it takes both pumps down 15 % and the loop settles in its starved equilibrium: riser quality 0.42 against a design 0.11, and the cold leg voiding at x 0.29 | high on the physics; the reading is ours |
| what the loop costs at rated flow | a Westinghouse RCP develops 0.6–0.7 MPa; a BN-600 primary pump 0.7–0.9; an RBMK-1000 MCP 1.5–2.0; the MSRE fuel pump 0.33 | **the guess and the solved loop agree.** Scaling each pump's solved rest dp to its own rated flow by (rated/q)², 10/09/26 at 5 s: STOCK 1.1233 against a guess of 1.1203 (0.3 %), NUSCALE 2.3 %, BN-600 3.5–3.9 %, EPR 3.4 %, WINDSCALE 5.3 %. MSRE is 25 % (0.2325 against 0.1861) and was 23 % before this work. Two presets cannot be read this way at all — BWR/4 and RBMK-1000 rest far off rated flow and their loop resistance moves with the void, so (rated/q)² is not their system curve | **the “a third light” figure is WITHDRAWN.** 1.4849 against 1.97 was never reproduced: measured on all nine presets the solved rest dp is the guess plus 5–10 % on every single-phase loop, which is the pump sitting a little up its own droop. **20/09/26 both open items are answered (`tools/loopsplit.js`).** MSRE's 25 %, leg by leg at q = 2623 kg/s against a rated 2600, guess vs solved in MPa: `cold:coreb-pump0b` 0.0735 / 0.1133; `hot:corer-tee0l` 0.0351 / 0.0786; `hot:sg0l-tee0r` 0.0523 / 0.1153; `part:sg0` 0.0249 / −0.0035; `cold:pump0t-sg0b` 0.0022 / −0.0652; total 0.1879 / 0.2386. **No single leg is wrong — every leg is off and most of it cancels.** The cause is a MISSING TERM, not a mispriced K: `loopHeadOf()`'s `dpOf()` is friction only, `K·w²/2ρA²`, with **no elevation term at all**, while the solve carries one per edge (`eStaticHA()`). MSRE's loop has real height — `sg0` at grid y = 7, `pump0` at y = 20 — and molten salt is dense (2085–2108 kg/m³), so each leg carries 0.04–0.07 MPa of hydrostatic head. Because the hot and cold legs differ in density the round-loop total does not cancel: it nets **+0.051 MPa, which is the whole residual**. **The two boiling presets now have their first readings**, taken by driving the pump until the solved flow equals the rated flow and reading the loop dp there, never by scaling: **RBMK-1000 converges cleanly** (demand 0.986, q 2010 against a rated 2006) and reads **2.352 MPa** against a real MCP's 1.5–2.0, consistent with the drawn risers adding their own ~1.2 MPa. BWR/4 could not be driven to rated flow at all: the flow pinned at 1162–1168 kg/s, 57 % of its rated 2025, up to five times the demand, and the first number was taken at that ceiling, 1.517 MPa. **20/09/26 the ceiling is GONE and the cause is named**: it was the riser sized at liquid density (row "...once it IS drawn as a direct cycle"), which choked the loop. Re-measured on the same sweep: at its own demand the plant carries **4456 kg/s, 1.10 of a rated 4050**, and more pump speed keeps buying flow — 1.5x demand 6593 kg/s, 2x 8080, 3x 9459, 5x **11 718 kg/s = 2.89 of rated**, pump dp 0.53 to 4.31 MPa. There is no ceiling. The RBMK reading of 2.352 MPa above was taken with the same undersized risers and is **stale**; the MCPs now buy 1.236 / 1.253 MPa | closed for eight of nine. **MSRE's residual is EXPLAINED**: the design guess is missing the elevation term the solve has, and the fix is to add a hydrostatic term to `dpOf()`. **BWR/4's flow ceiling is CLOSED and it was MODEL**: `runBoreSuggest()` handed the boiling riser the circuit's liquid density, so the recirculation loop was drawn about four times too small in area. Neither of the two guesses on the table (undersized drawing, overstated two-phase multiplier) was right | high on the MSRE leg table and on the BWR/4 sweep; **the RBMK 2.352 MPa figure above is stale** and the MCP row carries the current one |
| the core states its drop | a real machine spends real pressure between its own nozzles: a Westinghouse 4-loop vessel about 0.30 MPa at rated flow; a BWR/4 bundle with its inlet orifice about 0.15; an RBMK-1000's ZRK throttle, lower water line, channel and steam-water line most of a 1.5–2.0 MPa pump head; a BN-600's wire-wrapped assemblies most of 0.7–0.9; the MSRE core a small part of 0.33; a pebble or prismatic core on a blower of about 0.1 | **`COOLANT[].dpCore` from 10/09/26**, one figure per coolant, MPa at rated flow, placed entirely at the core's INLET face and turned into a `K` on the run that lands there (`coreEndK()` inside `runK0()`), so the design guess, the reference solve and the tick all read it through the one door. PWR **0.30**, BWR **0.15**, LWGR **1.00**, SFR **0.50**, MSR **0.04**, HTGR **0.06**; `coreDpOf()` = `coreD().dp0 ?? coreDpSuggest()`, one inspector row, nothing baked. It REPLACES `ROLE.core`'s old flat `kEnd:2`, which is why two pumps get smaller | it moves what the pumps are bought for towards the real machines on three presets and away on two. BN-600 0.2409 → **0.6200** MPa against a real 0.7–0.9; WINDSCALE 0.0748 → **0.1005** against ~0.1; RBMK-1000 0.7495 → **2.3463** against a real 1.5–2.0, now ABOVE it because the drawn risers carry 1.20 MPa of their own at the design state; STOCK PWR 1.0937 → **1.1203** against a real 0.6–0.7, still nearly twice it. Against those: MSRE 0.2234 → **0.1861** against a real 0.33, because 0.04 MPa is less than the `kEnd:2` it replaced, and BWR/4 as in the row above | the six figures are the decision; the two that move the wrong way are named, not tuned | PWR medium-high, BWR medium, LWGR and SFR low-medium, MSR and HTGR low |
| a boiling loop's cold leg | comes back saturated | priced on the saturation line since 06/09/26 | closed | — | high |
| two-phase friction multiplier | a real and large term | tried, **measured, and it made BWR/4 worse** | not the missing term | — | high |

## Mass, water and books

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| water is conserved | exactly | the books close to 0.0 kg at rest and through a safety lift; no residual over 0.5 kg on any book over 600 s. The `advect` floor books at most 0.03 kg over 60 s at rest, and 1e-11 kg through a 15 s pipe burst on any preset now the donor limiter is swept to a fixed point (`COURANT_PASSES`) instead of priced once off the raw solve — one pass booked 285 kg on a BWR/4 burst, 1.6e6 kg on the `netTrivial` rig | none | — | high |
| how much water the plant admits to holding | every kilogram in it | every kilogram in it: the run holds its own water, so none of it lands on a node whose book would hide it. Stock 316 242 kg | none | — | high |
| ...on the first tick | mass is conserved or it is not | **the ~2 kg does not exist on the current tree, chased 20/09/26** (`tools/probe.js ledger`, extended to book tick 1 at full precision on all nine presets, dice on and off). `SC_MASSRES` on tick 1: STOCK −5.82e-11 kg on 371 889 kg held, NUSCALE 0, BWR/4 −1.16e-10, BN-600 0, EPR 0, RBMK-1000 +5.82e-11, MSRE +1.75e-10, CALDER HALL −2.91e-11, DUAL +2.33e-10 on 1 800 117 kg. That is `inventory × 2^-52` — double-precision roundoff, nothing else. `eLedgerOut()` is **exactly 0.0** on tick 1 on every preset and not one `E_BK_*` code fired, so there is no clamp, no overdrawn donor, no double-counted `eMassSeed()` stub and no book credited before a limiter. An independent residual computed outside `eLedgerA()` reproduces `SC_MASSRES` bit for bit, so the number is not an artefact of its own arithmetic | 1e-16 of inventory, which is the floor | **ERROR CLOSED.** Mass closes on tick one to machine precision on every preset. What removed the 2 kg was NOT identified; the 19/09/26 `eEdgeGH()` fix on the row above is the likely candidate and was never cross-referenced back here, and that attribution is unconfirmed | high on the measurement; none on the attribution |
| a machine's duty on a tick nothing flowed | a machine's heat is a fact about the machine, not about that instant's flow | `advectStep()` takes `src[]` on a node with no inflow as `q·dt/mass`, since 09/09/26; a settle pass still takes none, because it is a steady-state sweep and not a march | **it took none at all.** The control volume was written as a relaxation toward `(inH + src)/inM`, so the whole term was skipped when `inM` was 0. A steam path at the condenser's vacuum holds a few kilograms and the Courant limiter meters it through in PULSES - every other tick - so the condenser was getting half its cooling and ran 15 K hot into a turbine trip at 3 s. **Amended 10/09/26:** those pulses were the TURBINE'S, and the exhaust hood is where they were declared spent — see the row below | closed 09/09/26 | high |
| a light vapour node in an explicit transport | an exhaust hood holds its own steam and its pressure follows the condenser smoothly | it does, since 10/09/26. `turbb` (11.025 m³) holds 0.257–0.259 kg over ticks 1000–1500 and never reaches zero; the duct behind it holds 10.22–10.29 kg instead of sloshing 4.31–16.60 kg on alternate ticks; both solve at 0.00428 MPa or above, off the `COND_P0` floor. STOCK MWe sd over 20–30 s 7.78 → 6.64, max step 0.253 → 0.098 MW a tick, books 0.0 kg residual on every book over 600 s | the old row named the cause as a one-tick-lagged field against an explicit mass integral. **That was wrong** — the solve's own store balance `C·(p − p0)` reproduces the duct's 12 kg swing tick for tick. Two terms were priced wrong and each owned one symptom. (1) `advectStep()`'s `kIn` capped a vessel at its EOS mass `V·ρ(pMax,h)`; at the condenser's vacuum 0.8 kJ/kg of pool enthalpy moves `condt` from 86 t to 16 t against 41 t held, so on alternate ticks the limiter refused every arrival (250 of 500 ticks) and the hood's 590 kg/s piled into the duct for one tick. A node with `F.void` now states its capacity liquid-full, the read `poolLvlOf()` already uses. (2) `staticH()` weighed a column at the MEAN of its two ends' mixture densities, so 1.4 m of steam between `condt` and the exhaust run priced at 0.0013–0.0068 MPa — more than the whole vacuum span — and drove the edge below the floor, where `netPStar()`'s clamp made the hood's store a permanent source and `kOut` clipped its integral to zero. A `gasAt` end now weighs at `F.rhoG`; the gas test is `gasEnd()`, the one predicate `flowG()` also calls | closed 10/09/26 | high; each term isolated, each removal removed exactly its own symptom, reproduced per tick, books closed |
| what the presets lost when the hotwell joined the field | — | measured 09/09/26, dice off, seed 1, against HEAD. 5 s / 60 s MWe: STOCK 386/372 (was 384/373), NUSCALE 207/191 (213/201), BWR/4 359/303 (295/296), EPR 423/287 (328/296), MSRE 318/321 (336/414), WINDSCALE 14/13 (15/13), DUAL 684/534 (452/425) | **BN-600 is closed** — see the separator row below. Re-measured 09/09/26 with the separator on vessels only, dice off, seed 1, 5 s / 60 s MWe against the same tree with a separator on every steam node: STOCK 386/357 (390/371), NUSCALE 216/193 (206/0), BWR/4 296/299 (380/315), **BN-600 429/371 (0/0)**, EPR 387/309 (313/310), RBMK-1000 337/0 (355/0), **MSRE 320/266 (115/2443, SG BURST 8 s)**, WINDSCALE 15/14 (12/15), DUAL 604/426 (609/432). A 60 s figure printed against a `died` note is the reading AT the trip, and `s.turbTrip` is condenser back-pressure, which resets. **RBMK-1000 still loses its generators** (shell 0.29 MPa against 4.07, level 0 by 38 s) and nobody has looked; the separator law did not move it. BWR/4 is the one preset that lost, 380 to 296 at 5 s. **CLASS: one of the nine is classified.** BN-600's collapse was a MODEL defect — `gasAt` on every vapour-run end, in a shared builder, moving five presets at once. Every other reading in this row is **UNCLASSIFIED**: nobody has said whether RBMK-1000's shell going to 0.29 MPa, BWR/4's 380 to 296, or any preset's shortfall against its own rating is the physics being wrong or the drawing being wrong, and the two are not the same finding. **Re-measured 10/09/26** after the commissioning rating gate (the row on what a pump is rated against, below), dice off, seed 1, with the probe now stopping on the first red `ANN` row: **BN-600 370/373, alive at 60 s** and MODEL, against 429/371 that ended SG DRY at 8 s. It binds only where the steel outweighs the fluid by orders, which on this plant is short stagnant lines | YES | high |
| **a pump running on what is in its casing** | `dp = rho·g·H`, so a pump on vapour makes about a thousandth of its rated pressure rise | `pumpRhoK()` — suction density over the density that pump was commissioned at, straight onto the head, no threshold | none in the law; the reference is the commissioned point rather than a stated design density, so a plant reads exactly 1.000 at its own design point by construction | YES | high |
| ...and what it is rated against while the plant is being commissioned | a pump is bought for a stated fluid, and the commissioning test states the density it was run at; the test does not rate the pump on the answer of the previous test | `pumpRhoK()` returns 1 for every design-time solve (`netStoreHeld`) — the settle and the reference solve ARE the rating point. `P.pumpRho0` is still taken once, after the last settle (`step.js`), and the tick reads it unchanged | none now. Until 10/09/26 each settle was rated on the previous settle's answer. BN-600 runs four settles; its feed suction seeds at the shell's own saturation (h 1940 kJ/kg, 45 % steam at the 1.2 MPa suction), so a pump rated at 993 kg/m³ ran on 14 kg/m³ and kept 1/70 of its head. Settle runs 2 to 4 delivered 0 kg/s, the tick started with the shells dry, and the plant made 0 MWe by 36 s. With the gate BN-600 holds 373 MWe, 16.52 MPa and level 50 at 60 s, and STOCK is unmoved at 384 | closed 10/09/26; **class MODEL** — shared commissioning code, one figure, wrong scope | high; measured pass by pass in the settle and reproduced by injection |
| a steam generator's shell | a real vessel, water and steam | **ONE two-phase node**, the machine's own steam face (`shellNode()`), the same law a pressurizer already had: water and steam are one holdup, the level is that node's void fraction (`holdLvlOf` × `SG_DOME`), the pressure is solved, and the feed valve, the steam nozzle, the tube leak and the burst opening are all real edges on it. Re-measured 09/09/26: every preset commissions at its design pressure and holds level 50.0, and the ledger closes with no book over 0.5 kg | no gap worth stating | — | high |
| ...how much water is in it | a Westinghouse U-tube generator holds roughly 50-55 t of secondary water at power, which is the whole of the boil-dry margin | `SGT[].water` is **74.3 m³** at 100 % level, weighed at the shell's own design state by `sgMassOf()`: on the stock plant 6.9046 MPa, 739.9 kg/m³, **54 982 kg**. The number was authored as 55 TONNES and read as 55 m³ by `partVol()` and `sgShellT()` at the same time; 09/09/26 the unit was settled as m³ and the value restated so the mass stays on the real machine. The book and the field agree to 1 kg (27 451 against 27 448 at 49.93 % level), where they were 27 443 against 20 306 | none worth stating on the inventory | — | medium on the real tonnage |

Until 09/09/26 one figure was split evenly over four faces, so the PRIMARY tube side was handed 27.5 m³ of the shell's own water and the pool node had 0.001 m³ while carrying 27 t | — | medium on the tube figure, **low on the once-through's 45 m³**, which is reasoned from bundle length and not checked |

There is no second figure and no private book | none: the steam space IS the steam node, and the DRIFT that said otherwise is closed | — | medium on `SG_DOME` itself, which is a level-span choice and not a measured drum |
| **a turbine with water in it** | a steam turbine passing liquid water does pass flow, and destroys its own blading doing it - bent buckets, a wrecked machine, in seconds | **closed 20/09/26.** `eTurbStep()` reads each turbine's inlet node off `PT.turbEdge` (donor end by the solved flow's sign) and accumulates `ST.turbWet` while the quality is at or under `E_TURB_WET_X`; at `E_TURB_WET_S` the machine is wrecked (`E_WHY_WATER`, rides `E_TXT_WHY`) with a `TURB_WATER` event naming it (built at drain time, never in the tick). `knobs.js water`, fault injected: a turbine fed liquid is wrecked inside 3 s, the same turbine on dry steam over the same march is not | water induction itself is modelled. | a condenser | a real heat exchanger with its own hydraulics | **a vessel in the field**, since 09/09/26. The exhaust space and the pool under it are ONE node holding the ship's stated condensate (`condVesNode()`, `condPoolVol()`); the condensate nozzle in its floor is the other. The pressure is solved, the water is `s.mBy`, the level is a read (`poolLvlOf()`), a wreck and the operator's drain are one break edge, and `s.tank.hotwell` is gone. `condRejOf()` is a source on the node and `s.condTBy` reads back off it | the backpressure now moves with duty, load and circulating water instead of following `condT` alone. STOCK PWR 386 MWe at 5 s and 372 at 60 s against 384/373 at HEAD, level 50, books closed | closed 09/09/26 | high |
| ...and where its vacuum comes to rest | a sink's pressure is the saturation of the water in it, and its temperature is set by the circulating water it rejects to | the pool's temperature is its own energy. `condPoolT()` (`step.js`) bisects the saturation curve for the T whose enthalpy — saturated liquid plus the vapour fraction the VOLUME forces at that T — is the node's `h`; `condTRead()` is that on a `condVacuum()` sink and `netTempAt()` on one spliced into a primary leg. `condSatP` stays `psat` of the pool's T and the `COND_P0` floor is kept. A node whose mass cannot be liquid at any saturation the volume allows falls back to `netTempAt()`, so a drained hotwell reads as before | measured 10/09/26, STOCK, dice off, seed 1, with that day's uncommitted `gasEnd`/`staticH`/advect-void-cap work in the tree. **The pair now agrees**: `\|Tsat(p) − condT\|` ≤ 0.16 K and `\|h − hOfT(condT)\|` 0.09 kJ/kg at 5, 10, 30 and 60 s, against the 28 K above. STOCK rests at **305.87 K / 0.00492 MPa** at 60 s and moves with load (307.59 / 307.92 / 306.36 / 305.87 K at 5 / 10 / 30 / 60 s) instead of pinning at 302.11 K. `cwIn` 293.98 K, so the approach is **11.89 K**, against the plan's derived `cwIn + 13`. `F.x` at the node 1.8e-4 (was 0.028), `F.rho` 163.6 kg/m³ (was 0.9). Books close (`none`). Tick cost is inside the noise: 1.640 / 1.815 / 1.809 ms before, 1.844 / 1.573 / 1.535 ms after. MWe at 60 s, before → after: STOCK 395.4 → 386.3, NUSCALE 211.5 → 207.9, BWR/4 313.7 → 307.6, BN-600 378.3 → 371.7, EPR (30 s) 315.4 → 310.5, RBMK-1000 311.7 → 306.0, MSRE 273.3 → 296.3, WINDSCALE 13.8 → 13.6, DUAL (10 s) 0.0 → 0.0. No preset trips inside the window that did not trip at HEAD. **CLASS: the MODEL defect is closed on all nine.** Eight rest off the floor; DUAL is the ninth and its sink never holds a pool (hotwell 0.147 % at 10 s, `turbTrip` and `condLost` at 0.00 s, SG burst at 0.04 s, identical before and after), so it takes the fallback by design — DUAL's failure is a **BUILD** defect on that preset and is untouched here. The fallback was seen to fire: emptying the node by hand makes `condTRead` equal `netTempAt` exactly, with no NaN. Whether a real sink SHOULD rest at 4 kPa is still a separate question nobody has answered here | **closed**, with one gap: nothing beyond 60 s was measured, because 600 sim-seconds is ~50 s of wall against the project's 10 s node budget | high on the readings; none on the 600 s rest |
| ...and a condenser's compliance at the vacuum | a hotwell's pressure is its own saturation, and it is very stiff: condensing a little steam hardly warms 40 t of water | `condStoreC()` states it - the steam space over the pool plus the pool and its steel following saturation - the same shape the SG shell used before its own pressure got high enough not to need one | **it cannot be a plain (p,h) store.** At 0.01 MPa the whole span from a full hotwell to an empty one is 3e-5 MPa of saturation shelf and 0.17 kJ/kg of enthalpy, so the void fraction is a knife edge, the level has to be read off the MASS, and the donor density has to be stated: `F.rhoD` at a pool node is liquid, because the drain is under the water, and `F.void` says a nozzle in the space above draws steam | YES - the vacuum is the reason, and it is written down | high |
| ...and a condenser spliced into a primary leg | a vessel in a line either passes what is in the line or fails as a pressure boundary | **it passes and the books close**: 6167 kg/s forward and 5942 reverse of a 7301 kg/s leg, no burst, **0.0 kg** out (09/09/26, `tools/splice.js cond`). Its node reads 16.54 MPa, 576.9 K, x 0, 3334 kg — an ordinary vessel on the leg. At HEAD it was 2271/6593 kg/s at 515/546 K with 159/169 kg out. Three things were wrong and all three are the same rule: `condVacuum()` now names the set for the pool seed (`poolSet()`), the holdup (`nodeVol()` hands `condPoolVol()` to sinks at the vacuum only, so splicing one in no longer halves the stock hotwell) and the plant's readings (`condSinks()` is what `condPRead()`, `condTMean()` and `cwInMean()` average) | the leg runs **4.5 K hotter** than stock, not colder — 587.2 against 582.7. The machine is holdup and resistance in the line, flow falls 16 %, and the core's rise over it grows; `condRejOf()` takes about 2 K back out (576.9 K in, 574.5 K out), because the circulating-water side of a sink standing in a hot leg is stagnant | closed 09/09/26. The old row's 515 K forward was not the machine cooling anything: it was 45 m³ of hotwell seeded as a vacuum void, collapsing the loop to 0.3 MPa on tick 1 | high |
| the circulating water outside it | a closed cooling circuit has an expansion vessel: water that warms has to go somewhere, and the vessel sets the circuit's pressure | **a real circuit, real pump and a gas-charged surge tank**, since 09/09/26: `cwtank` (`buildStockPlumbing()`, 10 m³, 60 % full, `gas:{p0:0.6}`) on the CONDENSER's circulating-water outlet nozzle. Rigid, the nodes went over-full as the water warmed, `netPStar()` priced that at tens of MPa and the store row demanded thousands of kg/s the transport refused: at 30 s the solve read 17 346 kg/s into a run and 14 849 out of it. With the tank, STOCK reads 18 149 / 18 150 / 18 149 / −18 150 kg/s round the loop against a transport of 18 153 / 18 153 / 18 150 / −18 153, and the 3.6 kg/s difference is the tank's own branch. The tank hangs on the condenser and not on the pump's suction face because a tank a suction walk reaches is a RESERVE (`pumpResOf()`) and commissions that pump stopped | closed 09/09/26 | high |
| ...and what it cost | — | the pump now delivers its curve instead of fighting a spurious store: 18 150 kg/s against 14 940, +21 % | **that is the ERROR row above biting harder.** MWe at 60 s, before → after: STOCK 372.1 → 372.6, NUSCALE 191.4 → 174.7, BWR/4 302.9 → 299.0, BN-600 3.0 → 0.0, EPR 286.5 → 313.6, RBMK-1000 0.0 → 0.0, MSRE 289.0 → 15.4, WINDSCALE 12.8 → 0.0, DUAL 0.0 → 432.2.
