The charge is 0.6 MPa and not 0.3 because at 0.3 NUSCALE and DUAL rupture their vessels inside 60 s, and at 0.15 the loop cavitates (the pump passes 10 kg/s) | ERROR, inherited — the circulating-water circuit still has no resistance a real one is made of | high |
| the radiator panels against the duty | a heat sink is bought for the rejection at its design temperature | **re-sized 20/09/26.** `SINK_MARGIN` is 1.0: T^4 is convex, so `sum(T_i^4) > N·T_mean^4` and a series string sheds MORE than the same area at the mean — the old margin's stated reason had its sign backwards (`radiator.js series`: the pair-to-mean ratio against its analytic value to 1e-12). Measured on STOCK, dice off: the fleet rests at 313.7 K at 20 s, ~7 K over `RAD_TDES`, within a ±2.1 K series spread only as a stated gap; condenser 330.6 K; MWe 375.7 → 354.1 at 120 s (−5.7 %). BN-600 and MSRE commission past the turbine trip on the smaller sink (`condP0` 0.026 / 0.035 MPa against `TURB_TRIP_P` 0.02) and trip at tick 0: BN-600 survives derated (~0.89), MSRE collapses to ~0.56 with the turbine mostly offline | the sized duty undershoots actual rejection ~12 % (the turbine delivers 0.285 of heat against 0.360 sized; MSRE worse on its 0.697 FIT `eff`) | YES on the cut — the analytic check decided it; **MODEL** on the duty shortfall | high on the readings; medium on the sizing |
| a turbine's work | the isentropic enthalpy drop of its inlet steam to the exhaust pressure times a stage efficiency, 0.85–0.90 across a large machine's wet LP stages; the generator loses ~1.5 % | since 18/09/26 exactly that: saturated vapour expanded at constant entropy to the exhaust (liquid h and s off the IF97 tables), × 0.85 (`E_TURB_ETA`), × 0.985 at the generator (`eMWe()`); before, the design steam rise × the stated plant efficiency × an ideal-gas pressure-ratio shape. STOCK reads 0.306 thermal at 120 s | no reheat, no moisture separator, no extraction work: one expansion from saturation | YES on the single expansion; 0.85 is the low end of the published band | medium |
| a turbine exhaust's enthalpy | a hood passes what the last stage left in the steam, superheated or wet | it does. `turbb` reads h 3076.9 kJ/kg at 30 s and 3078.9 at 60 s on STOCK, x 1.0, and what lands on the condenser reads the same 3079 | **the ERROR is closed, and `c8ddde0` closed it.** The runaway was `advectStep()` crediting a `gasAt` donor `−m·(hg − h)`, which at superheat is a source feeding itself; `c8ddde0` narrowed the `gasAt` set on a vapour run from BOTH ends (`ea.gasAt = u; eb.gasAt = v`) to an end that `separates()`, and a hood is not a vessel with a free surface, so no edge on `turbb` carries `gasAt` any more (verified on the built net, 10/09/26). The credit itself is unchanged and still applies to a real separator | closed 10/09/26 | high on the reading and on the mechanism |
| the feed train at commissioning | a standby EFW line is at the header's pressure and does not ring | the whole header rings for about 0.3 s on the first ticks: `run:feed:efwpl-efwteer` peaks at **16.580 MPa**, 1.42× its own 11.66 MPa rating, and `efwtee` swings 6.83 MPa. The stub commissions at 558 K (the shell's saturation, reached by the seed walk over the header) and is flushed to 318 K by tick 8 | it no longer bursts and it no longer depends on the frame rate: ten seeds with the dice ON give 16.580 MPa every time, no `PIPE BURST`, no damage, 60 s. The peak is still over the rating and only the 17.49 MPa burst point keeps the run whole. Seeding the stub cold is not available: it sits on the HEADER side of the EFW pump's check valve, so no walk reaches it from the tank. **RE-MEASURED 20/09/26 on the clean tree (`tools/ringprobe.js`, preset 0, per tick to 0.3 s and again to 2 s): THE RING IS GONE.** The header sits flat at 10.3990 MPa out of the settle and peaks 10.3995 MPa at tick 8, **0.89× its rating**, not 1.42×. All three candidates ruled on with numbers: the INERTIA term is present (`edI` 31.200 on both bordering edges) but never excited — the pump is in standby and `ST.edW` stays inside ±0.016 kg/s all window; the SOLVE is settled — the pre-tick field already reads 10.3992 MPa and the tick-to-tick swing is 0.0002–0.0003 MPa; and the SEED is falsified outright — the run node measures **309.03 K, subcooled, x = 0** at t = 0, where the row says 558 K. The shell itself still reads 558.00 K, so that half of the old measurement was real; the seed walk simply no longer carries it onto this node. **THE MECHANISM IS CLOSED 21/09/26.** `eAdvectSeed()` now takes the flow law's own diode test — one predicate off `PT.adjEdge`/`PT.edU`, no new state, nothing allocated — so the walk crosses a check valve only the way the water could. Water does not arrive where it cannot flow to. What the test actually changes, measured on all nine presets (the same walk with it against without it): the main feed pump's suction face `feedt` on eight of nine, **387-405 K blind against the condensate header's own 316-339 K walled**, plus `run:feed:cpumpb-feedr` on three of them. RBMK-1000 moves nowhere. Nothing else moves, so the walk was reaching the feed pump's SUCTION backwards through its own check valve and seeding it at feedwater-outlet temperature | **CLOSED.** The 09/09/26 ring is gone and its cause is still not known — that half of this row stands as written. What is closed is the diode-blind walk itself: it can no longer put an unreachable state behind a check valve on any drawing. `tests/physics/seed.js` is the rig (a 400 K boundary, a checked pressurizer pointing back at it, a stub nothing anchors): walled the stub commissions at 406.42 K off its own circuit, and with the diode stood down the walk carries the unreachable 400 K across, so the check is seen to fail | high on the new measurement; **none** on the cause of the 09/09/26 ring's disappearance |
| **an intermediate exchanger's second stream (`ROLE.ihx`)** | a real second circuit with its own pump, inventory and pressure | **two real internal paths**, the declaration `ROLE.sg` already makes: both streams are network nodes on their own circuits, `ihxQAt()` is counterflow NTU with both inlets read off the field, and the pot is gone | no gap worth stating on the stream itself | — | high |
| an exchanger's own tube rupture | a leak between two sodium circuits | `ROLE.ihx.sgtr` is `false` — the tubes cannot let go | no exchanger leak event at all | YES, deliberately out of scope 06/09/26 | high |
| **how many exchangers in series** | as many as the plant has | **any number, structurally.** `ihxFeeds()` is a circuit walk, `sgActive()` asks whether the core reaches the tube face, and nothing relates a stage to a stage by name | **FLOWN 20/09/26, and the walk holds.** `tools/sandbox/net.js` key `netIhx3`: a stock PWR core tapped by `ihx1`, relayed `ihx1 → ihx2 → ihx3` at UA 800 / 600 / 400 kW/K, 60 s. It lays clean with no refused run and no dangling end, and all three stages pass heat at once — adding a third broke nothing. The counterflow effectiveness was coded independently off `SX.stgFl`/`stgC`/`stgT`/`stgX` and `PT.stageUA` — not by calling `eIhxQ()` — and **matches the engine to four decimals on every stage at every sampled tick** (at 60 s: 0.9988, 0.9259, 0.9000 analytic against 0.9988, 0.9259, 0.9000 measured). Worth recording about the rig: `eStageFed()` only counts a stage's hot side as fed if it reaches the CORE's own loop or another stage's cold face, so a bare source tank will not start a chain; and a pumped relay ring with no expansion tank cavitates itself to zero head, measured, not assumed | DRIFT is DISCHARGED on the claim itself: the generality is real, measured against the analytic law. **Two things are open and are NOT defects until somebody isolates them.** (1) Energy closure along the chain is NOT shown: q1 holds about 37.3 MW off the core tap while q2 climbs 26 kW to 2.19 MW and q3 1.57 to 1.58 MW, both still rising at 60 s — the relay circuits' volume-to-flow ratio gives a slow fill, and 60 s is all that fits the 10 s wall-clock budget. It needs `engSnap()`/`@@MORE` chunking, which `sandbox.js` does not have. (2) At 60 s stage 1's secondary outlet reads **473.9 K against a 468.0 K hot inlet, 5.9 K over** — a literal second-law read. The effectiveness at that same tick was 0.9988, under 1, so the formula never claimed it; it appears once, after five clean samples, right as the outlet closed on its ceiling, which reads like a discrete-step overshoot in the node's own mixing. **Unclassified, and said so** | **high** on the walk and the effectiveness; **none** on the closure and the overshoot |
| a generator's tube-side heat capacity rate | its own flow and its own fluid | `sgQAt()` reads `stageStream(s,id,0,rf).C` — the solved flow on that machine's own tube run, times its own circuit's `cp`. `sgUACap()` takes the same figure, and the two-phase case is `stageStream()`'s infinite rate rather than a second quality test beside it | it was `fl·P.netRef/n·P.sat.cp`, the PRIMARY's reference flow and the PRIMARY's `cp`, on a generator that may stand on another circuit. Swapped 07/09/26. **No shipped preset exercises it**: every `PLANTPRE` row puts its generators on the core's own circuit, so the two figures agree — stock plant 20 s, turbine flow moved 622.747 → 622.745 kg/s and `P.sgUA` 45894.8 → 45896.3. It bites only on a bench plant with an exchanger spliced in, which is the plant BN-600 is supposed to be | no gap worth stating | high |
| **BN-600 has an intermediate sodium loop** | three circuits. The intermediate loop is why the plant is safe — a sodium-water reaction in the steam generator cannot reach radioactive primary sodium | **two circuits. Primary sodium goes straight into the generator tubes**, and `ROLE.sg.sgtr` is true, so a tube rupture is primary sodium against water. The MACHINE can now be built — the exchanger's second stream is real and a hand-drawn three-circuit plant forms three circuits with the barrier intact — but the preset still ships two | the plant does the one thing an SFR is built never to do | ERROR | high |

## Compartment, damage and crew

| what | real machine | this model | distance | on purpose? | sure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| air heat capacity | the mass actually in the cell, at its own species mixture, on c_p(T): dry air 1.005 kJ/kg/K at 300 K, 1.141 at 1000 K, 1.249 at 2000 K, and hydrogen's c_v is 14× air's per kilogram | **since 20/09/26 exactly that.** `ROOM_CGAME` is deleted. `eRoomGasA()` reads m·c_v over `ST.roomM`, `ST.roomVap` and `ST.roomH2`, each species' c_p(T) off its own NIST WebBook Shomate row through `shoCpA()` — the one polynomial the coolants already use. NIST has no row for air, so c_p,air is mass-weighted over N2/O2/Ar/CO2 at 0.7553/0.2315/0.0129/0.0005, which is how the published air tables are built. c_v = c_p − R/M per species, mixed by mass. A uniform 2034-point table from T_SPACE to `ROOM_TMAX` interpolates it, and u is the trapezoid integral of that same interpolant so du/dT is exactly the c_p the lookup answers. Outside 298.15–6000 K c_p is held at the end value: past 6000 K the hydrogen row's own polynomial turns c_v negative. Measured (`tests/physics/roomheat.js cp cv`): every species within 0.013 % of `shoCpA` across the whole grid; dry air 1.00505/1.14099/1.25038 against the published 1.005/1.141/1.249; γ 1.39989 at 300 K and 1.33623 at 1000 K; a half-air half-hydrogen cell holds 7.59× the all-air capacity to 1e-16; a cell vented to half its mass takes twice the rise for the same kilojoule (1.99764 against 2) | **none on the law.** What is still bought: the 298.15 K floor and the 6000 K ceiling on the fits, and Shomate extrapolated between 6000 K and `ROOM_TMAX` where real air would dissociate | NO — the ×50 cut is spent | high |
| structure that stores heat | a compartment holds air and it holds steel, joined by a surface conductance: the air responds on seconds, the steel on minutes to hours | **since 20/09/26 a second node, `ST.roomTS`, per cell.** `ROOM_CSTRUCT` is 8 mm mild plate (7850 kg/m³, c_p 0.49 kJ/kg/K) over the cell's own floor and deckhead, 2·MPC² = 0.4356 m², plus `ROOM_CSTRUCT_F` per outward hull face over `HULL_FACE_A`. `ROOM_GSTRUCT` = `ROOM_H` 6 W/m²/K over the same areas. A machine's own skin is NOT counted — `ROOM_HK` already exchanges with it. **And the hull plate is what radiates**: the Stefan-Boltzmann term moved off the air onto `roomTS`, taken backward about the plate's own temperature, because the air reaches space by convecting to the plate and never past it. Measured (`roomheat.js struct`): the two-body gap decays at 284.53 s against the analytic C_a C_s/(g(C_a+C_s)) = 284.66 s, 0.04 %. **What the old ballast turned out to be:** `ROOM_CGAME` 50 implied 51.2 kJ/K per cell, i.e. 104.5 kg of steel over 0.4356 m² — **30.6 mm of plate**, three to five times a real machinery-space flat. The ballast was not a real structure. The real 8 mm plate is 13.40 kJ/K per interior cell, so the compartment's long-run capacity is 14.2 kJ/K against the old 52.3 | stiffeners, girders and the condensation on them are not counted, so 13.40 kJ/K is a FLOOR on the real structure, not an estimate of it | NO | medium on the plate thickness, high on the law |
| the pressure of a cell holding steam or hydrogen | an ideal-gas mixture: p = n·R·T/V over the moles of every species; a kilogram of hydrogen is 14 times the moles of a kilogram of air | since 18/09/26 the moles of air, steam and hydrogen (`eRoomMolFill()`); before, every kilogram was priced at air's gas constant, so a hydrogen-laden cell read about a fourteenth of its partial pressure | exact at the ideal-gas level (`tests/physics/roomgas.js`, 0.001 %) | — | high |
| hydrogen autoignition | **two different quantities, and the literature keeps them apart.** BULK autoignition in air: 773–858 K (NFPA 497 500 °C, IEC 60079-20-1 560 °C, ISO/TR 15916 585 °C). HOT-SURFACE ignition, where a small hot body lights the gas passing it, is higher, because the gas beside it is only there briefly: measured 1000–1170 K (Mevel, Melguizo-Gavilanes, Boeck & Shepherd, *Int. J. Heat Fluid Flow* 2019, on a 9.3 × 5.1 mm SS316 glow plug — 1010 K at 5 % H2 to 1170 K at 74 %; Morreale et al., WHEC 2010, 1033 K on a steel coil; Tamm et al. 1987 via NUREG/CR-6530, 1050 K turbulent and 1123 K quiescent in 50 mol % steam) | **since 20/09/26, two constants.** `H2_IGN` 773 K is read at the GAS temperature (`eIgnites()`, bulk); `H2_IGN_SURF` 1050 K is read at a part's SKIN. Before, one constant did both and a hot pipe lit hydrogen 277 K earlier than any experiment says it would | `H2_IGN` stays at the low, conservative end of its own 85 K band, **decided 20/09/26:** a room cell is a large volume, where quenching is least and the bottom of the band is the physically appropriate end as well as the conservative one. `H2_IGN_SURF` is a single number standing for something that is not one: the threshold moves 128 K on cylinder orientation alone and up to 50 K on surface chemistry, and the same Caltech group's 2011 thesis reports 808–910 K for lean H2 where its own 2019 work reports 1010–1017 K. 1050 K is where three independent sources overlap | DRIFT closed 20/09/26; NO on the split — one constant was standing in for two mechanisms | high on the bulk band, medium on the hot-surface figure, and the literature itself says no single constant exists |
| what a jet, a vent or a boiling pool may heat the air TO | a stream cannot drive a gas above its own temperature. A flashing break delivers a two-phase mixture pinned at the saturation temperature of the space it flashes into; a relief passes steam at the shell's own temperature; a boiling pool gives up its latent heat at T_sat and no higher | **since 20/09/26, capped at exactly that** (`eJetQCapA()`), the same rule `eFireStep()` already took on a burning pool and `eAdvectSrc()` takes on a wall. Three callers: the break jet (`eJetLiqQ()`, donor = T_sat at the cell's own pressure), the relief and shell-vent plumes (donor = the hottest shell's steam), and the boiling term in `eWaterHeat()` (donor = T_sat). **This is not a tuning knob; without it job 1 is wrong.** The deposit is sensible heat with no donor temperature behind it, and on the ×50 ballast that was invisible — on a REAL air capacity a plume cell ran to thousands of kelvin no source in the plant can reach. Measured on the stock hot-leg break: the plume cell reads **363–457 K over the first 0.26 s** where before the cap it read 679–1195 K and the compartment ran away to `ROOM_TMAX` and 30 MPa within 5 s. | a compartment blast wave | a blast is a front: an overpressure that travels at the speed of sound in the gas it is in, falls off with distance from the charge, reflects off structure and leaves a rarefaction behind it. | what the blast layers DRAW | a pressure field is one quantity, so a picture of it is one colour and the magnitude is how strongly that colour is laid on. A staircase of hues reads as a set of regions, not as a wave passing | **Since 11/09/26 (`tools/wavemock.html` ported, its solver not):** PRESSURE NOW draws the SLOPE, `waveSlope()`: `|grad p|` in kPa per cell, each component the mean of the differences across the cell's OPEN faces (`G.bx`/`G.by` === 1; a machine face passes `ROOM_BLOCK` 0.12 and counts as shut), so a still room at any pressure is black and a wall gives no slope. It is coloured the mock's way (`paint()`), not in bands: one ramp `C.wave` to `C.waveHi` on `(s/ref)²`, alpha `min(0.92, √(s/ref))`, culled under 2 % and, since 11/09/26, wherever neither slope component reaches `WAVE_P_LO` 0.05 kPa - the gas step's own rest gate, below which it stops solving and leaves the step standing, so a 0.017 kPa step left by water pushing air aside drew as a patch that never faded, against `waveRef`, the loudest slope since the plant was built, which never falls and is floored at `PNOW_LO` 0.5 kPa per cell so float noise in a still room cannot become the reference. **The BLAST tool's charge is the mock's Gaussian**, `roomBlastCharge()`, `BLAST_SIG` 2.4 cells, peaking at the figure in the box, into open air only: a one-cell charge was a grid-scale source and, measured 11/09/26, 17-27 % of lit cells rang as a checkerboard against 0.0 % for the Gaussian; at 5 MPa in one cell 56-84 cells also sat on the vacuum floor. The box defaults to 0.1 MPa, the mock's charge, where it was 5 MPa. A charge throws the mock's 160 sparks and 24 puffs (`blastFx()`, off `s.blastEv`), and every spark and puff falls at 9.81 m/s² with a 0.6 s drag and is 0.3 cell across. BLAST is on by default, so scars and hits draw with the wave as they do in the mock. BLAST no longer fills cells: the `s.roomPPk` cell fill, its zone colours, band strokes and pulse are gone from the layer and live on only in the tooltip's BLAST PEAK row. It draws SCARS, one per passage: `roomScarStep()` books `peak − HIT_LO` (5 kPa) into `s.roomScar` each time a cell's bang has fallen to half its own peak, never decayed, and each machine face is bitten to `half a cell + 0.7 × depth × min(1, scar/45 kPa)`, smoothed 1-2-1, in half-cell blocks, four shades of `C.scar`. Measured 11/09/26, `probe.js blast`, three charges 1 s apart beside `pump0`, on the Gaussian charge: 100 kPa, scar sum 7510 / 15222 / 22894 kPa, ratio 1 : 2.027 : 3.048; 500 kPa, 182025 / 369846 / 557880, ratio 1 : 2.032 : 3.065. On the one-cell charge it was 1 : 1.995 : 2.990 at 500 kPa, and at 2000 kPa the ratio broke (1 : 1.008 : 1.312) because the plant bursts and the room is no longer linear. It also draws HITS, a 0.3-cell bar on every shut face of a cell carrying more than 5 kPa, held 0.12 s. The expanding rings `burnRings` are GONE: they were a Rankine-Hugoniot circle with a fitted slowdown, never the field. Sparks and smoke add the solved air (`s.roomPU`/`s.roomPV`) to their own velocity. History: 10/09/26: `BLASTFX` states ONE colour, `C.red`, for both blast layers, and the opacity is a continuous ramp, `BLASTFX.a0` 0 to `a1` 0.55. **`a0` is ZERO and that is not cosmetic**: the layer has a threshold, so any opacity AT the threshold is a hard edge, and a decaying field walks every cell in the room across that edge one at a time - which draws as speckle, not as a wave. Measured at `a0` 0.10, a 400 kPa source on the stock board: 22 to 37 % of the lit boundary was a lone cell against a dark one, and the fraction ROSE as the field decayed, because more of the room was sitting on the threshold. At `a0` 0 the worst alpha step between neighbours falls from 0.10-everywhere to 0.051 by 0.40 s and the picture fades out instead of switching off. The speckle was NOT the checkerboard: sign alternation between neighbours ran 6-23 % and FELL with time while the speckle rose, and raising `WAVE_D4` from 0.35 to 1 did not move it. **Both ramps are LOGARITHMIC and both ends are a real pressure.** PRESSURE NOW ramps the live field from `PNOW_LO` 0.5 kPa, the tooltip's own floor, to `BLASTFX.now` 70 kPa, the rung that takes heavy rotating plant; BLAST ramps `s.roomPPk` from `BLASTFX.lo` 15 kPa to `BLASTFX.scar` 200 kPa, the rung that takes a pressure vessel, on top of its soot fill. A linear ramp was tried first and **thrown out on measurement**: it was pinned on the p99 of a 5 MPa blast, 35 kPa, and the field this game actually draws spans two decades - measured over 2 s in an empty room, the median is 0.64 kPa on a 100 kPa charge and 1.94 on a 5 MPa one, with maxima 1.40 and 70.17 - so on anything but the biggest charge every cell drew within 5 % of the alpha floor and the picture had no contrast at all. Alpha at 0.64 / 1.76 / 5.61 / 21 / 70 kPa is now 0.119 / 0.197 / 0.286 / 0.388 / 0.480, against 0.101 / 0.114 / 0.156 / 0.24 / 0.48 linear. **PRESSURE NOW draws MAGNITUDE, `|P|`, not the signed gauge.** Gauge pressure is signed and half of an acoustic wave is a rarefaction, so drawing the positive half alone left the ring hollow and made every cell blink as the sign alternated: measured one tick after a 400 kPa source, 356 cells drew against 600 that carry a field, and about 40 % of the live wave was invisible at any moment. The cost is that a compartment vented to space, which sits at -101.3 kPa gauge and whose `roomPGauge()` floors at 0, now draws at full opacity and stays there. **The magnitude then has to be SMOOTHED, and this was found by looking at the running page rather than by measuring**: `|p - mean|` has a ZERO at every wavefront, a wave is nothing but wavefronts, so the raw magnitude puts a black hole in every cell whose pressure is passing through its compartment's mean - which is about half the room at any instant. On screen that is a uniform red field with random black holes punched through it, and it is what the user called spotted cheese. `BLASTFX.smooth` was 0 with a comment saying it added no information; it is 1 and it is not decoration, it is what fills the amplitude's own zeros. One 5-point pass (`blastSmooth()`) over the drawn scalar, not over `s.roomP`, so nothing in the physics sees it. Confirmed by eye on `plantPreset(0)`, a 1500 kPa blast at cell 30,28: holes gone, one smooth cloud. **The layer is drawn against the region MEAN and then smoothed, so it is no longer a per-cell reading of anything** - the tooltip is the reading and the layer is a picture. The five thresholds survive as `BLASTFX.zones`, which is now a LABEL ladder only and is read by `roomCellTip()`: 20 kPa a cabinet, 70 heavy rotating plant, 120 a pipe, 200 a pressure vessel, and those are the machines' own limits. The band-edge strokes both layers drew are gone, and both tooltips in `src/render/layers.js` were rewritten to describe what is drawn. `BLASTFX.full` 600 kPa stays and is now the EVENT's own scale only, read by `evFx()` for the particle count; `BLASTFX.smooth` stays 0 | **the colour no longer says which machine the pressure breaks.** Before this, a cell drawn amber meant "enough to take heavy rotating plant" at a glance, over the whole board at once. Now only the tooltip says it, one cell at a time, and reading which of five bands a cell is in costs a pointer move. That was asked for and it is a real loss | YES - decision 6 of the plan, taken by the user on 10/09/26 on the grounds that a wave walking up and down a colour staircase does not look like a wave | high - the ramp figures are p99s of a measured field and are stated here with the run they came off |

A part wrecked in the room sweep keeps what exceeded `LEAN_YIELD` 0.8 cell as `s.partSet[id]`; a repair deletes it. Every part leans, fittings included; the grid cell, ports, runs, name, lamp and hit rect stay put. **Both are held, by the user's call of 11/09/26**: `leanCap()` bends the drawn total (elastic plus set) softly under `LEAN_MAX` 1 cell and the kept set under `LEAN_SET_MAX` 0.1 cell, `max·tanh(m/max)`, so a small lean is still the linear one. Measured after the cap, 2000 kPa: every set 0.10 cell or under, `pump0` drawn at 0.77 cell. What the uncapped law gave, measured 11/09/26 on the stock plant, charge 3 cells left of `pump0`, largest elastic target over 1 s before the ×2 kick: 100 kPa, 1.47 cells on the 1x1 `rv0`; 500 kPa, 7.36 cells on `rv0` and 0.94 on the 2x2 `cwtank`; 2000 kPa, 99.5 cells on `tee0`. The wreck sets at 2000 kPa run to 70 cells on 1x1 fittings and 13-19 cells on `cwtank`, `efw`, `feed`, `ctrl`. The mock only ever carried 5x3 boxes and one 100 kPa charge. **The set's direction is not reliably away from the charge**: 11 of 24 sets at 2000 kPa point toward it, because the BLAST tool's damage is judged only on the tick of the act (`s.roomBang` lasts one tick): a 2000 kPa charge reads 18.5 kPa inside `pump0` three cells away on that tick and wrecks 2 parts, and the next charge's tick judges the whole room on what is still ringing and wrecks 225, so most sets are taken off an echo. | what a break's back-pressure sees | a choked orifice discharging into a compartment sees the volume, not the millimetre of gas at its own lip: the wave crosses the room far faster than the discharge empties anything | `regionP()` reads the region MEAN of `s.roomP`, since 09/09/26; since 11/09/26 `s.roomP` is each cell's own read and the mean is a view over it, not a lump anything evolves. `regionDP()` (hoop stress on the wall) and `roomPAt()` (what a machine's own cells took) stay on the WORST cell, because both are local by nature. **Amended 10/09/26.** `regionDP()` is a READOUT and nothing else now: the panels and the room layer print it, and the wall is judged cell by cell on `matCellDP()` - the difference across THAT cell, over the neighbours of it that are gas, floored at zero where only one of them is. | what a breached containment does to the heat | two spaces joined by a vertical opening exchange air two ways on the stack effect: V = (1/3)·C_d·W·H^1.5·sqrt(g·ΔT/T_c) each way, so Q = K·ΔT^1.5 and ΔT = (Q/K)^(2/3). C_d 0.65, the middle of the measured 0.6–0.7. Brown & Solvason, *Int. J. Heat Mass Transfer* 5 (1962) 859–868; SFPE Handbook, Vent Flows | **since 20/09/26 exactly that, and the law is on the OPENING.** `eRoomLive()` walks the live hole cells into contiguous VERTICAL runs; each run is one opening of height n·MPC and width `ROOM_DEPTH`, and the ordinary eddy conduction across the wall line is stood down — the buoyant term replaces it, it does not add to it. Capped at the rate that brings the two sides level in one tick, the same rule `eAdvectSrc()` takes on a wall. A hole in a DECK is a different correlation and keeps the ordinary term. Measured (`tests/physics/breach.js`): a 6-cell (2.80 × 4.00 m) opening passing 500 kW holds **90.61 K against the correlation's 90.71** (0.11 %); four times the heat holds **2.553× the gap against 4^(2/3) = 2.520** (1.3 %), where a conductance would hold four times; and a 2-cell against a 6-cell opening holds **0.341 against 0.333** (2.6 %), where the same law applied per FACE would hold 0.481. **The 11/09/26 model held 4.5 K where 62.4 was owed; it now lands.** The rig changed with it and that is stated: the cold space is HELD and the structure is held at the air, because the old rig measured the hot chimney against the whole ship's air and most of that gap was the ROOM's own transport, not the opening's | **it lands: 0.11 % on the gap, 1.3 % on the heat exponent, 2.6 % on the height ratio** (20/09/26). What is NOT covered: a hole in a DECK, which keeps the ordinary eddy term and is a different correlation. Everything that follows is the superseded 10–11/09/26 history and the 20/09/26 measurement of the OLD model, kept because the numbers were taken. **measured 11/09/26, stock plant, 5 MPa BLAST beside `pump0`: no hole in 25 s** (row: the gas is isothermal); the plant's own event at 25 s opens 7, and at 30 s the interior reads 609 K against 303 K ship air, at 73.6 against 75.4 kPa gauge. A wrecked bottom side-wall cell under a stock hot-leg burst passes water at 14.6 t/s at first and 414 kg/s at 14 s. The figures below are the 10/09/26 lumped model and are superseded. **CLASS: MODEL.** Before: **the mask was a design fact until 10/09/26**, so a breached compartment equalised its PRESSURE and still could not ventilate its heat: measured on the stock plant, 5 MPa BLAST, 70 holes open, interior 676.9 K against ship air 471.9 K at 60 s, and the gap closing only from 244 to 205 K over 50 s. After, on the charge that does breach it (25 cells, 6 holes), interior 701.2 K against 415.6 K, with the ship's air climbing 326 to 416 K over the same 50 s against a live decay-heat source. **Nobody has measured what gap a real 6-cell aperture should hold.** The wrecked cell itself is in no region, so its own mass is never levelled and its density stays whatever it was seeded at - ambient - which is the stand-in for a hole. **CLASS: MODEL** | NO — the fixed-conductance breach is closed | high: measured against a published correlation, three ways |

Before, it was shared evenly over every cell of the box, so each column the box spans rained its own thin thread. | **water on the floor** | it lands where it comes out, falls accelerating, runs toward level at the speed a shallow sheet runs (sqrt(g*h), ~2 m/s half a metre deep), stands where it stands, and a machine it rises round is drowned | **since 12/09/26 one law for both liquids: a pressure at every cell's floor and a speed on every face** (`liqStep()`, room.js; `liqSettle()` is its name at the two call sites). Each tick, per liquid, one implicit face-momentum solve of `roomGasStep()`'s shape (`roomCgSolve()`, the same SGS-preconditioned CG, `LIQ_CG_TOL` 1e-9), then the mass moves on the faces it solved and its energy rides upwind with it (`roomAdvect()`). The unknown is the pressure at the cell's own floor: a cell with room left is a free surface (`p = pGas + ρgh`, compliance A/g); a full cell carried by the floor with liquid or a ceiling over it is stiff (compliance cap/K, `WATER_BULK` 2.2e9 Pa, sodium `COOLANT.bulk` 5.8e9) and its pressure is STATE (`s.roomWP`, `s.roomPoolP`) - and since 12/09/26 "liquid over it" is a DEPTH, `h[i-GW] > LIQ_H_LO` (1 mm), never a bare `M > 0`; a full cell with gas over it is a free surface at its own ceiling, and goes stiff for a second pass the tick it is fed from the side; a full cell in the air is a lump that presses on nothing beside it. Gravity is in p. Horizontal drive = the hydrostatic force over the wetted face, over the mean depth of the water between the two centres, plus the gas difference: the shallow-water u-equation's g·dh/dx (**21/09/26**; it was over the deeper side's depth, `ρg(h_i²-h_j²)/(2·max h)`, which halved the drive at a front); vertical drive = the floor pressure over the receiver's ceiling pressure, or over its gas when it has room. Friction: Manning `LIQ_MANNING` 0.012 on the face's own depth, and the orifice loss `LIQ_CD` 0.6 where a standing body leaves through a hole shot in a wall. Upwind momentum advection on every face, so a stream falls at sqrt(2gz) and not at g·t; **since 21/09/26 the momentum is carried INTO a face from its upwind neighbours, v_up·|v_up|/L**, where it was advected at the face's own speed, so a face at rest - every new face at a front - took none of the water pushing into it. `faceLimit()` cuts a cell's outflow to what it holds and its inflow to the room it has, iterated to rest and then shaved exact by at most 16 tail passes, so no cell is ever filled past its cap by a face and no clamp makes mass up afterwards. Since 21/09/26 every cell is capped, by volume, and a carried cell over its cap sends the excess to the nearest cell with room before the solve, on `eLiqLand()`'s law (row below; from 12/09/26 only a carried cell was capped and its excess was burped up its own column); two cells both in the air have no face between them unless they move sideways faster than they fall (`lz`), so a stream has no side to leak out of; a stream landing on a body turns into the side faces of the cell it lands in at its own speed (`lat`; since 21/09/26 as momentum, where it was a force on the face, which drove a face of grams to the `LIQ_V_MAX` guard); a source lands at the speed it left its opening at (`liqLand()`'s `v0` - the pressure difference for a break, the stated rate over one cell face for the INJECT tool, the spray's own speed for metal) rather than at rest; and a body or a band under one device pixel is not drawn (`liqDraw()`, render/room.js). A source lands through `eLiqLand()` at the nearest cell with room that the liquid reaches (since 21/09/26; it walked up its own column and built a full column in the air under a 10 t/s break), and its volume is a source in the gas solve rather than a push into the ring. The per-face gas swap is gone since 21/09/26 except where a liquid falls out of a cell that held no gas: the liquid's volume reaches the gas as a squeeze the gas step relieves (`the implicit step's front`). `s.roomPool`/`s.roomPoolE` went to double. Gone: `liqLevelPass()`, `liqFlood()`, `liqFallPass()`, `liqGasSwap()`, the heap, `LIQ_FALL`, `waterCap()`, `regionSumpCap()`. **Re-measured 12/09/26** with the six laws above in, `tools/liqprobe.js`, `plantPreset(0)`, dice off, the same run on the tree without them in brackets. **Drain**, the user's own sequence - 40 t poured on the deck, the floor shot at 30,30, then only waiting: 16.57 t left on the floor at 15 s and still running at 1.27 t/s, against **21.26 t stalled at 0.09 t/s** with the stiff test on a bare `M > 0` and 20.03 t at 0.31 t/s on the tree before any of this work. The frozen case read columns 26-29 at 100 % full, standing, face speed +0.00 m/s, one cell from a hole that was 2 % full; they now sit at 37-55 % and run at 1.3-2.0 m/s. **Settle**, the same pour with no hole, four 2 s windows in a row: the surface tilt reads +82, -145, -128, -8 mm, so it is the basin's own seiche and not a held step, and the gas explains 0 mm of it. **Still pool** 2.5 cells deep laid over the floor: 0 cells change state over 10 s, surface flat within 0.0 mm, the solve stands down (2.54 ms a tick). **Row step**, two pools 4 cm apart laid one after the other, the second lay burying cell 21,29 by 3.544 kg over its own cap: the step reads 15.1 mm at 2 s, 3.0 at 4 s and 4.1 at 10 s, with no cell anywhere left over its cap (32.4 / 30.6 / 27.1 mm, and the buried cell still 3.544 kg over at 10 s). **Stream**, 10 t/s at 29,13 over 0.5-4 s, counting cells a board pixel wide beside the fall: rows 15-19 0.00 a tick, rows 20-24 1.03, 0 pillar ticks of 175 (9.52 / 7.23 / 0) - what is left is the crown thrown up at the impact, not a film down the whole fall. **Free fall**, 10 t/s from 7 m: lands at 1.22 s against sqrt(2h/g) 1.19 (1.42), 10.20 t in the air at 3 s against 11.9, fastest cell 11.5 m/s. **Floor hole** 20,30 under 1.87 m: 8.16 t/s over 0.5-1.5 s against the orifice's 6.78 (+20 %), **peak tick 11.25** - inside the probe's own "no tick over twice the orifice" bar for the first time, against 18.0 before the depth gate and 34.9 before any of it; the 9.5 this row used to carry was older still. Under a 0.2 m sheet 0.82 t/s against ~0.7, peak 1.06. **Side hole** 37,29, 1.63 m to its middle: 6.80 t/s against 6.34, first tick 1.19. **U-tube**, wall 25-28 at x 26: still the slosh, dz -11 mm at 24 s (-20 before the depth gate), the means equal. **U-tube with gas**, 5.0 kPa on one side: holds 0.506 m against dp/(ρg) 0.506. **Bubble**, a cell of air under two full cells: at the surface in 0.18 s, worst gas density 1.97x ambient. **Dam break**, one cell deep: the front runs as a bore at 1.46 m/s, 0.68 of sqrt(gh) 2.14 (1.43). **The jet** (hot-leg break 28,15, floor shot at 20,30, 10 t/s at 12,15): 7.42 cells over 10 kg change state a tick over 2-6 s, worst 20, floor gas spread 14.05 kPa (10.14, worst 40). **Re-measured 20/09/26 and the 12/09/26 figures do not stand: on the tree as committed the same run reads 46.70 flips a tick, worst 157, spread 176.63 kPa, surface span 3017 mm.** After the face-limiter rewrite below, 61.65 flips, worst 163, spread 373.49 kPa, span 2897 mm; at `FACE_TAIL` 64, 60.19 / 181 / 325.13 kPa / 3236 mm. The scene is chaotic and the three readings do not order, so nothing here is a measured distance from a real machine - the jet's state flips stay the open gap they were, and the gas spread now carries the through-flow the old tail was throwing away. **Books**: ledger residual 0, and the water on the deck closes against its own `sump` and `inject` lines to under 1e-6 kg on every scenario in this row, where the same check read 12.9 g created on one tick of the user's sequence before - `faceLimit()`'s fixed point stopped at its sweep count whether or not it had converged and `faceMove()`'s clamp made the difference up. **BN-600** `pipe:19,12` cut, 6 s: 12.10 kg burnt in flight and 221 kg on the deck, both unmoved. Cost: dry stock 2.39 ms a tick, the 10 t/s pour 6.10 ms, BN-600 metal spill 7.2-9.8 - this machine reads about 1.3x the times pinned above it, so the pour is the figure to compare against its own 6.8 ms on the same machine before the work | **the front of a dam break is a bore, not a tongue**, and **20/09/26 the TARGET was found wrong before the model was judged**. The ideal inviscid dry-bed front is **2·√(g·h₀)**, not √(g·h₀) (Ritter 1892, *Z. Ver. Deutscher Ing.* 36(33) 947-954; confirmed in Castro-Orgaz & Chanson, *Environ. Fluid Mech.* 2017, and the USGS FEQUTL manual — the same solution gives h = 4/9·h₀ and u = 2/3·√(g·h₀) at the dam line, which also checks out). A real frictional front decelerates from 2·√(g·h₀) toward √(g·h₀) with time (Whitham, *Proc. R. Soc. A* 227, 1955; Dressler, *J. Res. NBS* 49, 1952), and Dressler's own 1954 laboratory dam breaks measured **1.54–1.74·√(g·h₀)** a few seconds after release. At the probe's h₀ = 0.467 m that is an ideal 4.28 m/s and a real 3.30–3.72 m/s. The measured front ran 10 cells in 3.20 s = 1.46 m/s, **0.34 of the ideal solution and 0.39–0.44 of the real one**. **21/09/26, which term cost it, measured by standing each down alone on the probe's rig: neither Manning nor the upwind advection (1.33 → 1.35 and 1.21 m/s without them).** Two laws were wrong and are fixed: the drive over the deeper side's depth (half of g·dh/dx at a front) and momentum advected at the face's own speed (a face at rest took nothing from the water pushing into it). A third defect sat in the gas: a pool cell uncovering a sliver of room read its own lagging fill as a suction of -20 to -30 kPa and held the water back; a surface now feels its gas space's volume-weighted pressure. **`tests/physics/liquid.js dam`, one cell deep (h₀ 0.467 m): the front runs 2.07 m/s over its first second and 1.93 m/s over 2.4–4.8 s, 0.48 of Ritter and 0.55 of Dressler's band mean; it decelerates, 0.95 → 0.89 → 0.80 √(g·h₀); the dam line reads 0.233 m against 4/9·h₀ = 0.207 (+12 %) and 1.09 m/s against 2/3·√(g·h₀) = 1.43 (−24 %).** Still 1.8× slow. Not diagnosed further; what IS measured is that a dam two cells deep runs no faster than one (0.22 s a cell either way), where a real front scales with √h₀ - the stacked-cell water, not a friction or a drive term. **Withdrawn, measured 21/09/26: the passage under a wall holds at the right depth** - a diving bell six rows under the outside surface (`roomgas.js pocket`) settles with 21.21 kPa of gas difference against 21.18 of head, so "a gas-loaded passage under a wall holds" was the right behaviour; before the gas-space pressure the same rig read 25.96 against 20.79, its full cells' stored pressures differing sideways by up to 3.8 kPa at rest. The U-tube probe sloshes and decays, 123 mm at 12 s to -7 mm at 24 s (the probe asks 5 mm), with 0.00 kPa of gas across it. **Withdrawn: the guard** - `LIQ_V_MAX` binds on 0 faces over the dam break (fastest 1.58 m/s); it bound on films pushed by the splash force, now momentum at the jet's own speed. Other probes, same session, committed tree in brackets: orifice hole, side hole and falling stream PASS (all FAIL); the free fall's first kilogram lands at 0.82 s against sqrt(2h/g) 1.19 (1.50) - first-order upwind smearing puts a little mass ahead of the front now that a face at rest takes momentum, while the fastest cell stays at 9.2 m/s under free fall's 11.7; the bubble rises in 0.16 s at worst 3.57× ambient density (never rises). The plan's rig asked for the dam front within 25 % and fewer than 1 state change a tick under the jet; neither is met, and the figures stand as read - both already failed before the 12/09/26 work below and both moved toward the real figure with it. **Three closed 12/09/26.** (A) A buried cell over its cap priced its pressure off its own fill, which is a 4.4 kPa pit under a 97 % cell: the row above fed it 6.7 kg a tick, its own upward face read "down", and nothing could ever leave it, so a 37 mm step in a surface stood for ever (22 mm still there at 20 s). (B) A cell of water in the air had a sideways face carrying the gas difference and a free-surface compliance, so a 10 t/s stream pushed 42-72 kg into the columns beside it down the whole height of the fall - half a board pixel wide, smeared to one by the canvas, and slow to drain because a 1 mm film falls at Manning's 0.84 m/s. (C) With that leak shut the stream backed up into a full column and stood as a pillar for 0.7 s at a time, because the source landed at rest, the landing sheet spread only by hydrostatics, and the cap cut applied to cells in the air as well. (D) **A few grams on top of a full floor cell cemented it.** The stiff test asked `M[i-GW] > 0`, so 0.029 kg of spray landing on a cell holding 871 kg made the cell below it incompressible, pressure as state, and it would not give up water sideways at all: a pool with a hole shot in its floor stalled with 21 t aboard at 0.09 t/s, columns 26-29 reading 100 % full and +0.00 m/s one cell from a 2 %-full hole, which is the left band, gap and right band the user photographed. The gate is a DEPTH now (`LIQ_H_LO`, 1 mm, the figure the same function already uses for a film that steps nowhere), and the project's own rule - a solved quantity never meets a bare `> 0` - is what it had been breaking. Everything else in this row improved with it and nothing regressed. | **the implicit step's front, and the gas being adiabatic** | a compartment's air compresses and expands adiabatically on the acoustic time, so the medium's stiffness is γ·p, a front runs at sqrt(γ·R·T) = 343.2 m/s for dry air at 20 °C, and a charge's heat is shared by the air it expands into | **since 20/09/26 the gas is ADIABATIC, not isothermal.** Three things changed together. (1) The compliance is `vg/(γ·R·T)` at the cell's OWN mixture and temperature, not `vg/(R·T)`. (2) **Energy rides the mass**: what crosses a face carries h of its donor and lands in a U at fixed volume, so a compressed cell really heats and there is no separate p·dV term. Implicit upwind, the same walk `eRoomAdvect()` runs on the species — a donor exports at its own NEW h, so every update is a convex blend and the step is bounded however much a cell passes; the sweeps settle what each donor leaves at and one conservative face pass moves it. (3) The temperature is a read off the energy (`eRoomTofUA()`, Newton on u(T) whose slope is c_v(T)), never a relaxation. **21/09/26, the gas and the liquid are solved against each other.** The liquid's face solve carries each gas component's adiabatic compliance (the cells joined by open gas faces, eliminated exactly so the solve stays SPD), rejoins gas the pass sealed and steps a squeezed pocket's compliance toward the secant of its isentrope (`LIQ_GAS_IT` passes), and a pocket takes no more than that solve gave it when the limiter runs; a surface feels its gas space's volume-weighted pressure. The volume a liquid moves reaches the gas as `gsDisp`, off the floored gas volume and never for boiling or condensing; its p·dV enters the energy update itself, a squeeze at the pressure it started at and an expansion at the pressure it ends at, each exact on an isobar. Air pushed out of a flooded cell lands with its energy and its old volume, flashing stops at the water's own saturation pressure, and every outside exchange is backward in the gas temperature (`ePhiFill()`), so a cell with no gas exchanges nothing. `eGsMixA()`'s capped mixing term is deleted — it existed only because the heat was not riding the mass. Measured (`tests/physics/roomheat.js`): a sealed 45-cell box conserves gas internal energy plus structure to **6e-16 relative over 200 ticks** of transport and relaxation (the old capped-mixing term loses 1.3e-3 over the same run — injected and seen to fail); the gas left in a blowing-down cell follows T/T0 = (p/p0)^((γ−1)/γ) to **1.38 %**; a sealed box at 40 kW and at 3000 kW settles at (γ−1)E/V to **0.01 %** and **1.5 %**, and the hot one only lands because c_p(T) is real; the medium's sound speed reads **343.26 m/s against the published 343.2** | **the transport is still implicit at CFL ≈ 15** (343 m/s crosses a 0.4667 m cell in 1/15 of a 0.02 s tick), so a travelling FRONT is not resolved and is not claimed: what the solve carries is the quasi-static field. The isentrope check is one discharging tick for the same reason. **A liquid's squeeze does its work since 21/09/26**: the bulk modulus reads 143.3 kPa against γ·p 141.9 (`roomheat.js sound`, was 102.4); a diving bell rides p·V^γ to 0.24 % while the water drives in, keeps its air to 3e-8 and settles where its gas balances the head to 0.1 % (`roomgas.js pocket`); a cell filling from 97 % to full reads the gas over it to 0.04 kPa (`fill`). **Over 60 s of a steam-line break no cell reads more than the break is driven by: 5.51 MPa against 6.09** (`breaksl`, 21/09/26, since the STOCK PWR containment is rated for the generator's pressure and no longer bursts; it read 11.2 against 14.6 before). **The hot leg FAILS on the same rated containment: 17.9-52.3 MPa against 14.86 over three runs** (`breakhl`; backlog 21/09/26), where the old 0.5 MPa walls burst at ~4 s, vented the room and read 11.05. The committed tree before the gas coupling read 613 and 684 MPa; with the gas coupling stood down the hot leg reads 29.9 MPa. **Withdrawn, measured: `ROOM_VG_MIN` is not a guard to delete** - at 1e-4 of a cell a sliver of gas conditions the gas solve badly and flashing water fills it to hundreds of MPa, so it stays at 0.01 as the grid's resolution and a finer pocket rises as a bubble (`eGasDisplace()`) | YES on the unresolved front (decision 8, and now measured rather than asserted); NO on the isothermal state and NO on the liquid's work — both closed | high on the measurements |
| ...the water's temperature | water on a floor has one, it boils off what it holds past saturation at the gas over it, and it trades heat with the air over its surface | since 12/09/26 `s.roomWaterE` kJ per cell, datum liquid at `H_DATUM` on `CP_W`, riding the faces upwind with the water (`roomAdvect()`), read by `roomWaterT()`; since 18/09/26 the temperature is read off the IF97 liquid table (`eRoomWaterT()`), where `CP_W` 5.5 kJ/kg/K read 373 K water as 349 K. In: a break lands saturated liquid at the cell's own gas on water's own curve (`satH(SAT_WATER, p)`; the circuit's own curve reads 293 K at a bar, the vapour-density row), condensate at its air's temperature, the INJECT tool at `T_HULL`. Out (`roomWaterHeat()`), three terms: boil-off of what stands past `satT(SAT_WATER, p_gas)` into that cell's steam, `hfg`-limited, its enthalpy over the air's charged to the air as a break's flash is; convection on `ROOM_H` over the free surface both ways, capped at the water's own capacity; the sodium-water reaction takes the share of what it consumes. Every exit takes energy pro rata. One readout, the WATER row of the cell tooltip. Hot-leg break plus 5 t/s at `T_HULL`, 7 s: 88 t at 293-583 K (the hottest cells sit under the jet's own MPa) | no heat into the floor or the wall; the flash fraction at the opening is still `openFlashX()`'s on the circuit's curve, so the split between what lands and what is steam stands on the vapour-density ERROR row | NO - decision 4, 12/09/26 | high on the water, low on the split |
| ...how falling water is drawn | a stream is a ribbon that thins as it speeds up | since 12/09/26 a moving cell in the air (`liqSpeed()` over `LIQ_REST` 0.05 m/s, not standing) draws a band as wide as the share of its cell it fills, its ends the mean with the moving cell over and under it (`liqDraw()`); standing liquid draws as before, one rectangle per column run and a surface stroke. The width is continuity: a stream's fill falls as sqrt(2gz) rises. No sub-grid, no blur | a film at rest in the air draws nothing; a stream fanned sideways by a gas gradient draws as separate columns | decision 5, 12/09/26, on the user's eyes - not yet looked at | - |
| ...whether the room's transport conserves mass | a transport operator relocates what a cell holds and creates none of it, to the arithmetic's own precision | **since 20/09/26 exactly conservative by construction.** `eFaceTail()` grows one factor per donor cell from the always-safe start `k = min(1, M/out)`, alternating forward and backward Gauss-Seidel sweeps of `k[i] = min(1, (M[i] + sum of inflow x k[donor])/out[i])`, and applies it once to the entry-state faces. The map is monotone and bounded by 1, and a cell's true final inflow is never below the inflow its own bound was taken against, so **every iterate is feasible and any stopping point conserves**; the pass count `FACE_TAIL` 16 is a through-flow quality knob, not a correctness one. Before, the tail iterated DOWNWARD from k = 1, so every iterate before convergence was still infeasible, and its last pass computed a correction and returned without applying it. New state `ST.sc[SC_FACERES]`: the kilograms `eFaceMove()`'s `Math.max(0, ...)` creates, zeroed at the head of `eRoomStep()`, so all three calls in a tick are covered. **Measured 20/09/26**, `plantPreset(0)`, dice off, hot-leg break at `pipe:28,15` + floor hole at `mat:20,30` + 10 t/s at 12,15 over 6 s (`tools/liqprobe.js conserve`): **34.908 kg invented, worst tick 31.586 kg, against 0.000000 kg now.** In `tests/physics/roomgas.js move`, 200 ticks of the same scenario: 4.02e-13 kg, against a double-ulp floor of 1.49e-10 kg; the room's gas total over a 5 MPa BLAST holds to 1.43e-9 relative against an f32 storage floor of 100 x 2^-24; a 5 kg/s hydrogen source over 50 ticks raises the total by 5.0000133 kg against 5; no cell holds negative gas in any of them. The check was seen to fail: with the tail skipped it reads 19.586 kg. | **a residue, and it is the through-flow, not the mass.** On the hardest scenario the pass cap still binds on **10 of 592 tail calls** (1.7 %), so on those the fixed point is not reached and a chain longer than 16 cells passes less than the solve asked for. At `FACE_TAIL` 64 it binds on 3 of 592, so raising it 4x does not remove it and 16 is kept: **a deliberate cut, taken 20/09/26 on the measurement**, because the quantity traded is recovered through-flow and never mass. Cost: the limiter's own worst case (every cell overdrawn, 2040 cells) 0.497 ms/call against 0.491 before, whole tick 1.125 ms against 1.119, both inside the 0.9-1.3 ms budget. The call allocates nothing, measured in isolation at 1.9 B/call against a 1.9 B/call floor, the same as before | YES on both: the scheme, and 16 passes over 64 | high on the conservation, which is a construction proof and not a fit; high on the kilograms, which are direct reads; medium on the 1.7 % bind rate, which is one scenario |
| ...what hydrogen fraction a cell reads | moles of hydrogen over the cell's own moles | since 11/09/26 off the cell's own air and steam (`roomMolX()`), and hydrogen is part of `s.roomM`. A G8-style pulse (pulse at 2 s rather than 10, so the run fits 10 s): 98 kg of hydrogen in the air by 12 s and none burned, where the pinned run lit on entry - not comparable, the plant was in a different state | a pressurised cell dilutes the same hydrogen; a steam-filled cell is inert | YES | medium |
|  what the BLAST fault injects | a charge releases ENERGY. What it is worth as a pressure is that energy over the volume it is released into, and what it leaves behind is a hot compartment that vents through whatever it opened | **21/09/26, the judge counts a front, not a shared rise.** A cell is judged on the part of its sudden change its compartment did not share: `bang = (P - Q) - (P̄ - Q̄)`, the bars the gas-volume-weighted means over the region's gas cells on the same tick (`eBang()`, `eRegExcess()`, `SX.regExc`); blast, scar and `RR_PMAX` all read it. A uniform rise has no front and loads a structure by its pressure difference, dynamic load factor 1 for a rise long against its period (Biggs 1964, ramp loading): measured, the core's compartment raised 2 MPa/s for 0.5 s through `roomBang()`'s own law wrecks **0** parts on the blast path (`blastdmg.js ramp`), where the old `P - Q` judge wrecked 111 (seen to fail). Steam-line break, stock, dice off: **0 blast-path wrecks in the first second**, the worst intact part `reltk` at 5.27 of its 25 kPa; before, `reltk` was wrecked at 0.04 s and `rods` at 0.06 s on a 35 → 110 kPa compartment-wide rise the one-pole baseline trailed by 28-44 kPa, which jammed the rods. `late`, `stale` and `charge` read as before. No `pburst` moved. **20/09/26, the damage-timing half is closed.** A structure fails on the peak side-on overpressure it sees, WHENEVER it sees it. `eBlastStep()`'s `burn` gate is deleted and `SC_ROOMBANG` with it: every part is judged every tick against its own cell's excess over a QUASI-STATIC baseline, `ST.roomPQs`, a one-pole lag whose time constant is DERIVED — `max(GW,GH)·MPC/sqrt(γ·R·T_HULL)` = 0.0843 s, the time sound takes to cross the board, so it is slower than the acoustic traverse and faster than anything a plant does. No fitted number enters it. `eScarStep()` reads the same baseline; `eRoomRegMean()` and `SX.rPStat` are deleted — one baseline, one concept. Measured (`tests/physics/blastdmg.js`): over 222 blast-limited parts, **every one is wrecked on exactly the tick its own cell first met its own limit, and 220 of them on a tick AFTER the one the charge was placed on** — under the old gate those 220 were never judged at all. A second charge 5 s after a first wrecks exactly the set that met its limit on that tick, 0 wrecked without and 0 met without being wrecked. A sustained 200 kg/s steam release leaves a dynamic excess of **0.94 % of the static rise it causes** and wrecks nothing on the blast path; frozen, the same baseline leaves 100 % and the check fails. **The 11/09/26 reading of 11 of 24 wreck sets pointing at the charge is now 19 of 24** (2000 kPa, 24 placements, re-measured). **11/09/26: nothing posts; the charge is heat into its cells (`roomBang()`, `dT = kJ/ROOM_CVAIR`) and the pressure is the read.** Measured, stock, 5 MPa beside `pump0`: the region's mean temperature goes to 835.8 K, whose constant-volume worth is 187.6 kPa gauge, and the read is 29.3 kPa at 1 s, 82.1 at 10 s, 105.7 at 20 s - no wall breaks (row: the gas is isothermal). What follows is the 10/09/26 text. Since 10/09/26 the figure typed stays MPa and is read as the constant-volume rise it is worth in the cell it is placed in: `roomBang()` does `dT = kJ/ROOM_CVAIR` and posts `ROOM_P0*dT/T_HULL`, exactly the two lines the hydrogen deflagration and the metal fire already had, which now call the same door. Measured: the deflagration reads 12.7127 kg burned, 15640.0 K peak and 6231.6 kPa peak wave through either spelling, to every digit | **the timing is closed; the near field and the `ROOM_TMAX` dial cap are not, and both were weighed 20/09/26 below.** **before 10/09/26 the tool carried neither mass nor energy**: it posted a pressure onto a field that relaxed back to an attractor it had never moved, and 5 s after a 300 kPa blast the cell was at 293.06 K with nothing left. After, the same 300 kPa leaves the cell at 326.44 K. **5 MPa is now about 10.9 MJ, near 2.6 kg of TNT: 14 460 K in one 0.87 m3 cell, and 5.3 kPa quasi-static once it is shared over a 760 m3 compartment.** Two things follow and are stated rather than hidden. `ROOM_TMAX` 20000 K CAPS the tool: above about 50 MPa on the dial the cell saturates and the post stops growing at 6813 kPa, so the dial does less than it says. And the near field is thrown away - the source cell is left at 14 755 K with its density unchanged, so the impedance step traps most of the impulse in the cell and the front that leaves it is small; measured, the whole 5 MPa gives a region peak of 10 kPa. **Re-measured 10/09/26 when the fourth difference replaced the second, and it MOVED - the wrong way.** Method, stated because it is not the one that gave the 10 kPa: the stock board's geometry with a hand-made empty room at 300 K and a zero gauge, `roomBang()` fed the same `kPa/ROOM_P0*T_HULL*ROOM_CVAIR` the tool feeds it, 150 ticks. The source cell reaches 14 762 K either way. The worst excess over gauge anywhere is **36.1 kPa at 0.06 s against 55.2 kPa at 0.12 s** on the second difference, and the worst cell other than the source one tick in is **16.9 kPa against 36.3**. Less dissipation makes the far field BIGGER on a cold post - 166.0 kPa against 53.4 one tick after a bare 5 MPa `roomBlastPost()` - and the near field SMALLER on a hot one, because the impulse now rings inside the hot cell instead of being smeared out of it by the damping. The trapped near field is therefore a stiffer gap than it was, not a softer one. **CLASS: MODEL on both.** The shield lift keeps `roomBlastPost()` and gains no heat, because a shield being thrown off is mechanical work. **11/09/26: the charge became a Gaussian (`roomBlastCharge()`, `BLAST_SIG` 2.4 cells) and lost its heat again** - a bare post. Every cell of the Gaussian now goes through `roomBang()` at its own share, so the heat is the pressure's constant-volume worth cell by cell. Measured on STOCK, 1 MPa at 21,28: 3139 K in the centre cell on the first tick, region mean gauge +68.2 kPa at 5 s and +27.6 kPa at 35 s. The dial starts at 1 MPa | **BOTH ARE WEIGHED NOW, 20/09/26 (`tools/blastweigh.js`), and one of them turned out not to exist.** (a) **The `ROOM_TMAX` cap binds at 6.8134 MPa on the dial, not "roughly 50 MPa"** — that older figure was wrong. `eRoomBlastCharge()`/`eRoomBang()` write `ST.roomT` synchronously inside `act("blast", ...)`, so the source cell's constant-volume worth can be read with no tick and no gas solve at all: it climbs exactly linearly with the dial (3406.7 kPa at 3.4 MPa) up to **6813.4 kPa at 6.8134 MPa and is then dead flat all the way to 340 MPa**, a fifty-fold dial move buying nothing. Bisected to 1e-4 MPa. (b) **The near field is NOT trapped in the source cell.** On the row's own case — 5 MPa beside `pump0`, nine Chebyshev rings out to the Gaussian's reach — the DEPOSIT is concentrated (14 755 K at ring 0 falling to 349 K at ring 8), but the PRESSURE is not: peak `ST.roomP` over the first 0.1 s reads 43.1–44.5 kPa on rings 0 through 7, with rings 2–4 slightly ABOVE the source cell, and only ring 8 lower at 37.3. The source cell's share of the summed ring peaks is **0.111, which is 1/9 — statistically uniform**, unchanged between 0.1 s and 1 s. Since the 11/09/26 Gaussian rewrite the pressure is already near-uniform across the near field inside one tick. The row's trapping claim described the superseded single-cell model. **21/09/26, weighed against Baker, in an empty sealed liner box so nothing but the charge acts** (`tests/physics/blastdmg.js charge`): the room gains the charge's Brode energy to 1e-9 (393.8 MJ at 5 MPa, the Gaussian cut at 3σ as the tool cuts it); 0 cell-ticks at `ROOM_TMAX`, the hottest cell 10 469 K. The near-field peak one cell outside the 1/e² radius is **407 kPa against Baker et al. 1983's starting shock of 2171 kPa** for p1/p0 50.3 and a 10 469 K source (0.19), and the peak falls from 616 kPa at the centre to 149 kPa 20 cells out. Energy all there, pressure not: the implicit solve spreads a charge over about 7 m inside one 0.02 s tick, which is decision 8's unresolved front (`the implicit step's front`), so the near field is a consequence of that cut and not a separate defect. The 20/09/26 ring table above is stale: fired beside `pump0` the charge now breaks the primary and the rings read the blowdown (6.6-10 MPa in 0.1 s; 4.0-7.8 MPa and one 2.7 GPa cell on the committed tree), and the dial cap read 9.929 MPa there on 21/09/26 | high on the arithmetic, none on any real charge; **high on both new numbers**, which were read off the code path in use; the near field against Baker is YES, decision 8's, confirmed by the user 21/09/26  |
| the flux signal | a detector counts discrete neutron arrivals, so its scatter is 1/sqrt(N) and N goes with power: visibly noisy at low power, quiet at high. It is why a real startup is slow and why plants carry redundant channels | one smooth analytic number at every power. No reading anywhere carries measurement scatter | no measurement noise at all, so a low-power hold is exactly as easy as a full-power one and redundant instrument channels have nothing to vote against | DRIFT — nobody chose it. The INSTRUMENT CHANNELS purchase priced this mechanic and was deleted 08/09/26 because it never existed: `P.noise` had one reader, a label restating the purchase | high |
| an instrument channel | a transmitter fails, and it fails in ways that read: stuck at its last value, or slammed to full scale. It is the whole reason a plant carries three of them and votes 2-out-of-3 | every `source` block reads the plant directly and is always right. Nothing can fail one | no instrument fault exists, so three sources through a `sel`/median - which that block offers as how a plant votes a liar out - protects against nothing, and the instrument-cabinet hit can only stop the cabinet computing, never make it lie | DRIFT — nobody chose it. The INSTRUMENT CHANNELS purchase priced this and was deleted 08/09/26 rather than left selling it | high |
| what a blast breaks (`ROLE[].pburst`, `PIPE_PBURST`) | side-on overpressure damage is published (Clancey 1972, in Lees, Loss Prevention): 1 kPa typical window glass fails; 7 partial demolition of houses; 7 to 14 corrugated steel and aluminium panel fastenings fail, then the panels buckle; 9 steel frame of a clad building slightly distorted; 14 to 21 unreinforced block walls shattered; 21 heavy machines of about 1.4 t in an industrial building take little damage, steel frame building distorted and pulled off its foundations; 21 to 28 frameless steel panel building demolished and OIL STORAGE TANKS RUPTURE; 28 cladding of light industrial buildings ruptured; 35 wooden utility poles snapped, an 18 t hydraulic press slightly damaged; 48 loaded rail wagons overturned; 62 loaded boxcars demolished; 69 probable total destruction of buildings, heavy machine tools of about 3 t moved and badly damaged, very heavy ones of about 5.4 t survive. There is NO published overpressure rung for an electrical cabinet, a pipe wall or a pressure vessel: a vessel rated for MPa inside is not crushed by 200 kPa outside, and pipe fails at its supports and by displacement. Those are killed by missiles, by what they are bolted to moving, and by the fire, not by the pressure itself. **Searched 20/09/26** for a rung covering any of the unsourced classes: Clancey 1972 (in Lees), DoE-STD-3014, DoD 6055.09, the ASCE petrochemical blast guide, Baker et al. *Explosion Hazards and Evaluation*, Glasstone & Dolan, UK HSE. None carries an overpressure threshold for an electrical cabinet, a finned coil, a control-rod drive, a shell-and-tube exchanger, a pipe wall or a pressure vessel. The absence is now a searched result, not an assumption | one kPa per role, no source stated when written: radiator 15; ctrl, bkp, vent, inert 20; rods, cond 35; pump, turb 70; tank 100 (per instance override on `D.tanks[].pburst`); fitting, pipe 120 (the fitting row IS `PIPE_PBURST`); core, sg, ihx 200. `minPburst()` is the weakest fitted one and gates the bang event; `hurtStep()` ramps a sustained squeeze against the same figure. **Re-pinned 11/09/26: tank 100 to 25**, the storage-tank rupture rung. Pump and turbine at 70 sit on the 69 kPa heavy-machine-tool rung and were left where they were. **Re-pinned 12/09/26: fitting 70 to 120**, written as `PIPE_PBURST` itself rather than a copy of it: a tee, a throttle and a relief valve are bodies in the pipe run, and a valve that let go before the pipe either side of it put the weakest thing on the board inside the run rather than at its supports. It is a consistency argument, not a measurement - the 69 kPa rung it leaves was the sourced figure and 120 is not sourced at all. Everything else was left as it was and is listed here as unsourced | pump, turb: on a published rung. Tank: now on one. Fitting: no rung since 12/09/26 - it inherits pipe's, which is the wrong quantity, and the trade was a sourced figure for a self-consistent run, and the 69 kPa it left was sourced to the wrong OBJECT (a 3 t machine tool, not a pipe fitting), so the trade gave up a real citation without gaining one. Radiator 15 is under the 7 to 14 cladding rung but a radiator panel is not building cladding; no rung. Cabinets 20, rods 35, condenser 35: no rung. Pipe 120 and the three vessels at 200: no rung exists and the failure mode is not pressure, so any figure here is the wrong quantity. The label ladder `BLASTFX.zones` in `render/room.js` (20, 70, 120, 200) is a copy of this table and inherits every gap | **YES - DECIDED 21/09/26 by the user, on the 20/09/26 search.** The sources named above carry no overpressure rung for an electrical cabinet, a finned coil, a control-rod drive, a shell-and-tube exchanger, a pipe wall or a pressure vessel, and for most of those the real failure is missiles, mounting failure and fire rather than side-on overpressure. The figures are therefore a ladder chosen to read well, and they stay as they are: an unsourced number swapped for another unsourced number buys nothing. A decision, not an oversight; `BLASTFX.zones` carries the same decision | high on the Clancey rungs; high that the other five classes have no published rung at all, after the 20/09/26 search; none on the figures themselves, which have no rung to be sure of |

## Water and steam properties

Checked by `tests/physics/props.js` against the IAPWS-IF97 verification tables (tables 5, 15, 33, the B23 line) and its saturation table, through an independent test-side IF97 (`tests/physics/lib.js`: regions 1, 2, 3, B23, `if97pT()`) that is itself checked against those tables to 1e-8 (19/09/26). Since 19/09/26 all water is IF97: region 4 for the saturation line, regions 1, 2 and 3 for everything else, including the dome's own h_f, h_g, rho_f and rho_g at psat (Wagner & Pruss and the Clapeyron latent heat are gone). Single-phase water is one table over (p, h) and (T, p), built once at load (`WT_*` in `pipenet.js`); a node reads T and rho at its own pressure. No water `COOLANT` row states a figure: `coolFig()` derives rho, Ts, h_fg and the rise's c_p from IF97 at the row's own P0 and Tref.

The saturated line `hOfT()` is kept for a T that is a saturation temperature | 115.33, 184.14 and 975.54 against the same (19/09/26); c_p 4.173, 4.010, 4.655 against 4.173, 4.010, 4.656 | — | high |

| a boiling core's rated flow | set by the exit quality it is designed to: x w of steam leaves, the same mass of feed mixes back into the separated water, so w = Q/(x (h_g - h_feed)). A BWR/4 passes ~12 900 kg/s at 3293 MWt (~3.92 kg/s per MWt, x 0.146, feed 489 K), an RBMK-1000 ~10 400 kg/s at 3200 MWt (~3.25, x 0.145, feed 438 K) | **closed 19/09/26; the RBMK's feed temperature stated 20/09/26.** A boiling row states `xOut` (BWR 0.146, LWGR 0.145) and no rise; `coolFig()` derives h_in = h_f - x(h_f - h_feed) at the row's P0 and `feedTOf()`, dT0, c_p = (h_f - h_in)/dT0 and the rated enthalpy rise h_out - h_in. `FEED_T0` 490 K is the fallback and it is right for BWR/4, whose published feed is 489 K; the `RBMK-1000` `PLANTPRE` row now states `feedT:438`, the published 165 C at the drum (INSAG-7 annex I), through the same per-design knob CALDER HALL already used. `coreRatedKgs()` is the one door: `P.wRated`, each core's `wRated`, the pump duty (`legDutyKgs()`, `loopHeadOf()`), `T.G0`, the loop-inventory fallback and the core channel's own rise per unit heat (`PT.coreRiseH`). `boilflow.js`: BWR/4 **3.718** kg/s per MWt, RBMK-1000 **3.326** (was 3.741 at 490 K), both within 1e-6 of the law on IF97, and the RBMK is now checked against the published 10 400 / 3200 = 3.25 as well as against the law. At rest, dice off: RBMK-1000 exit quality **0.140** at drum pressure (`rbmk.js boil`), inlet subcooling **14.9 K** below the drum's own saturation (PASS against the published ~14 K). BWR/4 **0.497** at 0.545 of rated flow with a saturated inlet (GAP, row "BWR/4 cycle"). Seen to fail on HEAD with `xOut` on the rows: subcooling 30 vs 9.28 K, flow 12.74 and 5.49 kg/s per MWt, RBMK exit quality 0.082 | BWR/4 -5 % per MWt against the published figure; RBMK-1000 **+2.3 %**, where it was +15 % and all of that was the feed temperature | YES on the law. The **DRIFT** on the RBMK's feed temperature is closed; `FEED_T0` stays the one fallback for a plant that states none. BWR/4 at rest: **BUILD** (row "BWR/4 cycle") | medium on the published flows |
| heat capacity rate of a water stream across an exchanger | the stream's h(T) between its inlet and what it can reach | the secant of h(T) between the stream's inlet and the other side's temperature (`eStageSecant()`), so a heat priced in T lands in h | the next row | — | medium: the profile along the tube is still one exponential |
| Steam generator effectiveness | a stream against a boiling shell (Cr = 0) whose cp varies: the integral of dh/(T(h) − Ts) over the stream is UA/w | `hx.js`, re-measured 19/09/26 after the water table, STOCK at 10 s: the stage gives **1197 MW**; the exact law gives **1229 MW** on the model's own h(T, p) and 1228.6 MW on IF97 region 1 at the stream's pressure (UA 46 402 kW/K, w 6951 kg/s). Re-measured 20/09/26: **1237 MW** against **1267 MW** on both (UA 47 613 kW/K, w 8033 kg/s); the stage's heat follows STOCK's rating (1197 → 1236.6 MWt), what moved w by 16 % is not separated. NUSCALE −2.08 % and EPR −2.19 % are from before the table and were not re-measured. The outlet water carries the stage's heat to 0.09 %, so the gap is the stage's own law and not the outlet run's transport or the tube metal: `eSgQ()` prices the stream at the secant cp over Tin..Ts and applies 1 − exp(−UA/(w·cp)), which undercounts where cp climbs toward 600 K. The old check's constant-cp form was 2.5 % off the exact law, so the old −3.2 % was mostly the check. The model's own h(T, p) now agrees with IF97 region 1 at the stream's pressure to 0.03 %, so the pressure half of this row is closed; the stage law remains | **−2.4 %** of the transferred heat (20/09/26; −2.6 % on 19/09/26) | **NO, and 20/09/26 the cost was finally weighed (`tools/sgcost.js`) — it is prohibitive.** Swapping only the pricing line, everything else held: the secant costs **1.8 µs per stage per tick**, the exact quadrature against the model's own `tOfHA` costs **11.3–11.8 ms**, about **6000x**. A warm STOCK tick measures 0.9–1.3 ms the way `tools/ticktime.js` measures it, so **one stage's exact law costs 900–1200 % of a whole tick** — a single steam generator would blow the frame budget several times over before anything else ran. Against a from-scratch IF97 bisection at `hx.js`'s own `dh` 0.01 it is worse again: **5.5 s for one stage, one evaluation**. The fix is therefore not "integrate it exactly"; it would need a cache, a fit, or an adaptive step, and none of those is what was benched. **NUSCALE and EPR re-measured on the water table**, same method as STOCK, stage 0 at 10 s: NUSCALE 714.8 MW against an exact 733.5, **−2.55 %** (was −2.08); EPR 249.5 against 256.6, **−2.77 %** (was −2.19). Both moved FURTHER from the exact law, the same way STOCK did. **The 16 % move in `w` is NOT separated, only narrowed.** Stage 0 now reads 8029.3 kg/s, confirming the tree is the 20/09/26 snapshot. The two obvious commits are ruled out with live numbers: for STOCK's non-boiling row `P.sat.cp` and `coolFig(a).cp` are the same cached object (5.7822) and `coreDT0()` equals `a.dT0` (30.000), so `35b7417` and `48c553d` are a mathematical no-op here and bite only boiling rows. What is left is the Fink UO₂ pin and film rework of 19/09/26, which lands in the window and does touch a UO₂ core — **not ablated, so not confirmed** | high (the law is exact); **high on the cost measurement**; none on the cause of the 16 % |
| Drum at rest | a drum at rest passes what arrives: riser in equals steam and downcomer out, in mass and in enthalpy, and the core's heat is the steam out less the feed in | **closed on both plants, 19/09/26.** The drum probe (`drum.js probe`): mass 6.5e-5 of its steam, first law 1.9e-4, circuit 1.0002; 60 s march (`drum.js march`): mass 4e-11, energy 4e-12, pressure, level and steam all within 1.4e-4 of commissioned. RBMK-1000 (`drum.js 5`): drum0 mass 3.6e-5 and first law 3.6e-5, drum1 1e-13 and 2e-9, circuit 1.00001, each loop's channels heat = w(h_out − h_in) to 4e-6 of their share. What closed RBMK-1000, beyond the probe's fixes: one core water node per loop (row "a tube core's water, per loop"), and the governor fit compared the nozzle flow through a bleed read taken before `eInHSeed()` moved it (15.7 kg/s short on drum0); the condenser and feed settle now re-read the inflow and the turbine every pass. Injected: the split core folded back fails drum0 mass (−0.46) and drum1 first law (−0.18) | none | — | high |
| a tube core's water, per loop | a pressure-tube core has no plenum: each loop's group headers feed its own half of the channels (INSAG-7 annex II: "two parallel loops each of which cools half of the fuel channels") | **since 19/09/26** a tube core piped into several loops is one network node per loop (`coreLoops()`, `coreLoopNode()`, `runEndNode()` in `pipenet.js`): loop k > 0 is the core's id and one mark, so every reader that slices a face off a name still finds the core; a run's loop is `loopMap()` of its far end, an unclaimed run (HPI) lands on the first. Each node takes 1/n of the heat, the vessel, floor and cavity openings are one edge per node (cavity channels split by n), `coreInH` is inflow-weighted over the nodes and `eCoreWaterA()` (core.js) is the one door for mass, volume and pressure. The design graph (`nodeGraph()`) keeps one core node | the neutronic mesh is r-z and cannot tell the halves apart: every mesh node reads the flow-weighted mean of the halves' water, so a real core's halves can differ and this one's cannot, neutronically. Loop flows 1802 / 1865 kg/s (the drawn loop 0 goes through the surge tee) | **YES** on the mesh; a vessel core is untouched | high |

## Steady state against its family

A preset is inspired by its namesake (row "Plant size"). What counts here is BEHAVIOUR its family
shows (a direct cycle boils in the core, a sodium plant keeps its water off the primary); a
pressure, flow or MWe off the namesake's own sheet is a deliberate cut and is recorded, not worked.

`tests/physics/presets.js`, 120 s from commissioning, 18/09/26. Mass and energy close on all nine (mass under 6e-10 of inventory, energy under 7e-11 of core heat; re-run after the reference solve moved onto the engine).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Core exit quality at rest **0.497** against 0.146, at 0.545 of the rated flow with a saturated inlet (`boilflow.js bwr`, GAP, 20/09/26; 0.529 at 0.53 on 19/09/26): the generators hand the core back two-phase water, so the pumps push half the flow the core is rated for. **20/09/26, with the axial pressure profile built:** the march used to read `h_fg` at the coolant row's DESIGN pressure and now reads it at the plane's own solved pressure, which on this preset is 1.7 % apart (1505.1 against 1479.4 kJ/kg) because the core sits at 7.39 MPa and the row is drawn at 7.17. The same drawing puts quality 0.24 water on both recirculation pumps' suctions at rest (`settle.js`, 18/09/26), where a real BWR's recirculation suction is feedwater-subcooled downcomer water; and its shell settle has no root: the Broyden never converges (235 iterations on HEAD), and since 18/09/26 it stops after 8 without improvement and lands its best iterate. So the plant round the core moves from tick 1 (19/09/26): with the core at its own fixed point and rods and boron held, it sees 3.67 pcm and 0.62 % heat over 3 s (`core.js`, 20/09/26; 2.0 and 0.38 % on 19/09/26), and its first tick moves 1.15e-6 of a node's mass (`settle.js`, 20/09/26, GAP against 1e-6; 8.8e-7 on 19/09/26) | void 2x, boron that a BWR does not carry, two-phase pump suctions | **BUILD**: the RBMK row shows the direct cycle is drawable (`drum:true`); this row does not draw it | high |

| RBMK-1000 reference solve | — | **closed 19/09/26.** All four reference solves settle (26, 23, 2 and 31 passes; one loop 44, 24, 2, 28), and `settle.js 5` and `core.js 5` are gated: settled continuity 1.9e-11, first tick 9.4e-9, 3 s at rest 0.09 pcm and 1.5e-4 heat. The cause was the reference STATE: every node of the core's circuit was seeded at the loop's design enthalpy, and on a direct cycle that circuit includes the condensate and feed train, so saturated loop water flashed in the condenser (density 0.2 kg/m3) and the pump edges drove `cpumpb` to −7.8 MPa; a NaN node of an authored circuit read T-avg, which is vapour at condenser pressure; and the drums were seeded as saturated liquid, so drum1's pressure crossed its own saturation line pass by pass (the 2-cycle on its steam line). `nodeRefH` now skips nodes off the recirculating loop, `eNodeHStruct()` reads T-avg only on the loop, and a drum's reference is its two-phase seed at its level (`holdSeedH()`) | none | **MODEL**, in the shared build code; only drum plants (RBMK-1000, the drum probe) have their feed train on the core's circuit, and the eight other presets are unmoved (60 s, dice off) | high on the pass counts |

| RBMK-1000 at rated power | stable under its automatic power regulators; drum 6.9 MPa; about 31 % efficient (1000 MWe of 3200 MWt) | **flies, 19/09/26; re-flown 20/09/26 on the redrawn core** (row "Plant size": 775 MWt, was 1037). `presets.js 5`, 120 s: drum **6.90 MPa**, void **0.273**, efficiency **0.288** under Carnot 0.4096, heat balance 0.9977, mass 2.0e-15, energy 1.2e-11: every check PASS. `rbmk.js rest`, 60 s, dice off: the regulator holds neutron power to 3.0e-4 of rated, the drum to 7.3e-4 of commissioned, and the core's heat against steam out less feed in reads 0.9990. `rbmk.js step`: a -10 % demand step is inside 1 % of its new setpoint by 34.2 s and swings 0.003 of rated over the last 20 s, the drum staying between 6.745 and 6.900 MPa, well inside its trip and its lift. What made it rest: the feed mixing before the pumps, one core water node per loop, the reference state (rows above), the governor's bleed read, and a power regulator | none at rest. Efficiency 0.288 against the real machine's ~0.31, on a condenser that fell with the rating (Tc 329.4 K); 0.288 x 775 MWt is ~223 MWe, and the real machine is 1000 MWe of 3200. The dynamics are the next two rows | - | high on the real machine and the readings |
| RBMK-1000 power regulator | the RCPS automatically maintains the preset power level (INSAG-7 annex II) on in-core and ex-core chambers; the turbine holds the drum | `buildPowerAuto()` (`ctl.js`, section POWER REG) on every direct cycle in place of the T-avg controller: `nfr` against a CONST setpoint (the operator's demand, 1), times `TPROG_SPAN` so the plant's own rod tune applies, clamped ±6 K, PID with blank gains (the plant's tune) and no RATE, i.e. rod speed proportional to the error; dead band 0. `rbmk.js`: at rest power held to 4.5e-5 of rated over 60 s, drum to 2e-5, core heat = steam − feed to 1e-5; injected, rod sink off, 0.98 of rated off (fails). The rods run at the RBMK's own 0.4 m/s: `ARCHPRE` RBMK states `rodSpd` 0.4/7, `archPreset()` sets it or deletes it like `tube`, `P.rodRate` and `PT.coreRodRate` follow, `autorodTune()` does not read it; `rbmk.js scram`: normal stroke **17.5 s** and scram rate = drive rate, one servo (PASS; 83.3 s and 0.012 against 0.057 on the tree before, fails). The turbine governor (`loadDem`'s PID, `PRESS_KP` 1000, `PRESS_TI` **67 s**, `step.js`) holds the drum in an outer loop around the rods. No published RBMK governor tune was found (19/09/26: OSTI abstracts of *Power Technology and Engineering* 49(1) "Improvement of Algorithms for Pressure Maintenance Systems in Drum-Separators of RBMK-1000 Reactors" and ETDEWEB 6558032 "Tuning parameter choice for the level and pressure regulators in the RBMK-1000 steam-drum" name the subject and give no figure; OSTI full text refused the connection; the Kyoto Malko PDF did not read), so Ti is derived: 3× the rod regulator's Ti 22.2 s (`autorodTune()`), the low end of the 3–5× separation for nested loops. `rbmk.js stepoff` checks the other side: governor off after the step, the drum drifts as p∞ + a·e^(−t/τ) with τ **97 s** (fit on 30/130/230 s) toward 6.19 MPa, slower than Ti (PASS). A −10 % demand step (19/09/26, `rbmk.js step`): power **0.44 % off** the setpoint over the last 20 s (PASS, limit 0.9 %), **0.20 % peak to peak** (PASS, limit 1 %), 0.9024–0.9044; worst undershoot 0.042 of rated; last outside 1 % at **29.8 s** (19.8 s after the step). Drum over the 90 s **6.713–6.913 MPa**, flown on to 180 s once 6.713–6.949 (peak 0.7 % over) and settling back toward 6.90, against the drawing's own limits read at run time: safety valve lift 7.314 MPa (0.365 MPa clear) and low-pressure trip 5.934 MPa (0.779 MPa clear); no published drum pressure band was found. Injected (`rbmk.js stepdeep`): governor off and a −20 % step leave the trip at 118.1 s (fails). Governor off at −10 % never leaves: with a fixed turbine valve steam flow goes with pressure, so the drum falls about as far as the power, 6.90 → 6.19 = −10.3 %, inside the 14 % trip | with `AUTOROD_DB` (0.8 K, 4.4 % of power through the slope) the power wandered to the band's edges and escaped at 110 s, so the band is 0: the chambers carry no noise here. Before 19/09/26 the governor's Ti was 5 s, inside the rods' band, and the step swung round 0.90 at a ~40 s period (1.15 % off, 1.48 % peak to peak); the rods were not why (0.012 and 0.057 of travel/s read the same). The drum recovers slowly: 90 s after the step it is still 0.2 % over 6.90 MPa | **BUILD**, RBMK-1000: the governor's tune, closed by the derived Ti; the rod speed is BUILD too and closed. Saved cabinets keep their own `ti` | high on the real split; the 67 s is a derivation, not a published figure; the step has no published response to pin |
